## Empty State

*Category: Feedback.* Exports: `EmptyState`.

### Empty State · 1. Component Overview

A neutral placeholder for a list, table, or section with nothing to show yet — an icon, a title, an optional description, and an optional action.

- React: `import { EmptyState } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-empty-state>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Empty State · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `EmptyState`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `icon` | `string` | `"folder"` | Icon name — see src/core/icons.ts for the available set (default: "folder"). |
| `title` **required** | `ReactNode` | — | Heading text shown under the icon. |
| `children` | `ReactNode` | — | The description/body. |
| `action` | `ReactNode` | — | e.g. a <Button>, rendered below the description. |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class name(s) applied to the root element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; icon?: string; title?: string; description?: string; action?: string; }` | — | Per-part class overrides — merged after (and win over) the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay` — see §1.7.

### Empty State · 3. Events

_No component-specific events._ Native DOM events (`click`, `input`, `focus`…) bubble from the rendered element as usual.

### Empty State · 4. Variants

_This component has no variant prop — it has a single look (restyle it with colors / `classNames`)._

### Empty State · 5. Colors and Theming

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set. It has no color prop of its own.

### Empty State · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### Empty State · 7. Component States

_No dedicated state props — the visual states are default, hover, focus-visible and active._

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### Empty State · 8. Slots / Children / Content

| Component | React prop | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `EmptyState` | `title` | `ReactNode` | Heading text shown under the icon. |
| `EmptyState` | `children` | `ReactNode` | The description/body. |
| `EmptyState` | `action` | `ReactNode` | e.g. a <Button>, rendered below the description. |

Web Component extras (slots and plain HTML attributes accepted by the `<l-*>` tag in addition to the props above):

| Component | Name | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `EmptyState` | `heading` | `string` | Text shown as the title. Named `heading` here because `title` is a native HTMLElement attribute. |

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Empty State · 9. Framework Interfaces

**`EmptyState`** → `<l-empty-state>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `icon` | `icon` | string |
| `heading` | `heading` | string |
| `transition` | `transition` | string |
| `transitionDuration` | `transition-duration` | number |
| `transitionDelay` | `transition-delay` | number |

Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Basic*):

**React**
```tsx
<EmptyState title="No items yet" />
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-EmptyState title="No items yet"></l-EmptyState>

<script type="module">import "lojee-ui/elements";</script>
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Empty State · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Basic

Just an icon and a title.

```tsx
<EmptyState title="No items yet" />
```

#### With description

Add a description below the title for more context.

```tsx
<EmptyState title="No projects">\n  Create your first project to get started.\n</EmptyState>
```

#### Custom icon

Swap the icon for the context — e.g. a search icon for empty search results.

```tsx
<EmptyState title="No results found" icon="search">\n  Try adjusting your filters.\n</EmptyState>
```

#### With action

Give people a way out of the empty state with an action below the description.

```tsx
<EmptyState
  title="No items yet"
  icon="folder"
  action={<Button label="Add item" onClick={handleAdd} />}
>
  Get started by creating your first item.
</EmptyState>
```

#### Transitions

Enter transitions via `transition` (with `transitionDuration` / `transitionDelay`). They play on mount — reload the page, or use Replay in the playground.

```tsx
<EmptyState title="Fade" transition="fade" />
<EmptyState title="Slide up" transition="slide-up" />
<EmptyState title="Slide right" transition="slide-right" transitionDelay={100} />
<EmptyState title="Zoom" transition="zoom" />
<EmptyState title="Flip" transition="flip" />
<EmptyState title="Blur" transition="blur" />
<EmptyState title="Bounce" transition="bounce" />
<EmptyState title="Drop" transition="drop" transitionDuration={700} />
```

### Empty State · 11. Component Composition

- Combined in its own examples with: `Button`.

### Empty State · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: none beyond native element semantics.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: none.
- Keyboard: native keyboard behavior of the underlying element (Tab to focus, Enter / Space to activate).
- Focus: uses the browser focus outline / the underlying control's ring.
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Empty State · 13. Responsive Behavior

- No breakpoint-specific rules — it is fluid and adapts to its container width.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Empty State · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `EmptyState`: `root`, `icon`, `title`, `description`, `action`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with the theme accent; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`.

## Skeletons

*Category: Feedback.* Exports: `Skeleton`.

### Skeletons · 1. Component Overview

A grey placeholder in the shape of content that is still loading — text lines, a block, or an avatar — so a page doesn&apos;t jump when the real content arrives.

- React: `import { Skeleton } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-skeleton>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Skeletons · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `Skeleton`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `SkeletonVariant` | `"text"` | Shape: "text" (a line of text, default), "rect" (a block) or "circle" (an avatar). |
| `width` | `string \| number` | — | Width: any CSS length or a number of px (default: fills its container; a circle uses `size`). |
| `height` | `string \| number` | — | Height: any CSS length or a number of px (default: one text line for "text", 80px for "rect"). |
| `size` | `number` | `40` | Diameter of a "circle" in px (default: 40). |
| `lines` | `number` | `1` | For "text": how many lines to draw. The last one is shorter, like real text (default: 1). |
| `animation` | `SkeletonAnimation` | `"pulse"` | How it shows that something is loading: "pulse" (default), "shimmer" (a light sweep) or "none". Respects `prefers-reduced-motion`. |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class name(s) appended to the root element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; line?: string }` | — | Per-part class overrides — merged after (and win over) the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay` — see §1.7.

### Skeletons · 3. Events

_No component-specific events._ Native DOM events (`click`, `input`, `focus`…) bubble from the rendered element as usual.

### Skeletons · 4. Variants

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Skeleton` | `variant` | `text`, `rect`, `circle` | `"text"` | Shape: "text" (a line of text, default), "rect" (a block) or "circle" (an avatar). |

### Skeletons · 5. Colors and Theming

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set. It has no color prop of its own.

### Skeletons · 6. Sizes

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Skeleton` | `width` | `string \| number` | — | Width: any CSS length or a number of px (default: fills its container; a circle uses `size`). |
| `Skeleton` | `height` | `string \| number` | — | Height: any CSS length or a number of px (default: one text line for "text", 80px for "rect"). |
| `Skeleton` | `size` | `number` | `40` | Diameter of a "circle" in px (default: 40). |

### Skeletons · 7. Component States

_No dedicated state props — the visual states are default, hover, focus-visible and active._

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### Skeletons · 8. Slots / Children / Content

_No children or slots — it is configured entirely through props._

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

| --- | --- | --- |
| `variant` | `variant` | string |
| `width` | `width` | string |

| `size` | `size` | number |
| `lines` | `lines` | number |
| `animation` | `animation` | string |
| `transition` | `transition` | string |
| `transitionDuration` | `transition-duration` | number |
| `transitionDelay` | `transition-delay` | number |

Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Shapes*):

**React**
```tsx
<Skeleton variant="circle" size={48} />
<Skeleton lines={3} />
<Skeleton variant="rect" height={96} />
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Skeletons · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Shapes

```tsx
<Skeleton variant="circle" size={48} />
<Skeleton lines={3} />
<Skeleton variant="rect" height={96} />
```

#### Animation

```tsx
<Skeleton variant="rect" animation="shimmer" />
```

#### A loading card

Put several together in the layout of the real thing — a card, a list row, a table — and swap them for the content once it has loaded.

```tsx
{loading ? (
  <div>
    <Skeleton variant="rect" height={140} animation="shimmer" />
    <Skeleton variant="circle" size={36} animation="shimmer" />
    <Skeleton lines={2} animation="shimmer" />
  </div>
) : (
  <Card>…</Card>
)}
```

### Skeletons · 11. Component Composition

- Combined in its own examples with: `Card`.

### Skeletons · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: none beyond native element semantics.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: `aria-hidden`.
- Keyboard: native keyboard behavior of the underlying element (Tab to focus, Enter / Space to activate).
- Focus: uses the browser focus outline / the underlying control's ring.
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Skeletons · 13. Responsive Behavior

- No breakpoint-specific rules — it is fluid and adapts to its container width.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Skeletons · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `Skeleton`: `root`, `line`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with the theme accent; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`.

## ── Navigation ──

## Navbar

*Category: Navigation.* Exports: `Navbar`, `NavbarItem`.

### Navbar · 1. Component Overview

A top-of-app horizontal navigation bar with a brand, nav links, and right-aligned actions.

- React: `import { Navbar } from "lojee-ui"` · `import { NavbarItem } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-navbar>` · `<l-navbar-item>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Navbar · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `Navbar`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `brand` | `ReactNode` | — | Logo/brand area, left-most. |
| `children` | `ReactNode` | — | Nav links/content, typically rendered next to the brand — composed directly (e.g. `<NavbarItem>` children) alongside, or in addition to, the data-driven `items` shortcut below. |
| `actions` | `ReactNode` | — | Right-side content — search, buttons, avatar, etc. |
| `items` | `NavbarItemSpec[]` | — | Data-driven shortcut for a simple, evenly-spaced link row — the same self-managed active-state system as Sidebar's own `items` (see `SidebarProps.items`): clicking a link updates the selection immediately, a link's `href` is matched against the current URL on load/back-forward-navigation, and every rendered link shares the row's width equally regardless of label length. Renders alongside (before) any directly-composed `children` — not an exclusive alternative. |
| `defaultActiveItem` | `string` | — | Only an initial default for the `items` shortcut (see `NavbarItemSpec.active` for the full seed-vs-lock distinction) — which link starts selected when nothing else (a matching `href`, an `active: true` entry) already determines it. |
| `onActiveItemChange` | `(item: NavbarItemSpec) => void` | — | Called with the full `items` row object whenever the active link changes — a click, a URL match on mount/back-forward-navigation, or a row's `active` field changing to point elsewhere. Use this to read which link is active without tracking it yourself. Not called for links composed directly via `children` (only the data-driven `items` shortcut has a "current link" concept). |
| `sticky` | `boolean` | `false` | Sticks to the top of its scroll container (default: false). |
| `bordered` | `boolean` | `true` | Bottom border (default: true) — only meaningful for "light"/"dark"/"gradient". Has no effect on "minimal" (never shows a border, by design — the whole point of that one) or on the detached-panel variants "bordered"/"elevated", each of which controls its own border entirely through `variant` itself (see below), not this toggle. |
| `variant` | `NavbarVariant` | `"light"` | Visual theme (default: "light"): - "bordered"/"elevated" both float as a detached card instead of docking full-width to the page — kept as separate names since each still has its own distinct bar look on top of that shared shape: "bordered" has a thick `color`-tinted border, shadow, and rounded corners (see `color`/`borderWidth`); "elevated" has that same shadow and rounded corners but no border — depth from the shadow alone, Material-bar style. Both are self-contained — an inset backdrop is included automatically (padding + a light tint of the theme accent) so the bar always reads correctly (rounded corners) with no wrapper markup needed on your end. - "dark" — a deep shade of the theme accent (accent-950), brand text (and, by inheritance, any plain text/links) switches to white. - "minimal" — no background/border at all, blends into the page. - "gradient" — a left-to-right gradient built from `color` (600 → 700). |
| `color` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | `"accent"` | Accent color (default: "accent" — follows the theme accent) — one of the built-in ColorNames, or any other CSS color value (e.g. "#7c3aed" from a color-wheel picker) for a fully custom accent, unconstrained by the fixed palette. For "gradient" it's the gradient itself (600→700-equivalent; a custom hex gets a programmatically darkened second stop); for "bordered" it tints the bar's own border (has no effect on "elevated", which has no border to tint). Has no effect on "light"/"dark"/"minimal". |
| `gradientTo` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | — | Second color of the "gradient" variant: a `ColorName` or any CSS color such as "#ec4899" (default: a darker shade of `color`). |
| `gradientDirection` | `GradientDirection` | `"to-right"` | Direction of the "gradient" variant: "to-right" \| "to-left" \| "to-bottom" \| "to-top" \| "to-br" \| "to-bl" \| "to-tr" \| "to-tl" (default: "to-right"). |
| `borderWidth` | `number` | — | "bordered"'s own border thickness in px (default: 2). Has no effect on any other variant — every other variant's border (where it has one at all) is a fixed-width neutral divider, not an adjustable, colored one. |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class(es) added to the root element, merged before `classNames.root`. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; brand?: string; links?: string; actions?: string; }` | — | Per-part class overrides — merged after (and win over) the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect` — see §1.7.

#### `NavbarItem`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `icon` | `string` | — | Icon name, e.g. "home" — see src/core/icons.ts for the available set. |
| `children` | `ReactNode` | — | The link's label. |
| `href` | `string` | — | Renders as a link when set; otherwise a `<button type="button">`. |
| `onClick` | `() => void` | — | Called with no arguments when the item is clicked. |
| `active` | `boolean` | — | Highlights this as the current page/section (default: false). |
| `disabled` | `boolean` | — | Prevents interaction and dims the item (default: false). |
| `dark` | `boolean` | — | Use the translucent active/hover treatment made for dark surfaces (default: false) — pass `true` alongside a Navbar `variant="dark"/"gradient"`. |
| `vividActive` | `boolean` | — | Strengthens the active link's background/ring beyond `dark`'s usual subtle overlay (default: false) — pass `true` alongside a Navbar `variant="gradient"` specifically (not "dark"), since those sit on a translucent or already-colorful surface where the normal overlay is much easier to lose than it is against "dark"'s plain, solid fill. Has no effect when `dark` is false. |
| `color` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | — | Accent color for the active state (default: "accent" — follows the theme accent) — one of the built-in ColorNames, or any other CSS color value; pair it with the same `color` you gave the parent Navbar. |
| `activeStyle` | `"fill" \| "text"` | — | How `active` renders (default: "fill"): "fill" colors this item's own background/ring, the usual self-contained look; "text" only switches text color/weight and leaves the background transparent — pass this when something else already supplies the active fill, e.g. Navbar's own `items` shortcut, which slides one shared pill behind whichever link is active instead of coloring each link's own background (a continuous slide reads far smoother than every link cross-fading its own background independently). Composing `<NavbarItem>` directly still defaults to "fill", since there is no such shared pill to rely on outside that shortcut. |
| `slotName` | `string` | — | Advanced/rarely-needed: the internal `<slot>` this label renders into is unnamed by default (so a standalone `<l-navbar-item>My Label</l-navbar-item>` just works, the same as any plain default slot) — pass a unique value only if you're rendering several `NavbarItem`s that end up sharing one *other* element's shadow root (the way Navbar's own `items` shortcut does), where multiple unnamed slots in that single root would otherwise all compete for the same unassigned/whitespace light-DOM text nodes (see this component's own `content` for the full reasoning). Has no effect on anything other than which `<slot>` this label's fallback content lives in. |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class(es) added to the item's root element, merged before `classNames.root`. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; icon?: string; label?: string; }` | — | Per-part class overrides — merged after (and win over) the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect` — see §1.7.

### Navbar · 3. Events

| Component | React callback | Signature | Web Component event | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Navbar` | `onActiveItemChange` | `(item: NavbarItemSpec) => void` | `activeitemchange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with the full `items` row object whenever the active link changes — a click, a URL match on mount/back-forward-navigation, or a row's `active` field changing to point elsewhere. Use this to read which link is active without tracking it yourself. Not called for links composed directly via `children` (only the data-driven `items` shortcut has a "current link" concept). |
| `NavbarItem` | `onClick` | `() => void` | — (React only) | Called with no arguments when the item is clicked. |

### Navbar · 4. Variants

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Navbar` | `variant` | `light`, `dark`, `bordered`, `elevated`, `minimal`, `gradient` | `"light"` | Visual theme (default: "light"): - "bordered"/"elevated" both float as a detached card instead of docking full-width to the page — kept as separate names since each still has its own distinct bar look on top of that shared shape: "bordered" has a thick `color`-tinted border, shadow, and rounded corners (see `color`/`borderWidth`); "elevated" has that same shadow and rounded corners but no border — depth from the shadow alone, Material-bar style. Both are self-contained — an inset backdrop is included automatically (padding + a light tint of the theme accent) so the bar always reads correctly (rounded corners) with no wrapper markup needed on your end. - "dark" — a deep shade of the theme accent (accent-950), brand text (and, by inheritance, any plain text/links) switches to white. - "minimal" — no background/border at all, blends into the page. - "gradient" — a left-to-right gradient built from `color` (600 → 700). |
| `NavbarItem` | `activeStyle` | `fill`, `text` | — | How `active` renders (default: "fill"): "fill" colors this item's own background/ring, the usual self-contained look; "text" only switches text color/weight and leaves the background transparent — pass this when something else already supplies the active fill, e.g. Navbar's own `items` shortcut, which slides one shared pill behind whichever link is active instead of coloring each link's own background (a continuous slide reads far smoother than every link cross-fading its own background independently). Composing `<NavbarItem>` directly still defaults to "fill", since there is no such shared pill to rely on outside that shortcut. |

### Navbar · 5. Colors and Theming

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Navbar` | `color` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | `"accent"` | Accent color (default: "accent" — follows the theme accent) — one of the built-in ColorNames, or any other CSS color value (e.g. "#7c3aed" from a color-wheel picker) for a fully custom accent, unconstrained by the fixed palette. For "gradient" it's the gradient itself (600→700-equivalent; a custom hex gets a programmatically darkened second stop); for "bordered" it tints the bar's own border (has no effect on "elevated", which has no border to tint). Has no effect on "light"/"dark"/"minimal". |
| `Navbar` | `gradientTo` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | — | Second color of the "gradient" variant: a `ColorName` or any CSS color such as "#ec4899" (default: a darker shade of `color`). |
| `NavbarItem` | `color` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | — | Accent color for the active state (default: "accent" — follows the theme accent) — one of the built-in ColorNames, or any other CSS color value; pair it with the same `color` you gave the parent Navbar. |

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set.

### Navbar · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### Navbar · 7. Component States

| Component | Prop | Type | Default | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Navbar` | `sticky` | `boolean` | `false` | Sticks to the top of its scroll container (default: false). |
| `Navbar` | `bordered` | `boolean` | `true` | Bottom border (default: true) — only meaningful for "light"/"dark"/"gradient". Has no effect on "minimal" (never shows a border, by design — the whole point of that one) or on the detached-panel variants "bordered"/"elevated", each of which controls its own border entirely through `variant` itself (see below), not this toggle. |
| `NavbarItem` | `active` | `boolean` | — | Highlights this as the current page/section (default: false). |
| `NavbarItem` | `disabled` | `boolean` | — | Prevents interaction and dims the item (default: false). |
| `NavbarItem` | `dark` | `boolean` | — | Use the translucent active/hover treatment made for dark surfaces (default: false) — pass `true` alongside a Navbar `variant="dark"/"gradient"`. |

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed · disabled (dimmed, not focusable).

### Navbar · 8. Slots / Children / Content

| Component | React prop | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Navbar` | `brand` | `ReactNode` | Logo/brand area, left-most. |
| `Navbar` | `children` | `ReactNode` | Nav links/content, typically rendered next to the brand — composed directly (e.g. `<NavbarItem>` children) alongside, or in addition to, the data-driven `items` shortcut below. |
| `Navbar` | `actions` | `ReactNode` | Right-side content — search, buttons, avatar, etc. |
| `NavbarItem` | `children` | `ReactNode` | The link's label. |

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Basic*):

**React**
```tsx
<Navbar
  brand="Lojee"
  items={[
    { label: "Home", active: true },
    { label: "Products" },
    { label: "Pricing" },
  ]}
  actions={<Avatar initials="JD" size="sm" />}
/>
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-Navbar id="basic-navbar" brand="Lojee">
  <div slot="actions">
    <l-Avatar initials="JD" size="sm"></l-Avatar>
  </div>
</l-Navbar>

<script type="module">
  import "lojee-ui/elements";

  document.getElementById("basic-navbar").items = [
    { label: "Home", active: true },
    { label: "Products" },
    { label: "Pricing" },
  ];
</script>
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Navbar · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Basic

items is the data-driven shortcut for a simple, evenly-spaced link row — the same self-managed active-state system as Sidebar's own items (compose NavbarItem directly instead when you need per-link disabled state or custom content).

```tsx
<Navbar
  brand="Lojee"
  items={[
    { label: "Home", active: true },
    { label: "Products" },
    { label: "Pricing" },
  ]}
  actions={<Avatar initials="JD" size="sm" />}
/>
```

#### Active link

Leave active unset on every link (the common case) and Navbar determines and manages it itself: a link's href is matched against the current URL on load/back-forward-navigation, and clicking any link updates it immediately — no router wiring or state needed. onActiveItemChange reports the full item object whenever the active link changes, e.g. to sync it elsewhere.

```tsx
<Navbar
  brand="Lojee"
  defaultActiveItem="Dashboard"
  onActiveItemChange={(item) => console.log(item)}
  items={[
    { label: "Dashboard", href: "/dashboard" },
    { label: "Projects", href: "/projects" },
    { label: "Team", href: "/team" },
  ]}
/>
```

#### Sticky

Set sticky so the bar pins to the top of its scroll container.

```tsx
<div className="h-64 overflow-y-auto">
  <Navbar sticky brand="Lojee">
    <Button variant="ghost" label="Home" />
    <Button variant="ghost" label="Products" />
  </Navbar>
  <div className="p-6 space-y-4">
    {rows.map((row, i) => <p key={i}>{row}</p>)}
  </div>
</div>
```

#### Variants

```tsx
<Navbar
  variant="dark"
  brand={<span className="text-white">Lojee</span>}
  items={[
    { label: "Home", active: true },
    { label: "Products" },
  ]}
/>

{/* Also available:
    variant="bordered" / "elevated" — detached-panel looks (rounded corners, floats
      inside a page instead of docking full-width). Their backdrop (padding + a neutral background)
      is built in, so no extra markup is needed. "bordered" is a solid bar on the page surface with a
      color-tinted border (see `color`/`borderWidth`); "elevated" is the same bar but
      shadow-only, no border.
    variant="minimal"  — no background/border at all, blends into the page.
    variant="gradient" — a left-to-right gradient built from `color` (600 → 700). */}
```

#### Custom styling

Override the root with className, or target the brand/links/actions slots with classNames.

```tsx
<Navbar
  brand="Lojee"
  classNames={{ root: "bg-indigo-50 dark:bg-indigo-950/40", brand: "text-indigo-900 dark:text-indigo-200" }}
>
  <Button variant="ghost" label="Home" className="text-indigo-700 hover:bg-indigo-100 dark:text-indigo-300 dark:hover:bg-indigo-900/40" />
  <Button variant="ghost" label="Products" className="text-indigo-700 hover:bg-indigo-100 dark:text-indigo-300 dark:hover:bg-indigo-900/40" />
</Navbar>
```

_1 more examples are on the docs page._

### Navbar · 11. Component Composition

- Built from `Navbar`, `NavbarItem` — use them together as shown in the examples above.
- Combined in its own examples with: `Avatar`, `Button`.
- Data-driven: pass `items` instead of child components (works the same as a Web Component).

### Navbar · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: none beyond native element semantics.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: `aria-current`, `aria-disabled`, `aria-hidden`.
- Keyboard: native keyboard behavior of the underlying element (Tab to focus, Enter / Space to activate).
- Focus: uses the browser focus outline / the underlying control's ring.
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Navbar · 13. Responsive Behavior

- Breakpoint modifiers in its source: `min-[1024px]`.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Navbar · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `Navbar`: `root`, `brand`, `links`, `actions` · `NavbarItem`: `root`, `icon`, `label`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with `color`; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect`.

## Sidebar

*Category: Navigation.* Exports: `SidebarHeader`, `SidebarFooter`, `Sidebar`, `SidebarMenuItem`.

### Sidebar · 1. Component Overview

A vertical navigation panel — an app-shell shell for the rest of the library's nav content.

- React: `import { SidebarHeader } from "lojee-ui"` · `import { SidebarFooter } from "lojee-ui"` · `import { Sidebar } from "lojee-ui"` · `import { SidebarMenuItem } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-sidebar>` · `<l-sidebar-menu-item>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Sidebar · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `SidebarHeader`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `children` | `ReactNode` | — | Content rendered in the header area, docked above the nav rows. |

#### `SidebarFooter`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `children` | `ReactNode` | — | Content rendered in the footer area, pinned below the nav rows. |

#### `Sidebar`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `children` | `ReactNode` | — | Nav content, plus optionally a <SidebarHeader> and/or <SidebarFooter> among the children — order doesn't matter, they're pulled out and docked to the top/bottom; everything else renders as the scrollable nav body (compose it with List/ListItem, links, etc). |
| `header` | `string` | — | Simple string header label — a lighter-weight alternative to composing a `<SidebarHeader>` child when you just need a name/logo-label pair with no custom markup. A `<SidebarHeader>` child, if also given, takes precedence over this. Pair with `headerIcon` for a leading icon. |
| `headerIcon` | `string` | — | Icon name shown before `header`, e.g. "zap" — see src/core/icons.ts for the available set. Has no effect without `header`. For anything beyond a single named icon (an image, a custom SVG), compose it directly into a `<SidebarHeader>` child instead. |
| `footer` | `string` | — | Simple string footer label — a lighter-weight alternative to composing a `<SidebarFooter>` child. A `<SidebarFooter>` child, if also given, takes precedence over this. |
| `items` | `(SidebarMenuItemSpec \| SidebarMenuCategorySpec)[]` | — | A simple, data-driven nav list — a lighter-weight alternative to composing `<SidebarMenuItem>`s directly when every row is just a label + icon with no per-item onClick/disabled state (compose `<SidebarMenuItem>` yourself for that instead). Mix in `{ category, items }` entries for labeled, collapsible section groups — each one defaults open if it's the first group or contains an `active` row, and toggles independently after that (not a controlled prop; local UI state, like the built-in collapse toggle's hover state). Rendered above any `children`, automatically matched to this Sidebar's own `collapsed`/`color`/`variant`. Which row is active is also self-determined by default (see `SidebarMenuItemSpec.active`) — each row's `path` is matched against the current URL, no router wiring needed. |
| `defaultActiveItem` | `string` | — | Label of the `items` row that should start active, as a lighter-weight alternative to adding `active: true` to the row itself (handy when the same `items` list is reused in more than one place with a different default, or built from data you don't want to mutate). Purely an initial default — after the first render it behaves exactly like a click already happened, i.e. plain self-managed selection from then on. A row's own `active` field (if any row sets it) and a matching `path` against the current URL both take priority over this when present. Has no effect on rows composed directly via `children`. |
| `width` | `number` | `256` | Pixel width when expanded (default: 256). |
| `height` | `string \| number` | `"100vh"` | CSS height (default: "100vh") — a full-viewport-height rail, the common case for a docked app-shell sidebar. `h-full`/100% would need every ancestor up the chain to have an explicit height set, which isn't the case in most real layouts — pass e.g. "100%" yourself if this Sidebar instead lives inside an already-sized flex/grid container and should fill that instead. |
| `sticky` | `boolean` | `false` | Sticks to the top of its scroll container (default: false) — same idea as Navbar's own `sticky`. A 100vh-tall Sidebar placed in the page's ordinary document flow *below* something else (e.g. a `<Navbar>` docked above it, the common "navbar on top, sidebar+content below" app-shell shape) pushes the page taller than the viewport by that something-else's own height — without `sticky`, scrolling the page scrolls the Sidebar away with it instead of leaving it in place. Pass `sticky` to pin it at the viewport's top edge instead once you scroll past whatever's above it, so it then stays fully visible on screen for the rest of the page, same as a real app-shell sidebar should. If it's inside a flex row instead (e.g. alongside a `<main>` that scrolls on its own), give that `<main>` its own `overflow-y-auto` and leave `height="100%"` on this Sidebar (see `height` above) — `sticky` isn't needed there, since the Sidebar already never scrolls out of its own container. |
| `collapsed` | `boolean` | — | Collapses to an icon-only rail (default: false) — composed `children` (nav rows, a `<SidebarHeader>`) are still rendered as given; it's up to the consumer to pass icon-only content for those (pair `header` with `headerIcon`, or branch on `collapsed` yourself for directly-composed nav rows/`SidebarMenuItem`s). A `<SidebarFooter>` (or the `footer` shortcut) has no icon-only equivalent, so it fades out automatically instead while collapsed. Leave unset manage this itself — the built-in `collapsible` toggle then works with zero extra wiring. Pass this explicitly only if something outside `Sidebar` also needs to drive/observe the collapsed state (e.g. a hamburger button elsewhere in your app); once passed, it becomes a fully controlled prop and you're responsible for updating it via `onCollapsedChange`. |
| `variant` | `SidebarVariant` | `"light"` | Visual theme (default: "light"): - "bordered"/"elevated" both float as a detached card instead of docking to a screen edge — kept as separate names since each still has its own distinct panel look on top of that shared shape: "bordered" has a thick `color`-tinted border, shadow, and rounded corners (see `color`/`borderWidth`); "elevated" has that same shadow and rounded corners but no border — depth from the shadow alone, Material-card style. Both are self-contained — an inset backdrop is included automatically (padding + a light tint of the theme accent) so the panel always reads correctly (rounded corners) with no wrapper markup needed on your end. - "minimal" — no background/border at all, blends into the page (no backdrop added, by design — that's the whole point of this one). - "gradient" — a top-to-bottom gradient built from `color` (600 → 700). |
| `color` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | `"accent"` | Accent color (default: "accent" — follows the theme accent) — one of the built-in ColorNames, or any other CSS color value (e.g. "#7c3aed" from a color-wheel picker) for a fully custom accent, unconstrained by the fixed palette. For "gradient" it's the gradient itself (600→700-equivalent; a custom hex gets a programmatically darkened second stop); for "bordered" it tints the panel's own border (has no effect on "elevated", which has no border to tint); it always also tints the built-in toggle button's hover state — pair it with the same value on your own active nav-item styling for a coordinated look. |
| `gradientTo` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | — | Second color of the "gradient" variant: a `ColorName` or any CSS color such as "#ec4899" (default: a darker shade of `color`). |
| `gradientDirection` | `GradientDirection` | `"to-bottom"` | Direction of the "gradient" variant: "to-right" \| "to-left" \| "to-bottom" \| "to-top" \| "to-br" \| "to-bl" \| "to-tr" \| "to-tl" (default: "to-bottom"). |
| `borderWidth` | `number` | — | "bordered"'s own border thickness in px (default: 2). Has no effect on any other variant — "elevated" has no border at all (shadow-only), and every other variant's border is a fixed-width neutral divider, not an adjustable, colored one. |
| `collapsible` | `boolean` | `false` | Shows a built-in collapse/expand toggle button, inline at the start of the header row (with a divider before any `<SidebarHeader>` content) — while collapsed it takes over that row on its own. Works standalone with no other props needed — `Sidebar` tracks its own collapsed state internally unless you pass `collapsed` yourself (see above). |
| `onCollapsedChange` | `(collapsed: boolean) => void` | — | Called whenever the collapsed state changes — whether toggled by the built-in button in uncontrolled mode, or requested while `collapsed` is a controlled prop. Optional either way; only needed if something outside `Sidebar` cares about the current state. |
| `onActiveItemChange` | `(item: SidebarMenuItemSpec) => void` | — | Called with the full `items` row object whenever the active row changes — a click, a URL match on mount/back-forward-navigation, or a row's `active` field changing to point elsewhere (see `SidebarMenuItemSpec.active`). Use this to read which item is active without tracking it yourself, e.g. to drive your own router's navigation or to sync active state elsewhere in your app. Not called for rows composed directly via `children` (only the data-driven `items` shortcut has a "current item" concept). |
| `onNavigate` | `(path: string, item: SidebarMenuItemSpec) => void` | — | Called with a row's `path` when it is clicked, so your router can navigate without a page reload (e.g. React Router's `navigate`). Without it, `Sidebar` changes the URL itself with `history.pushState` and fires a `popstate` event, which routers that watch the history pick up — still no reload. Modified clicks (Ctrl/Cmd/Shift, middle button) and paths on another origin keep the browser's normal link behaviour. |
| `showLabel` | `boolean` | `true` | While collapsed: true (default) shows a small label under each item icon and no tooltip; false shows icons only, with each item's label in a tooltip on hover. |
| `tooltipTransition` | `TransitionVariant` | `"bounce"` | Enter/exit transition of the tooltips shown in the collapsed rail and on the collapse button: "fade" \| "slide-up" \| "slide-down" \| "slide-left" \| "slide-right" \| "zoom" \| "zoom-out" \| "flip" \| "blur" \| "bounce" \| "rotate" \| "drop" \| "skew" (default: "bounce"). |
| `tooltipTransitionDuration` | `number` | — | Enter/exit duration in ms of those same tooltips (default: 450). |
| `tooltipColor` | `ColorName \| "neutral"` | `"accent"` | Color of those same tooltips — a ColorName, or "neutral" for the theme-inverted bubble (default: "accent"). |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class name(s) appended to the root element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; header?: string; body?: string; footer?: string; toggle?: string; }` | — | Per-part class overrides — merged after (and win over) the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay` — see §1.7.

#### `SidebarMenuItem`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `icon` | `string` | — | Icon name, e.g. "home" — see src/core/icons.ts for the available set. |
| `children` | `ReactNode` | — | The row's label. |
| `href` | `string` | — | Renders as a link when set; otherwise a `<button type="button">`. |
| `onClick` | `(event: React.MouseEvent<HTMLElement>) => void` | — | Called when the row is clicked, with the click event (not called while `disabled`). |
| `active` | `boolean` | — | Highlights this row as the current page/section (default: false). |
| `disabled` | `boolean` | — | Disables the row so it can't be clicked or focused and renders dimmed (default: false). |
| `collapsed` | `boolean` | — | Narrows to an icon-only row and shows `children` in a fly-out tooltip instead. Leave this unset to have it mirror the nearest ancestor `<l-sidebar>`'s own `collapsed` state automatically (Web Component usage only — nothing to configure). In plain React, where there's no such DOM ancestor to detect, it just defaults to `false`; pass Sidebar's own `collapsed` value here explicitly. |
| `showLabel` | `boolean` | — | While `collapsed`: true (default) keeps a small label under each icon and shows no tooltip; false makes the row icon-only and shows the label in a tooltip on hover instead. |
| `tooltipPosition` | `TooltipPortalPosition` | — | Tooltip placement while `collapsed` (default: "right" — the usual fly-out direction for a left-docked collapsed rail). |
| `dark` | `boolean` | — | Use the translucent active/hover treatment made for dark surfaces (default: false) — pass `true` alongside a Sidebar `variant="dark"/"gradient"`. |
| `vividActive` | `boolean` | — | Strengthens the active row's background/ring beyond `dark`'s usual subtle overlay (default: false) — pass `true` alongside a Sidebar `variant="gradient"` specifically (not "dark"), since those sit on a translucent or already-colorful surface where the normal overlay is much easier to lose than it is against "dark"'s plain, solid fill. Has no effect when `dark` is false. |
| `color` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | — | Accent color for the active state (default: "accent" — follows the theme accent) — one of the built-in ColorNames, or any other CSS color value; pair it with the same `color` you gave the parent Sidebar. |
| `activeStyle` | `"fill" \| "text"` | — | How `active` renders (default: "fill"): "fill" colors this row's own background/ring, the usual self-contained look; "text" only switches text color/weight and leaves the background transparent — pass this when something else already supplies the active fill, e.g. Sidebar's own `items` shortcut, which slides one shared pill behind whichever row is active instead of coloring each row's own background (a continuous slide reads far smoother than every row cross-fading its own background independently — see Navbar's identical `NavbarItem.activeStyle` for the full reasoning). Composing `<SidebarMenuItem>` directly still defaults to "fill", since there is no such shared pill to rely on outside that shortcut. |
| `slotName` | `string` | — | Advanced/rarely-needed: the internal `<slot>` this label renders into is unnamed by default (so a standalone `<l-sidebar-menu-item>My Label</l-sidebar-menu-item>` just works, the same as any plain default slot) — pass a unique value only if you're rendering several `SidebarMenuItem`s that end up sharing one *other* element's shadow root (the way Sidebar's own `items` shortcut does), where multiple unnamed slots in that single root would otherwise all compete for the same unassigned/whitespace light-DOM text nodes (see Navbar's identical `NavbarItem.slotName` for the full reasoning). Has no effect on anything other than which `<slot>` this label lives in. |
| `tooltipTransition` | `TransitionVariant` | — | Enter/exit transition of the tooltip shown while `collapsed` (default: "bounce"). |
| `tooltipTransitionDuration` | `number` | — | Enter/exit duration of the collapsed-state tooltip in ms (default: 450). |
| `tooltipColor` | `ColorName \| "neutral"` | — | Collapsed-state tooltip color — a ColorName, or "neutral" for the theme-inverted bubble (default: "accent"). |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class name(s) appended to the root element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; icon?: string; label?: string; tooltip?: string; }` | — | Per-part class overrides — merged after (and win over) the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay` — see §1.7.

### Sidebar · 3. Events

| Component | React callback | Signature | Web Component event | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Sidebar` | `onCollapsedChange` | `(collapsed: boolean) => void` | `collapsedchange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called whenever the collapsed state changes — whether toggled by the built-in button in uncontrolled mode, or requested while `collapsed` is a controlled prop. Optional either way; only needed if something outside `Sidebar` cares about the current state. |
| `Sidebar` | `onActiveItemChange` | `(item: SidebarMenuItemSpec) => void` | `activeitemchange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with the full `items` row object whenever the active row changes — a click, a URL match on mount/back-forward-navigation, or a row's `active` field changing to point elsewhere (see `SidebarMenuItemSpec.active`). Use this to read which item is active without tracking it yourself, e.g. to drive your own router's navigation or to sync active state elsewhere in your app. Not called for rows composed directly via `children` (only the data-driven `items` shortcut has a "current item" concept). |
| `Sidebar` | `onNavigate` | `(path: string, item: SidebarMenuItemSpec) => void` | — (React only) | Called with a row's `path` when it is clicked, so your router can navigate without a page reload (e.g. React Router's `navigate`). Without it, `Sidebar` changes the URL itself with `history.pushState` and fires a `popstate` event, which routers that watch the history pick up — still no reload. Modified clicks (Ctrl/Cmd/Shift, middle button) and paths on another origin keep the browser's normal link behaviour. |
| `Sidebar` | `onUpdate` | — | `update` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | The value was committed (the native `change`); `detail` = the new value (`true` / `false` for a checkbox or switch, the value `string` / `number` otherwise). React: `onChange`. |
| `SidebarMenuItem` | `onClick` | `(event: React.MouseEvent<HTMLElement>) => void` | — (React only) | Called when the row is clicked, with the click event (not called while `disabled`). |

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Sidebar` | `variant` | `light`, `dark`, `bordered`, `elevated`, `minimal`, `gradient` | `"light"` | Visual theme (default: "light"): - "bordered"/"elevated" both float as a detached card instead of docking to a screen edge — kept as separate names since each still has its own distinct panel look on top of that shared shape: "bordered" has a thick `color`-tinted border, shadow, and rounded corners (see `color`/`borderWidth`); "elevated" has that same shadow and rounded corners but no border — depth from the shadow alone, Material-card style. Both are self-contained — an inset backdrop is included automatically (padding + a light tint of the theme accent) so the panel always reads correctly (rounded corners) with no wrapper markup needed on your end. - "minimal" — no background/border at all, blends into the page (no backdrop added, by design — that's the whole point of this one). - "gradient" — a top-to-bottom gradient built from `color` (600 → 700). |
| `SidebarMenuItem` | `activeStyle` | `fill`, `text` | — | How `active` renders (default: "fill"): "fill" colors this row's own background/ring, the usual self-contained look; "text" only switches text color/weight and leaves the background transparent — pass this when something else already supplies the active fill, e.g. Sidebar's own `items` shortcut, which slides one shared pill behind whichever row is active instead of coloring each row's own background (a continuous slide reads far smoother than every row cross-fading its own background independently — see Navbar's identical `NavbarItem.activeStyle` for the full reasoning). Composing `<SidebarMenuItem>` directly still defaults to "fill", since there is no such shared pill to rely on outside that shortcut. |

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Sidebar` | `color` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | `"accent"` | Accent color (default: "accent" — follows the theme accent) — one of the built-in ColorNames, or any other CSS color value (e.g. "#7c3aed" from a color-wheel picker) for a fully custom accent, unconstrained by the fixed palette. For "gradient" it's the gradient itself (600→700-equivalent; a custom hex gets a programmatically darkened second stop); for "bordered" it tints the panel's own border (has no effect on "elevated", which has no border to tint); it always also tints the built-in toggle button's hover state — pair it with the same value on your own active nav-item styling for a coordinated look. |
| `Sidebar` | `gradientTo` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | — | Second color of the "gradient" variant: a `ColorName` or any CSS color such as "#ec4899" (default: a darker shade of `color`). |
| `Sidebar` | `tooltipColor` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | `"accent"` | Color of those same tooltips — a ColorName, or "neutral" for the theme-inverted bubble (default: "accent"). |
| `SidebarMenuItem` | `color` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | — | Accent color for the active state (default: "accent" — follows the theme accent) — one of the built-in ColorNames, or any other CSS color value; pair it with the same `color` you gave the parent Sidebar. |
| `SidebarMenuItem` | `tooltipColor` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | — | Collapsed-state tooltip color — a ColorName, or "neutral" for the theme-inverted bubble (default: "accent"). |

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set.

### Sidebar · 6. Sizes

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Sidebar` | `width` | `number` | `256` | Pixel width when expanded (default: 256). |
| `Sidebar` | `height` | `string \| number` | `"100vh"` | CSS height (default: "100vh") — a full-viewport-height rail, the common case for a docked app-shell sidebar. `h-full`/100% would need every ancestor up the chain to have an explicit height set, which isn't the case in most real layouts — pass e.g. "100%" yourself if this Sidebar instead lives inside an already-sized flex/grid container and should fill that instead. |

Works standalone with no other props needed — `Sidebar` tracks its own collapsed state internally unless you pass `collapsed` yourself (see above). |

| `SidebarMenuItem` | `active` | `boolean` | — | Highlights this row as the current page/section (default: false). |
| `SidebarMenuItem` | `disabled` | `boolean` | — | Disables the row so it can't be clicked or focused and renders dimmed (default: false). |
| `SidebarMenuItem` | `collapsed` | `boolean` | — | Narrows to an icon-only row and shows `children` in a fly-out tooltip instead. Leave this unset to have it mirror the nearest ancestor `<l-sidebar>`'s own `collapsed` state automatically (Web Component usage only — nothing to configure). In plain React, where there's no such DOM ancestor to detect, it just defaults to `false`; pass Sidebar's own `collapsed` value here explicitly. |
