Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed · disabled (dimmed, not focusable).

### Sidebar · 8. Slots / Children / Content

| Component | React prop | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `SidebarHeader` | `children` | `ReactNode` | Content rendered in the header area, docked above the nav rows. |
| `SidebarFooter` | `children` | `ReactNode` | Content rendered in the footer area, pinned below the nav rows. |
| `Sidebar` | `children` | `ReactNode` | Nav content, plus optionally a <SidebarHeader> and/or <SidebarFooter> among the children — order doesn't matter, they're pulled out and docked to the top/bottom; everything else renders as the scrollable nav body (compose it with List/ListItem, links, etc). |
| `SidebarMenuItem` | `children` | `ReactNode` | The row's label. |

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Sidebar · 9. Framework Interfaces

- `SidebarHeader`: React only (no standalone Web Component — used through its parent).
- `SidebarFooter`: React only (no standalone Web Component — used through its parent).
**`Sidebar`** → `<l-sidebar>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `width` | `width` | number |
| `height` | `height` | string |
| `collapsed` | `collapsed` | boolean |
| `variant` | `variant` | string |
| `color` | `color` | string |
| `gradientTo` | `gradient-to` | string |
| `gradientDirection` | `gradient-direction` | string |
| `collapsible` | `collapsible` | boolean |
| `header` | `header` | string |
| `headerIcon` | `header-icon` | string |
| `footer` | `footer` | string |
| `items` | `items` | json (JSON string or object property) |
| `defaultActiveItem` | `default-active-item` | string |
| `borderWidth` | `border-width` | number |
| `sticky` | `sticky` | boolean |
| `showLabel` | `show-label` | boolean |
| `transition` | `transition` | string |
| `transitionDuration` | `transition-duration` | number |
| `transitionDelay` | `transition-delay` | number |
| `tooltipTransition` | `tooltip-transition` | string |
| `tooltipTransitionDuration` | `tooltip-transition-duration` | number |
| `tooltipColor` | `tooltip-color` | string |

**`SidebarMenuItem`** → `<l-sidebar-menu-item>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `icon` | `icon` | string |
| `href` | `href` | string |
| `active` | `active` | boolean |
| `disabled` | `disabled` | boolean |
| `collapsed` | `collapsed` | boolean |
| `dark` | `dark` | boolean |
| `vividActive` | `vivid-active` | boolean |
| `color` | `color` | string |
| `showLabel` | `show-label` | boolean |
| `tooltipPosition` | `tooltip-position` | string |
| `transition` | `transition` | string |
| `transitionDuration` | `transition-duration` | number |
| `transitionDelay` | `transition-delay` | number |
| `tooltipTransition` | `tooltip-transition` | string |
| `tooltipTransitionDuration` | `tooltip-transition-duration` | number |
| `tooltipColor` | `tooltip-color` | string |

Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Basic*):

**React**
```tsx
<Sidebar
  items={[
    { label: "Dashboard", icon: "home", active: true },
    { label: "Projects", icon: "folder" },
    { label: "Team", icon: "users" },
    { label: "Settings", icon: "settings" },
  ]}
/>
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-Sidebar id="basic-sidebar"></l-Sidebar>

<script type="module">
  import "lojee-ui/elements";

  document.getElementById("basic-sidebar").items = [
    { label: "Dashboard", icon: "home", active: true },
    { label: "Projects", icon: "folder" },
    { label: "Team", icon: "users" },
    { label: "Settings", icon: "settings" },
  ];
</script>
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Sidebar · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Basic

items is the data-driven shortcut for a simple nav list — label + icon rows with no active/path/onClick/disabled state (compose SidebarMenuItem directly instead when you need that).

```tsx
<Sidebar
  items={[
    { label: "Dashboard", icon: "home", active: true },
    { label: "Projects", icon: "folder" },
    { label: "Team", icon: "users" },
    { label: "Settings", icon: "settings" },
  ]}
/>
```

#### Active item

Leave `active` unset on every row (the common case) and Sidebar determines and manages it itself: a row's path is matched against the current URL on load/back-forward-navigation, and clicking any row updates it immediately — no router wiring or state needed. Clicking a row with a path changes the URL without reloading the page (history.pushState); pass onNavigate to hand the path to your own router instead. defaultActiveItem seeds which row starts active (Dashboard here) without touching the items themselves — purely an initial default, so clicking around afterward still works normally. onActiveItemChange reports the full item object whenever the active row changes, e.g. to sync it elsewhere.

```tsx
<Sidebar
  defaultActiveItem="Dashboard"
  onActiveItemChange={(item) => console.log(item)}
  items={[
    { label: "Dashboard", icon: "home", path: "/dashboard" },
    { label: "Projects", icon: "folder", path: "/projects" },
    { label: "Team", icon: "users", path: "/team" },
    { label: "Settings", icon: "settings", path: "/settings" },
  ]}
/>
```

#### With header and footer

header/headerIcon and footer are the data-driven shortcut for a workspace name pinned above the nav content and a user row pinned below it (compose SidebarHeader/SidebarFooter directly instead for custom markup like an avatar).

```tsx
<Sidebar
  header="Lojee Inc"
  headerIcon="zap"
  footer="Jordan Diaz"
  items={[
    { label: "Dashboard", icon: "home", active: true },
    { label: "Projects", icon: "folder" },
    { label: "Team", icon: "users" },
    { label: "Settings", icon: "settings" },
  ]}
/>
```

#### Categories

Mix { category, items } entries into items for labeled, collapsible section groups — click a heading to toggle it. Defaults open for the first group or one containing an active row; every other group starts closed.

```tsx
<Sidebar
  collapsible
  header="Lojee Inc"
  headerIcon="zap"
  footer="Jordan Diaz"
  items={[
    {
      category: "Workspace",
      items: [
        { label: "Dashboard", icon: "home", active: true },
        { label: "Projects", icon: "folder" },
      ],
    },
    {
      category: "Account",
      items: [
        { label: "Team", icon: "users" },
        { label: "Settings", icon: "settings" },
      ],
    },
  ]}
/>
```

#### Variants

```tsx
<Sidebar
  variant="dark"
  header="Lojee Inc"
  footer="Jordan Diaz"
  items={[
    { label: "Dashboard", icon: "home", active: true },
    { label: "Projects", icon: "folder" },
    { label: "Team", icon: "users" },
    { label: "Settings", icon: "settings" },
  ]}
/>

{/* Also available:
    variant="bordered" / "elevated" — detached-panel looks (rounded corners, floats
      inside a page instead of docking to a screen edge). Their backdrop (padding + a neutral
      background) is built in, so no extra markup is needed — just give them a height, e.g.
      height="100%" inside a sized parent. "bordered" is a solid panel on the page surface with a color-tinted
      border (see `color`/`borderWidth`); "elevated" is the same solid panel on the page surface but
      shadow-only, no border.
    variant="minimal"  — no background/border at all, blends into the page.
    variant="gradient" — a top-to-bottom gradient built from `color` (600 → 700). */}
```

_4 more examples are on the docs page._

### Sidebar · 11. Component Composition

- Built from `SidebarHeader`, `SidebarFooter`, `Sidebar`, `SidebarMenuItem` — use them together as shown in the examples above.

### Sidebar · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: none beyond native element semantics.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: `aria-current`, `aria-disabled`, `aria-expanded`, `aria-hidden`, `aria-label`.
- Keyboard: native keyboard behavior of the underlying element (Tab to focus, Enter / Space to activate).
- Focus: uses the browser focus outline / the underlying control's ring.
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Sidebar · 13. Responsive Behavior

- No breakpoint-specific rules — it is fluid and adapts to its container width.
- Measures its own container (container queries / ResizeObserver), so it adapts inside narrow panels, not only on small viewports.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Sidebar · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `Sidebar`: `root`, `header`, `body`, `footer`, `toggle` · `SidebarMenuItem`: `root`, `icon`, `label`, `tooltip`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with the theme accent; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: no per-component motion props.

## Top Bar

*Category: Navigation.* Exports: `TopBar`.

### Top Bar · 1. Component Overview

An app bar for the top of a screen or page: a title (with an optional back button), flexible center content, and a row of icon actions.

- React: `import { TopBar } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-top-bar>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Top Bar · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `TopBar`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` | `ReactNode` | — | Page or app title. |
| `subtitle` | `ReactNode` | — | Smaller line under the title. |
| `onBack` | `() => void` | — | Shows a back arrow before the title and calls this when it is pressed. |
| `back` | `boolean` | — | Force the back arrow on or off. Defaults to on when `onBack` is given; the Web Component always has an `onBack` (its `back` event), so there it is `back="true"` that shows the arrow. With `back` on and no `onBack`, pressing it goes back in the browser history. |
| `menu` | `boolean` | — | Built-in menu button (a hamburger) at the very start — e.g. to open a sidebar. Shown when `onMenuClick` is given (or `menu` is true; on the Web Component set `menu="true"` and listen for the `menuclick` event). |
| `onMenuClick` | `() => void` | — | Called with no arguments when the built-in menu (hamburger) button is pressed. |
| `menuLabel` | `string` | `"Menu"` | Accessible name of the menu button (default: "Menu"). |
| `search` | `boolean` | `false` | Built-in search: adds a search button that opens a search field in the bar. Enter submits (`onSearch`), typing reports (`onSearchChange`), Escape or the close button dismisses it. |
| `searchPlaceholder` | `string` | `"Search…"` | Placeholder text of the search field opened by `search`. |
| `onSearch` | `(query: string) => void` | — | Called with the query string when the user presses Enter in the search field. |
| `onSearchChange` | `(query: string) => void` | — | Called with the current query string on every keystroke in the search field. |
| `backLabel` | `string` | `"Back"` | Accessible name of the back button (default: "Back"). |
| `leading` | `ReactNode` | — | Free-form content at the very start — a logo, a menu toggle, an avatar. |
| `children` | `ReactNode` | — | Centered/flexible content between the title and the actions — a search field, tabs, breadcrumbs. |
| `actions` | `TopBarAction[]` | — | Icon buttons at the end of the bar. |
| `onActionClick` | `(action: TopBarAction, index: number) => void` | — | Called with the pressed action and its index. |
| `trailing` | `ReactNode` | — | Free-form content after the actions — an avatar, a button. |
| `variant` | `TopBarVariant` | `"light"` | Look of the bar (default: "light"): - "light" — the page surface with a bottom border. - "elevated" — the page surface with a shadow instead of a border. - "minimal" — no background, blends into the page. - "accent" — a solid `color` background with white text (the theme's accent by default). |
| `color` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | `"accent"` | Background color for `variant="accent"` (default: "accent", which follows the theme's accent color) — one of the built-in ColorNames, or any other CSS color value. Ignored by the other variants. |
| `size` | `TopBarSize` | `"md"` | Bar height: "sm" 48px, "md" 56px (default), "lg" 64px. |
| `sticky` | `boolean` | `false` | Sticks to the top of its scroll container (default: false). |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class name(s) appended to the root element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; title?: string; subtitle?: string; center?: string; actions?: string; action?: string; badge?: string; }` | — | Per-part class overrides — merged after (and win over) the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect` — see §1.7.

### Top Bar · 3. Events

| Component | React callback | Signature | Web Component event | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `TopBar` | `onBack` | `() => void` | `back` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Shows a back arrow before the title and calls this when it is pressed. |
| `TopBar` | `onMenuClick` | `() => void` | `menuclick` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with no arguments when the built-in menu (hamburger) button is pressed. |
| `TopBar` | `onSearch` | `(query: string) => void` | `search` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with the query string when the user presses Enter in the search field. |
| `TopBar` | `onSearchChange` | `(query: string) => void` | `searchchange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with the current query string on every keystroke in the search field. |
| `TopBar` | `onActionClick` | `(action: TopBarAction, index: number) => void` | `actionclick` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with the pressed action and its index. |

### Top Bar · 4. Variants

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `TopBar` | `variant` | `light`, `accent`, `minimal`, `elevated` | `"light"` | Look of the bar (default: "light"): - "light" — the page surface with a bottom border. - "elevated" — the page surface with a shadow instead of a border. - "minimal" — no background, blends into the page. - "accent" — a solid `color` background with white text (the theme's accent by default). |

### Top Bar · 5. Colors and Theming

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `TopBar` | `color` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | `"accent"` | Background color for `variant="accent"` (default: "accent", which follows the theme's accent color) — one of the built-in ColorNames, or any other CSS color value. Ignored by the other variants. |

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set.

### Top Bar · 6. Sizes

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `TopBar` | `size` | `sm`, `md`, `lg` | `"md"` | Bar height: "sm" 48px, "md" 56px (default), "lg" 64px. |

### Top Bar · 7. Component States

| Component | Prop | Type | Default | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `TopBar` | `sticky` | `boolean` | `false` | Sticks to the top of its scroll container (default: false). |

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### Top Bar · 8. Slots / Children / Content

| Component | React prop | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `TopBar` | `title` | `ReactNode` | Page or app title. |
| `TopBar` | `subtitle` | `ReactNode` | Smaller line under the title. |
| `TopBar` | `leading` | `ReactNode` | Free-form content at the very start — a logo, a menu toggle, an avatar. |
| `TopBar` | `children` | `ReactNode` | Centered/flexible content between the title and the actions — a search field, tabs, breadcrumbs. |
| `TopBar` | `trailing` | `ReactNode` | Free-form content after the actions — an avatar, a button. |

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Top Bar · 9. Framework Interfaces

**`TopBar`** → `<l-top-bar>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `title` | `title` | string |
| `subtitle` | `subtitle` | string |
| `back` | `back` | boolean |
| `backLabel` | `back-label` | string |
| `menu` | `menu` | boolean |
| `menuLabel` | `menu-label` | string |
| `search` | `search` | boolean |
| `searchPlaceholder` | `search-placeholder` | string |
| `actions` | `actions` | json (JSON string or object property) |
| `variant` | `variant` | string |
| `color` | `color` | string |
| `size` | `size` | string |
| `sticky` | `sticky` | boolean |
| `transition` | `transition` | string |
| `transitionDuration` | `transition-duration` | number |
| `transitionDelay` | `transition-delay` | number |
| `hoverEffect` | `hover-effect` | string |

Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Back button*):

**React**
```tsx
<TopBar
  title="Order #1042"
  subtitle="Placed today"
  onBack={() => navigate(-1)}
  actions={[{ icon: "bell", label: "Notifications", badge: "3" }]}
/>
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-top-bar id="top-bar-back" title="Order #1042" subtitle="Placed today" back="true"></l-top-bar>

<script type="module">
  import "lojee-ui/elements";

  const bar = document.getElementById("top-bar-back");
  bar.actions = [{ icon: "bell", label: "Notifications", badge: "3" }];
  bar.addEventListener("back", () => history.back());
</script>
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Top Bar · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Back button

```tsx
<TopBar
  title="Order #1042"
  subtitle="Placed today"
  onBack={() => navigate(-1)}
  actions={[{ icon: "bell", label: "Notifications", badge: "3" }]}
/>
```

#### Center content

The default slot (children) sits between the title and the actions — a search field, tabs, breadcrumbs. leading and trailing are free-form slots at the two ends.

```tsx
<TopBar
  title="Lojee"
  leading={<Logo />}
  actions={[{ icon: "bell", label: "Notifications", badge: "3" }]}
  trailing={<Avatar initials="JD" size="sm" />}
>
  <input type="search" placeholder="Search…" />   {/* center content */}
</TopBar>
```

#### Built-in menu, back and search

Built-in behavior, no wiring needed: `menu` adds a hamburger (onMenuClick), `back` goes back in the browser history when you give no onBack, and `search` adds a search button that opens a search field in the bar — Enter submits (onSearch), typing reports (onSearchChange), Escape closes it.

```tsx
<TopBar
  title="Library"
  menu                                   // hamburger — or just pass onMenuClick
  onMenuClick={() => setSidebarOpen(true)}
  search                                 // built-in search button + field
  searchPlaceholder="Search components…"
  onSearch={(query) => runSearch(query)} // Enter
  onSearchChange={(query) => filter(query)}
  back                                   // no onBack → goes back in history
/>
```

#### Action callback

onActionClick fires with the pressed action (an `actionclick` event on the Web Component). An action with an href renders as a link.

```tsx
<TopBar
  title="Inbox"
  actions={[
    { icon: "search", label: "Search" },
    { icon: "bell", label: "Notifications", badge: "3" },
  ]}
  onActionClick={(action, index) => console.log(action.label, index)}
/>
```

#### Transitions

Enter transitions via `transition` (with `transitionDuration` / `transitionDelay`) and hover effects via `hoverEffect`. They play on mount — reload the page, or use Replay in the playground.

```tsx
const actions = [
  { icon: "search", label: "Search" },
  { icon: "bell", label: "Notifications", badge: "3" },
  { icon: "settings", label: "Settings" },
];

<TopBar title="Fade" actions={actions} transition="fade" />
<TopBar title="Slide down" actions={actions} transition="slide-down" />
<TopBar title="Slide right" actions={actions} transition="slide-right" transitionDelay={100} />
<TopBar title="Zoom" actions={actions} transition="zoom" />
<TopBar title="Blur" actions={actions} transition="blur" />
<TopBar title="Drop" actions={actions} transition="drop" transitionDuration={700} />

<TopBar title="Scale" actions={actions} hoverEffect="scale" />
<TopBar title="Ring" actions={actions} hoverEffect="ring" />
<TopBar title="Glow" actions={actions} hoverEffect="glow" />
```

### Top Bar · 11. Component Composition

- Combined in its own examples with: `Avatar`.

### Top Bar · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: none beyond native element semantics.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: `aria-disabled`, `aria-expanded`, `aria-label`.
- Keyboard: has keyboard handling (e.g. Escape / arrow keys) — see the docs page for the exact keys.
- Focus: visible focus ring (`focus-visible`).
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Top Bar · 13. Responsive Behavior

- Breakpoint modifiers in its source: `lg`, `md`, `sm`.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Top Bar · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `TopBar`: `root`, `title`, `subtitle`, `center`, `actions`, `action`, `badge`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with `color`; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect`.

## Header

*Category: Navigation.* Exports: `Header`.

### Header · 1. Component Overview

A page-level header — title, description, and actions, e.g. the bar at the top of a dashboard page.

- React: `import { Header } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-header>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Header · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `Header`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` **required** | `ReactNode` | — | Main heading content. |
| `description` | `ReactNode` | — | Secondary text shown below the title. |
| `breadcrumbs` | `ReactNode` | — | Optional content above the title, e.g. a <Breadcrumbs> trail. |
| `actions` | `ReactNode` | — | Right-side content — action buttons. |
| `variant` | `HeaderVariant` | `"light"` | Visual theme (default: "light"), identical set to Sidebar/Navbar: - "bordered"/"elevated" both float as a detached card instead of sitting flush in the page's content flow — kept as separate names since each still has its own distinct look on top of that shared shape: "bordered" has a thick `color`-tinted border, shadow, and rounded corners (see `color`/`borderWidth`); "elevated" has that same shadow and rounded corners but no border — depth from the shadow alone, Material-card style. Both are self-contained — an inset backdrop is included automatically (padding + `bg-surface-muted`) so the card always reads correctly (rounded corners) with no wrapper markup needed on your end. - "dark" — a deep shade of the theme accent (accent-950), title/description switch to white/white-ish. - "minimal" — no background/border at all, blends fully into the page (unlike "light", which keeps a white background and bottom divider). - "gradient" — a left-to-right gradient built from `color` (600 → 700). |
| `color` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | `"accent"` | Accent color (default: "accent" — follows the theme accent) — one of the built-in ColorNames, or any other CSS color value (e.g. "#7c3aed" from a color-wheel picker) for a fully custom accent, unconstrained by the fixed palette. For "gradient" it's the gradient itself (600→700-equivalent; a custom hex gets a programmatically darkened second stop); for "bordered" it tints the card's own border (has no effect on "elevated", which has no border to tint). Has no effect on "light"/"dark"/"minimal". |
| `gradientTo` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | — | Second color of the "gradient" variant: a `ColorName` or any CSS color such as "#ec4899" (default: a darker shade of `color`). |
| `gradientDirection` | `GradientDirection` | `"to-right"` | Direction of the "gradient" variant: "to-right" \| "to-left" \| "to-bottom" \| "to-top" \| "to-br" \| "to-bl" \| "to-tr" \| "to-tl" (default: "to-right"). |
| `borderWidth` | `number` | — | "bordered"'s own border thickness in px (default: 2). Has no effect on any other variant. |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class name(s) applied to the root element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; breadcrumbs?: string; title?: string; description?: string; actions?: string; }` | — | Per-part class overrides — merged after (and win over) the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay` — see §1.7.

### Header · 3. Events

_No component-specific events._ Native DOM events (`click`, `input`, `focus`…) bubble from the rendered element as usual.

### Header · 4. Variants

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Header` | `variant` | `light`, `dark`, `bordered`, `elevated`, `minimal`, `gradient` | `"light"` | Visual theme (default: "light"), identical set to Sidebar/Navbar: - "bordered"/"elevated" both float as a detached card instead of sitting flush in the page's content flow — kept as separate names since each still has its own distinct look on top of that shared shape: "bordered" has a thick `color`-tinted border, shadow, and rounded corners (see `color`/`borderWidth`); "elevated" has that same shadow and rounded corners but no border — depth from the shadow alone, Material-card style. Both are self-contained — an inset backdrop is included automatically (padding + `bg-surface-muted`) so the card always reads correctly (rounded corners) with no wrapper markup needed on your end. - "dark" — a deep shade of the theme accent (accent-950), title/description switch to white/white-ish. - "minimal" — no background/border at all, blends fully into the page (unlike "light", which keeps a white background and bottom divider). - "gradient" — a left-to-right gradient built from `color` (600 → 700). |

### Header · 5. Colors and Theming

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Header` | `color` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | `"accent"` | Accent color (default: "accent" — follows the theme accent) — one of the built-in ColorNames, or any other CSS color value (e.g. "#7c3aed" from a color-wheel picker) for a fully custom accent, unconstrained by the fixed palette. For "gradient" it's the gradient itself (600→700-equivalent; a custom hex gets a programmatically darkened second stop); for "bordered" it tints the card's own border (has no effect on "elevated", which has no border to tint). Has no effect on "light"/"dark"/"minimal". |
| `Header` | `gradientTo` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | — | Second color of the "gradient" variant: a `ColorName` or any CSS color such as "#ec4899" (default: a darker shade of `color`). |

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set.

### Header · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### Header · 7. Component States

_No dedicated state props — the visual states are default, hover, focus-visible and active._

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### Header · 8. Slots / Children / Content

| Component | React prop | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Header` | `title` | `ReactNode` | Main heading content. |
| `Header` | `description` | `ReactNode` | Secondary text shown below the title. |
| `Header` | `breadcrumbs` | `ReactNode` | Optional content above the title, e.g. a <Breadcrumbs> trail. |
| `Header` | `actions` | `ReactNode` | Right-side content — action buttons. |

Web Component extras (slots and plain HTML attributes accepted by the `<l-*>` tag in addition to the props above):

| Component | Name | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Header` | `heading` | `string` | Text shown as the title. Named `heading` here because `title` is a native HTMLElement attribute. |

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Header · 9. Framework Interfaces

**`Header`** → `<l-header>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `heading` | `heading` | string |
| `variant` | `variant` | string |
| `color` | `color` | string |
| `gradientTo` | `gradient-to` | string |
| `gradientDirection` | `gradient-direction` | string |
| `borderWidth` | `border-width` | number |
| `transition` | `transition` | string |
| `transitionDuration` | `transition-duration` | number |
| `transitionDelay` | `transition-delay` | number |

Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Basic*):

**React**
```tsx
<Header title="Team settings" description="Manage members, roles, and billing for your workspace." />
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-Header heading="Team settings" description="Manage members, roles, and billing for your workspace."></l-Header>

<script type="module">import "lojee-ui/elements";</script>
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Header · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Basic

A title and a short description.

```tsx
<Header title="Team settings" description="Manage members, roles, and billing for your workspace." />
```

#### With actions

Action buttons render on the right, aligned with the title.

```tsx
<Header
  title="Projects"
  description="All projects across your workspace."
  actions={
    <>
      <Button variant="outline" label="Import" />
      <Button label="New project" />
    </>
  }
/>
```

#### Variants

```tsx
<Header
  variant="dark"
  title="Team settings"
  description="Manage members, roles, and billing for your workspace."
  actions={<Button label="New project" />}
/>

{/* Also available:
    variant="bordered" / "elevated" — detached-panel looks (rounded corners, floats
      inside a page instead of sitting flush in its content flow). Their backdrop (padding + a
      neutral background) is built in, so no extra markup is needed. "bordered" is a solid page-surface
      card with a color-tinted border (see `color`/`borderWidth`); "elevated" is the same card
      but shadow-only, no border.
    variant="minimal"  — no background/border at all, blends into the page.
    variant="gradient" — a left-to-right gradient built from `color` (600 → 700). */}
```

#### With breadcrumbs

A breadcrumb trail rendered above the title.

```tsx
<Header
  breadcrumbs={
    <Breadcrumbs>
      <BreadcrumbItem href="#" icon="home">Home</BreadcrumbItem>
      <BreadcrumbItem href="#">Projects</BreadcrumbItem>
      <BreadcrumbItem>Lojee Website Redesign</BreadcrumbItem>
    </Breadcrumbs>
  }
  title="Lojee Website Redesign"
  description="Created by Jordan Diaz on Sep 12."
/>
```

#### Transitions

Enter transitions via `transition` (with `transitionDuration` / `transitionDelay`). They play on mount — reload the page, or use Replay in the playground.

```tsx
<Header title="Fade" description="Enter transition" transition="fade" />
<Header title="Slide down" description="Enter transition" transition="slide-down" />
<Header title="Slide right" description="Enter transition" transition="slide-right" transitionDelay={100} />
<Header title="Zoom" description="Enter transition" transition="zoom" />
<Header title="Blur" description="Enter transition" transition="blur" />
<Header title="Drop" description="Enter transition" transition="drop" transitionDuration={700} />
```

### Header · 11. Component Composition

- Combined in its own examples with: `BreadcrumbItem`, `Breadcrumbs`, `Button`.

### Header · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: none beyond native element semantics.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: none.
- Keyboard: native keyboard behavior of the underlying element (Tab to focus, Enter / Space to activate).
- Focus: uses the browser focus outline / the underlying control's ring.
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Header · 13. Responsive Behavior

- No breakpoint-specific rules — it is fluid and adapts to its container width.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Header · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `Header`: `root`, `breadcrumbs`, `title`, `description`, `actions`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with `color`; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`.

## Footer

*Category: Navigation.* Exports: `Footer`.

### Footer · 1. Component Overview

A site-wide bottom footer with link columns and a copyright bar.

- React: `import { Footer } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-footer>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Footer · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `Footer`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `children` | `ReactNode` | — | Main content area — link columns, etc. |
| `bottom` | `ReactNode` | — | Bottom bar content — copyright, legal links. Rendered below a top border. |
| `variant` | `FooterVariant` | `"light"` | Visual theme (default: "light"): - "dark" — slate-900 background, muted light text for the bottom bar. - "minimal" — no background at all, blends into the page. - "accent" — a solid `color` background with white text; `color` defaults to the theme's accent, so it changes with the accent picker. |
| `color` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | `"accent"` | Background color for `variant="accent"` (default: "accent", which follows the theme's accent color) — one of the built-in ColorNames, or any other CSS color value. Ignored by the other variants. |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class name(s) applied to the root element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; content?: string; bottom?: string; }` | — | Per-part class overrides — merged after (and win over) the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay` — see §1.7.

### Footer · 3. Events

_No component-specific events._ Native DOM events (`click`, `input`, `focus`…) bubble from the rendered element as usual.

### Footer · 4. Variants

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Footer` | `variant` | `light`, `dark`, `minimal`, `accent` | `"light"` | Visual theme (default: "light"): - "dark" — slate-900 background, muted light text for the bottom bar. - "minimal" — no background at all, blends into the page. - "accent" — a solid `color` background with white text; `color` defaults to the theme's accent, so it changes with the accent picker. |

### Footer · 5. Colors and Theming

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Footer` | `color` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | `"accent"` | Background color for `variant="accent"` (default: "accent", which follows the theme's accent color) — one of the built-in ColorNames, or any other CSS color value. Ignored by the other variants. |

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set.

### Footer · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### Footer · 7. Component States

_No dedicated state props — the visual states are default, hover, focus-visible and active._

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### Footer · 8. Slots / Children / Content

| Component | React prop | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Footer` | `children` | `ReactNode` | Main content area — link columns, etc. |
| `Footer` | `bottom` | `ReactNode` | Bottom bar content — copyright, legal links. Rendered below a top border. |

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Footer · 9. Framework Interfaces

**`Footer`** → `<l-footer>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `bottom` | `bottom` | string |
| `variant` | `variant` | string |
| `color` | `color` | string |
| `transition` | `transition` | string |
| `transitionDuration` | `transition-duration` | number |
| `transitionDelay` | `transition-delay` | number |

```tsx
  <div>

    <ul>
      <li><a href="#">Features</a></li>
      <li><a href="#">Pricing</a></li>
      <li><a href="#">Changelog</a></li>
    </ul>
  </div>

    <ul>

      <li><a href="#">Blog</a></li>
    </ul>

    <ul>
      <li><a href="#">Docs</a></li>
      <li><a href="#">Guides</a></li>
      <li><a href="#">Support</a></li>
    </ul>
  </div>
  <div>
    <h4>Legal</h4>
```

**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-Footer bottom="© 2026 Lojee, Inc. All rights reserved.">
  <div>
    <h4>Product</h4>
```

Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Footer · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Basic

A grid of link columns above a copyright line.

```tsx
<Footer bottom="© 2026 Lojee, Inc. All rights reserved.">
  <div>
    <h4>Product</h4>
    <ul>
      <li><a href="#">Features</a></li>
      <li><a href="#">Pricing</a></li>
      <li><a href="#">Changelog</a></li>
    </ul>
  </div>
  <div>
    <h4>Company</h4>
    <ul>
      <li><a href="#">About</a></li>
      <li><a href="#">Careers</a></li>
      <li><a href="#">Blog</a></li>
    </ul>
  </div>
  <div>
    <h4>Resources</h4>
    <ul>
      <li><a href="#">Docs</a></li>
      <li><a href="#">Guides</a></li>
      <li><a href="#">Support</a></li>
    </ul>
  </div>
  <div>
    <h4>Legal</h4>
    <ul>
      <li><a href="#">Privacy</a></li>
      <li><a href="#">Terms</a></li>
    </ul>
  </div>
</Footer>
```

#### Variants

Four looks: "light" (default), "dark", "minimal" (no background, blends into the page) and "accent" (a solid color background — the theme's accent by default, so it changes with the accent picker; pass color for another).

```tsx
<Footer variant="dark" bottom={<span className="text-slate-400">© 2026 Lojee, Inc.</span>}>
  <LinkColumns />
</Footer>

{/* Also available: variant="minimal" (no background, blends into the page)
    and variant="accent" — a solid background in the theme accent: */}
<Footer variant="accent" bottom="© 2026 Lojee, Inc.">
  <LinkColumns />
</Footer>

{/* pin another color */}
<Footer variant="accent" color="emerald" bottom="© 2026 Lojee, Inc." />
```

#### Bottom bar only

Just the bottom bar — copyright plus a couple of legal links, no columns.

```tsx
<Footer
  bottom={
    <div className="flex flex-wrap items-center justify-between gap-3">
      <span>© 2026 Lojee, Inc. All rights reserved.</span>
      <div className="flex items-center gap-4">
        <a href="#">Privacy</a>
        <a href="#">Terms</a>
      </div>
    </div>
  }
/>
```

#### Transitions

Enter transitions via `transition` (with `transitionDuration` / `transitionDelay`). They play on mount — reload the page, or use Replay in the playground.

```tsx
<Footer bottom="Fade" transition="fade" />
<Footer bottom="Slide down" transition="slide-down" />
<Footer bottom="Slide right" transition="slide-right" transitionDelay={100} />
<Footer bottom="Zoom" transition="zoom" />
<Footer bottom="Blur" transition="blur" />
<Footer bottom="Drop" transition="drop" transitionDuration={700} />
```

### Footer · 11. Component Composition

- Standalone: drop it into any layout (Card, Section, Grid, Container, App shell) or inside forms and overlays.

### Footer · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: none beyond native element semantics.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: none.
- Keyboard: native keyboard behavior of the underlying element (Tab to focus, Enter / Space to activate).
- Focus: uses the browser focus outline / the underlying control's ring.
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Footer · 13. Responsive Behavior

- Breakpoint modifiers in its source: `sm`.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Footer · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `Footer`: `root`, `content`, `bottom`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with `color`; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`.

## Navigation Menu

*Category: Navigation.* Exports: `NavigationMenu`.

### Navigation Menu · 1. Component Overview

A data-driven row of top-level site nav links with an active-item indicator.

- React: `import { NavigationMenu } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-navigation-menu>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Navigation Menu · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `NavigationMenu`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `items` **required** | `NavigationMenuItem[]` | — | The menu entries, in display order; each has a `label` and optionally `href`, `icon`, `active`, `disabled` and `content`. |
| `orientation` | `NavigationMenuOrientation` | `"horizontal"` | Layout direction of the menu: "horizontal" (default) or "vertical". |
| `color` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | `"accent"` | Color of the active item (default: "accent" — follows the theme accent) — one of the built-in ColorNames (including "accent", which follows the theme accent), or any other CSS color value. Same as Navbar / Sidebar. |
| `variant` | `ActiveVariant` | — | How the active item is drawn: "solid", "outline" or "soft". Leave it out to follow the theme's active-item style (`ThemeProvider`'s `defaultActiveVariant`, default "solid"). |
| `defaultActiveItem` | `string` | — | Only an initial default (see `NavigationMenuItem.active`): which item starts selected when nothing else — a matching `href`, an `active: true` entry — already determines it. |
| `onActiveItemChange` | `(item: NavigationMenuItem) => void` | — | Called with the full item whenever the active item changes — a click, a URL match on mount/back-forward navigation, or an item's `active` field changing to point elsewhere. Same as Sidebar / Navbar. |
| `onChange` | `(index: number, item: NavigationMenuItem) => void` | — | Called whenever a (non-disabled) item is clicked, with its index and data. |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class(es) added to the root element, merged before `classNames.root`. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; item?: string; activeItem?: string; indicator?: string; content?: string; }` | — | Per-part class overrides — merged after (and win over) the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect` — see §1.7.

### Navigation Menu · 3. Events

| Component | React callback | Signature | Web Component event | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `NavigationMenu` | `onActiveItemChange` | `(item: NavigationMenuItem) => void` | `activeitemchange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with the full item whenever the active item changes — a click, a URL match on mount/back-forward navigation, or an item's `active` field changing to point elsewhere. Same as Sidebar / Navbar. |
| `NavigationMenu` | `onChange` | `(index: number, item: NavigationMenuItem) => void` | `change` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called whenever a (non-disabled) item is clicked, with its index and data. |
| `NavigationMenu` | `onUpdate` | — | `update` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | The value was committed (the native `change`); `detail` = the new value (`true` / `false` for a checkbox or switch, the value `string` / `number` otherwise). React: `onChange`. |

### Navigation Menu · 4. Variants

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `NavigationMenu` | `orientation` | `horizontal`, `vertical` | `"horizontal"` | Layout direction of the menu: "horizontal" (default) or "vertical". |
| `NavigationMenu` | `variant` | `solid`, `outline`, `soft` | — | How the active item is drawn: "solid", "outline" or "soft". Leave it out to follow the theme's active-item style (`ThemeProvider`'s `defaultActiveVariant`, default "solid"). |

### Navigation Menu · 5. Colors and Theming

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `NavigationMenu` | `color` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | `"accent"` | Color of the active item (default: "accent" — follows the theme accent) — one of the built-in ColorNames (including "accent", which follows the theme accent), or any other CSS color value. Same as Navbar / Sidebar. |

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set.

### Navigation Menu · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### Navigation Menu · 7. Component States

_No dedicated state props — the visual states are default, hover, focus-visible and active._

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### Navigation Menu · 8. Slots / Children / Content

_No children or slots — it is configured entirely through props._

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Navigation Menu · 9. Framework Interfaces

**`NavigationMenu`** → `<l-navigation-menu>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `items` | `items` | json (JSON string or object property) |
| `orientation` | `orientation` | string |
| `color` | `color` | string |
| `variant` | `variant` | string |
| `defaultActiveItem` | `default-active-item` | string |
| `transition` | `transition` | string |
| `transitionDuration` | `transition-duration` | number |
| `transitionDelay` | `transition-delay` | number |
| `hoverEffect` | `hover-effect` | string |

Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Basic*):

**React**
```tsx
<NavigationMenu
  items={[
    { label: "Home", href: "#", active: true },
    { label: "Products", href: "#" },
    { label: "Pricing", href: "#" },
    { label: "About", href: "#" },
    { label: "Contact", href: "#" },
  ]}
/>
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-NavigationMenu id="nav-menu-basic"></l-NavigationMenu>

<script type="module">
  import "lojee-ui/elements";

  document.getElementById("nav-menu-basic").items = [
    { label: "Home", href: "#", active: true },
    { label: "Products", href: "#" },
    { label: "Pricing", href: "#" },
    { label: "About", href: "#" },
    { label: "Contact", href: "#" },
  ];
</script>
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Navigation Menu · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Basic

A horizontal row of links, one marked active — click any of them and the active item moves, just like Sidebar and Navbar.

```tsx
<NavigationMenu
  items={[
    { label: "Home", href: "#", active: true },
    { label: "Products", href: "#" },
    { label: "Pricing", href: "#" },
    { label: "About", href: "#" },
    { label: "Contact", href: "#" },
  ]}
/>
```

#### Vertical orientation

```tsx
<NavigationMenu
  orientation="vertical"
  items={[
    { label: "Overview", href: "#", active: true },
    { label: "Analytics", href: "#" },
    { label: "Reports", href: "#" },
    { label: "Settings", href: "#" },
  ]}
/>
```

#### With icons

Each item can carry an icon, rendered before the label.

```tsx
<NavigationMenu
  items={[
    { label: "Home", href: "#", icon: "home", active: true },
    { label: "Search", href: "#", icon: "search" },
    { label: "Notifications", href: "#", icon: "bell" },
    { label: "Profile", href: "#", icon: "user" },
  ]}
/>
```

#### Selection callback

Like Sidebar and Navbar, the menu keeps track of the active item itself — click one and it updates. onActiveItemChange fires with the whole item whenever it changes (a click, a URL match, or an active-prop change).

```tsx
const [active, setActive] = useState("Home");

<NavigationMenu
  items={[
    { label: "Home", href: "/", active: true },
    { label: "Products", href: "/products" },
    { label: "Pricing", href: "/pricing" },
  ]}
  onActiveItemChange={(item) => setActive(item.label)}
/>

{/* No state needed to highlight the clicked item — and an item's href is also matched
    against the current URL on load and on back/forward. */}
```

#### Paired with content

When items carry `content`, NavigationMenu manages the active item itself — like Tabs, but with a nav-style look. Handy for a settings page.

```tsx
<NavigationMenu
  orientation="vertical"
  items={[
    { label: "Profile", icon: "user", content: <ProfilePanel /> },
    { label: "Notifications", icon: "bell", content: <NotificationsPanel /> },
    { label: "Security", icon: "lock", content: <SecurityPanel /> },
  ]}
/>
```

_2 more examples are on the docs page._

### Navigation Menu · 11. Component Composition

- Data-driven: pass `items` instead of child components (works the same as a Web Component).

### Navigation Menu · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: none beyond native element semantics.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: `aria-current`, `aria-disabled`, `aria-hidden`.
- Keyboard: native keyboard behavior of the underlying element (Tab to focus, Enter / Space to activate).
- Focus: uses the browser focus outline / the underlying control's ring.
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Navigation Menu · 13. Responsive Behavior

- No breakpoint-specific rules — it is fluid and adapts to its container width.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Navigation Menu · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `NavigationMenu`: `root`, `item`, `activeItem`, `indicator`, `content`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with `color`; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect`.

## Bottom Navigation

*Category: Navigation.* Exports: `BottomNavigation`.

### Bottom Navigation · 1. Component Overview

A mobile-style bottom tab bar — icon and label per tab, active tab highlighted.

- React: `import { BottomNavigation } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-bottom-navigation>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Bottom Navigation · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `BottomNavigation`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `items` **required** | `BottomNavigationItem[]` | — | The tabs to display, in order — each with an icon, label, optional `href` and optional badge. |
| `color` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | `"accent"` | Color of the active tab (default: "accent", which follows the theme accent) — one of the built-in ColorNames, or any other CSS color value. Same as Navbar / Sidebar. |
| `variant` | `ActiveVariant \| "text"` | — | How the active tab is drawn: "solid", "outline" or "soft" fill, or "text" — no fill at all, only the icon and label are highlighted in `color`. Leave it out to follow the theme's active-item style (`ThemeProvider`'s `defaultActiveVariant`, default "solid"; the theme never picks "text"). |
| `defaultActiveItem` | `string` | — | Only an initial default (see `BottomNavigationItem.active`): which tab starts selected when nothing else — a matching `href`, an `active: true` entry — already determines it. |
| `onActiveItemChange` | `(item: BottomNavigationItem) => void` | — | Called with the full item whenever the active tab changes — a click, a URL match on mount/back-forward navigation, or an item's `active` field changing to point elsewhere. |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class names applied to the root bar. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; item?: string; activeItem?: string; icon?: string; label?: string; badge?: string; }` | — | Per-part class overrides (`root`, `item`, `activeItem`, `icon`, `label`, `badge`) — merged after the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect` — see §1.7.

### Bottom Navigation · 3. Events

| Component | React callback | Signature | Web Component event | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `BottomNavigation` | `onActiveItemChange` | `(item: BottomNavigationItem) => void` | `activeitemchange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with the full item whenever the active tab changes — a click, a URL match on mount/back-forward navigation, or an item's `active` field changing to point elsewhere. |
| `BottomNavigation` | `onUpdate` | — | `update` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | The value was committed (the native `change`); `detail` = the new value (`true` / `false` for a checkbox or switch, the value `string` / `number` otherwise). React: `onChange`. |

### Bottom Navigation · 4. Variants

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `BottomNavigation` | `variant` | `solid`, `outline`, `soft`, `text` | — | How the active tab is drawn: "solid", "outline" or "soft" fill, or "text" — no fill at all, only the icon and label are highlighted in `color`. Leave it out to follow the theme's active-item style (`ThemeProvider`'s `defaultActiveVariant`, default "solid"; the theme never picks "text"). |

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `BottomNavigation` | `color` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | `"accent"` | Color of the active tab (default: "accent", which follows the theme accent) — one of the built-in ColorNames, or any other CSS color value. Same as Navbar / Sidebar. |

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set.

### Bottom Navigation · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### Bottom Navigation · 7. Component States

_No dedicated state props — the visual states are default, hover, focus-visible and active._

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### Bottom Navigation · 8. Slots / Children / Content

_No children or slots — it is configured entirely through props._

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Bottom Navigation · 9. Framework Interfaces

```tsx
/>
```

### Bottom Navigation · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Basic

Click any tab — the bar keeps track of the active one itself, like Sidebar and Navbar. Rendered inside a mock phone frame; the component itself is a normal flow element you position however you like.

```tsx
<BottomNavigation
  items={[
    { icon: "home", label: "Home", active: true },
    { icon: "search", label: "Search" },
    { icon: "heart", label: "Saved" },
    { icon: "user", label: "Profile" },
  ]}
/>
```

#### Selection callback

onActiveItemChange fires with the whole item whenever the active tab changes — a click, or a URL / active-prop change.

```tsx
const [active, setActive] = useState("Home");

<BottomNavigation
  items={[/* ... */]}
  onActiveItemChange={(item) => setActive(item.label)}
/>
```

#### With links

Give a tab an href and it renders as a real link; the bar also matches the current URL on load and back/forward, so a routed app needs no extra wiring.

```tsx
<BottomNavigation
  items={[
    { icon: "home", label: "Home", href: "/" },
    { icon: "search", label: "Search", href: "/search" },
    { icon: "user", label: "Profile", href: "/profile" },
  ]}
/>
```

#### With a badge

A tab's `badge` renders as a small count pill on the icon's top-right corner.

```tsx
<BottomNavigation
  items={[
    { icon: "home", label: "Home", active: true },
    { icon: "search", label: "Search" },
    { icon: "bell", label: "Alerts", badge: "3" },
    { icon: "user", label: "Profile" },
  ]}
/>
```

#### Transitions

Enter transitions via `transition` (with `transitionDuration` / `transitionDelay`) and hover effects via `hoverEffect`. They play on mount — reload the page, or use Replay in the playground.

```tsx
const items = [
  { icon: "home", label: "Home", active: true },
  { icon: "search", label: "Search" },
  { icon: "heart", label: "Saved" },
  { icon: "user", label: "Profile" },
];

<BottomNavigation items={items} transition="fade" />
<BottomNavigation items={items} transition="slide-down" />
<BottomNavigation items={items} transition="slide-right" transitionDelay={100} />
<BottomNavigation items={items} transition="zoom" />
<BottomNavigation items={items} transition="blur" />
<BottomNavigation items={items} transition="drop" transitionDuration={700} />

<BottomNavigation items={items} hoverEffect="lift" />
<BottomNavigation items={items} hoverEffect="glow" />
<BottomNavigation items={items} hoverEffect="shine" />
```

### Bottom Navigation · 11. Component Composition

- Data-driven: pass `items` instead of child components (works the same as a Web Component).

### Bottom Navigation · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: none beyond native element semantics.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: `aria-current`, `aria-hidden`.
- Keyboard: native keyboard behavior of the underlying element (Tab to focus, Enter / Space to activate).
- Focus: uses the browser focus outline / the underlying control's ring.
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Bottom Navigation · 13. Responsive Behavior

- No breakpoint-specific rules — it is fluid and adapts to its container width.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Bottom Navigation · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `BottomNavigation`: `root`, `item`, `activeItem`, `icon`, `label`, `badge`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with `color`; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect`.

## Tabs

*Category: Navigation.* Exports: `Tabs`.

### Tabs · 1. Component Overview

A self-contained, data-driven tabbed panel.

- React: `import { Tabs } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-tabs>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Tabs · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `Tabs`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `tabs` **required** | `TabItem[]` | — | The tabs, in order — each has a `label`, its panel `content`, and optional `disabled`. |
| `defaultIndex` | `number` | `0` | 0-indexed tab selected on first render (default: 0); the component tracks the selection itself afterwards (uncontrolled). |
| `index` | `number` | — | The selected tab (0-indexed) — set it to select a tab from outside; the component also keeps its own selection, so a tab click works with nothing wired up. Pair with `onChange` for two-way binding. |
| `onChange` | `(index: number) => void` | — | Called with the new tab index when the user selects a tab (the web component's `change` / `update` event, detail = the index). |
| `color` | `ColorName` | `"accent"` | Accent color of the active tab (default: "accent" — follows the theme accent). |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class name(s) appended to the root element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; list?: string; tab?: string; activeTab?: string; panel?: string; }` | — | Per-part class overrides — merged after (and win over) the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay` — see §1.7.

### Tabs · 3. Events

| Component | React callback | Signature | Web Component event | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Tabs` | `onChange` | `(index: number) => void` | `change` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with the new tab index when the user selects a tab (the web component's `change` / `update` event, detail = the index). |
| `Tabs` | `onUpdate` | — | `update` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | The value was committed (the native `change`); `detail` = the new value (`true` / `false` for a checkbox or switch, the value `string` / `number` otherwise). React: `onChange`. |

### Tabs · 4. Variants

_This component has no variant prop — it has a single look (restyle it with colors / `classNames`)._

### Tabs · 5. Colors and Theming

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Tabs` | `color` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | `"accent"` | Accent color of the active tab (default: "accent" — follows the theme accent). |

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set.

### Tabs · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### Tabs · 7. Component States

_No dedicated state props — the visual states are default, hover, focus-visible and active._

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### Tabs · 8. Slots / Children / Content

_No children or slots — it is configured entirely through props._

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Tabs · 9. Framework Interfaces

**`Tabs`** → `<l-tabs>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `index` | `index` | number |
| `tabs` | `tabs` | json (JSON string or object property) |
| `defaultIndex` | `default-index` | number |
| `color` | `color` | string |
| `transition` | `transition` | string |
| `transitionDuration` | `transition-duration` | number |
| `transitionDelay` | `transition-delay` | number |

Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Basic*):

**React**
```tsx
<Tabs
  tabs={[
    { label: "Overview", content: <p>A quick summary of the project.</p> },
    { label: "Activity", content: <p>Recent activity shows up here.</p> },
    { label: "Settings", content: <p>Adjust your preferences.</p> },
  ]}
/>
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-Tabs id="basic-tabs"></l-Tabs>

<script type="module">
  import "lojee-ui/elements";

  document.getElementById("basic-tabs").tabs = [
    { label: "Overview", content: "A quick summary of the project." },
    { label: "Activity", content: "Recent activity shows up here." },
    { label: "Settings", content: "Adjust your preferences." },
  ];
</script>
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Tabs · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Basic

A plain list of tabs, each with its own content.

```tsx
<Tabs
  tabs={[
    { label: "Overview", content: <p>A quick summary of the project.</p> },
    { label: "Activity", content: <p>Recent activity shows up here.</p> },
    { label: "Settings", content: <p>Adjust your preferences.</p> },
  ]}
/>
```

#### Disabled tab

A tab can be disabled and skipped over.

```tsx
<Tabs
  tabs={[
    { label: "Plan", content: <p>Choose your plan.</p> },
    { label: "Billing", content: <p>Billing details.</p>, disabled: true },
    { label: "Review", content: <p>Review and confirm.</p> },
  ]}
/>
```

#### Color

The active tab's underline/text follows `color`.

```tsx
<Tabs
  color="indigo"
  defaultIndex={1}
  tabs={[
    { label: "Details", content: <p>Item details.</p> },
    { label: "Reviews", content: <p>What people are saying.</p> },
    { label: "Shipping", content: <p>Shipping and returns.</p> },
  ]}
/>
```

#### Transitions

Enter transitions via `transition` (with `transitionDuration` / `transitionDelay`). They play on mount — reload the page, or use Replay in the playground.

```tsx
const tabs = [
  { label: "Overview", content: <p>A quick summary of the project.</p> },
  { label: "Activity", content: <p>Recent activity shows up here.</p> },
  { label: "Settings", content: <p>Adjust your preferences.</p> },
];

<Tabs tabs={tabs} transition="fade" />
<Tabs tabs={tabs} transition="slide-up" />
<Tabs tabs={tabs} transition="slide-right" transitionDelay={100} />
<Tabs tabs={tabs} transition="zoom" />
<Tabs tabs={tabs} transition="blur" />
<Tabs tabs={tabs} transition="drop" transitionDuration={700} />
```

### Tabs · 11. Component Composition

- Data-driven: pass `tabs` instead of child components (works the same as a Web Component).

### Tabs · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: `tablist`, `tab`, `tabpanel`.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: `aria-hidden`, `aria-selected`.
- Keyboard: native keyboard behavior of the underlying element (Tab to focus, Enter / Space to activate).
- Focus: uses the browser focus outline / the underlying control's ring.
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Tabs · 13. Responsive Behavior

- No breakpoint-specific rules — it is fluid and adapts to its container width.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Tabs · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `Tabs`: `root`, `list`, `tab`, `activeTab`, `panel`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with `color`; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`.

## Stepper

*Category: Navigation.* Exports: `Stepper`, `StepperItem`.

### Stepper · 1. Component Overview

A multi-step progress indicator — numbered circles connected by a line.

- React: `import { Stepper } from "lojee-ui"` · `import { StepperItem } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-stepper>` · `<l-stepper-item>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Stepper · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `Stepper`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `steps` **required** | `StepperStep[]` | — | The steps to render, in order — each has a `label`, optional `description`, optional `content` section and optional `disabled`. |
| `currentStep` | `number` | — | 0-indexed — steps before this are complete, this one is current, after are upcoming; past the last index, every step shows as complete. Like Sidebar's `active`, it is a *default*, not a lock: the stepper manages the current step itself (clicks, Back / Next, `ref`), and follows this prop whenever it changes. |
| `defaultStep` | `number` | `0` | Initial step when `currentStep` isn't given (default: 0). |
| `orientation` | `StepperOrientation` | `"horizontal"` | Layout direction: "horizontal" (default) or "vertical". |
| `color` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | `"accent"` | Color of complete / current steps and the connector (default: "accent", which follows the theme's accent color) — one of the built-in ColorNames, or any other CSS color value. |
| `navigation` | `boolean` | `false` | Show the built-in Back / Next buttons (Finish on the last step) — they change the current step for you (default: false). Works with or without step `content`. |
| `clickable` | `boolean` | — | Let the step circles be clicked to jump to that step (default: on when any step has `content`). |
| `backLabel` | `string` | `"Back"` | Text of the built-in Back button when `navigation` is on (default: "Back"). |
| `nextLabel` | `string` | `"Next"` | Text of the built-in Next button when `navigation` is on (default: "Next"). |
| `finishLabel` | `string` | `"Finish"` | Text of the Next button on the last step when `navigation` is on (default: "Finish"). |
| `sections` | `boolean` | `false` | Reserve a section for every step even when no step has `content`. For Web Components the sections are `<l-stepper-item step="0">…</l-stepper-item>` children (any framework's components can go inside; they turn this on by themselves), or plain slots: `<div slot="step-0">…</div>` and `slot="completed"`. React users use `<StepperItem>` or `content`. |
| `completedContent` | `StepperContent` | — | Shown instead of a step's section once every step is complete (after Finish). An element or a component (rendered with a `StepperStepContext`, `index === steps.length`). |
| `children` | `ReactNode` | — | `<StepperItem step={n}>` sections (React): each shows while the stepper's current step is `n`. They and any component inside can call `useStepper()` to change the step. |
| `onStepChange` | `(index: number, step: StepperStep \| undefined) => void` | — | Called with the new index and step whenever the current step changes (a click, Back / Next, `ref`, or the `currentStep` prop). `index === steps.length` means every step is complete. |
| `ref` | `Ref<StepperHandle>` | — | Imperative handle — `ref.current.next()`, `.prev()`, `.goTo(2)`, `.reset()` (React 19 passes `ref` as a prop). |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class name(s) appended to the root element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; step?: string; circle?: string; completeCircle?: string; currentCircle?: string; label?: string; description?: string; connector?: string; panel?: string; navigation?: string; }` | — | Per-part class overrides — merged after (and win over) the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect` — see §1.7.

#### `StepperItem`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `step` **required** | `number \| "complete"` | — | The step this section belongs to (0-indexed) — it renders only while the stepper's current step equals it. `"complete"` shows it once every step is done (after Finish). |
| `children` | `ReactNode` | — | Content shown while this section's step is current. |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class name(s) applied to the wrapper element around `children`. |

#### Hooks

| Hook | Signature | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `useStepper` | `useStepper(): StepperStepContext` | The surrounding `<Stepper>`'s current step and controls — for a component rendered inside a step. |

### Stepper · 3. Events

| Component | React callback | Signature | Web Component event | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Stepper` | `onStepChange` | `(index: number, step: StepperStep \| undefined) => void` | `stepchange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with the new index and step whenever the current step changes (a click, Back / Next, `ref`, or the `currentStep` prop). `index === steps.length` means every step is complete. |
| `Stepper` | `onUpdate` | — | `update` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | The value was committed (the native `change`); `detail` = the new value (`true` / `false` for a checkbox or switch, the value `string` / `number` otherwise). React: `onChange`. |

### Stepper · 4. Variants

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Stepper` | `orientation` | `horizontal`, `vertical` | `"horizontal"` | Layout direction: "horizontal" (default) or "vertical". |

### Stepper · 5. Colors and Theming

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Stepper` | `color` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | `"accent"` | Color of complete / current steps and the connector (default: "accent", which follows the theme's accent color) — one of the built-in ColorNames, or any other CSS color value. |

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set.

### Stepper · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### Stepper · 7. Component States

_No dedicated state props — the visual states are default, hover, focus-visible and active._

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### Stepper · 8. Slots / Children / Content

| Component | React prop | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Stepper` | `children` | `ReactNode` | `<StepperItem step={n}>` sections (React): each shows while the stepper's current step is `n`. They and any component inside can call `useStepper()` to change the step. |
| `StepperItem` | `children` | `ReactNode` | Content shown while this section's step is current. |

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Stepper · 9. Framework Interfaces

**`Stepper`** → `<l-stepper>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `steps` | `steps` | json (JSON string or object property) |
| `currentStep` | `current-step` | number |
| `defaultStep` | `default-step` | number |
| `orientation` | `orientation` | string |
| `color` | `color` | string |
| `navigation` | `navigation` | boolean |
| `sections` | `sections` | boolean |
| `clickable` | `clickable` | boolean |
| `backLabel` | `back-label` | string |
| `nextLabel` | `next-label` | string |
| `finishLabel` | `finish-label` | string |
| `completedContent` | `completed-content` | string |
| `transition` | `transition` | string |
| `transitionDuration` | `transition-duration` | number |
| `transitionDelay` | `transition-delay` | number |
| `hoverEffect` | `hover-effect` | string |

**`StepperItem`** → `<l-stepper-item>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `step` | `step` | string |

Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Basic*):

**React**
```tsx
<Stepper
  currentStep={2}
  steps={[
    { label: "Cart" },
    { label: "Shipping" },
    { label: "Payment" },
    { label: "Confirm" },
  ]}
/>
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-Stepper id="stepper-basic" current-step="2"></l-Stepper>

<script type="module">
  import "lojee-ui/elements";

  document.getElementById("stepper-basic").steps = [
    { label: "Cart" },
    { label: "Shipping" },
    { label: "Payment" },
    { label: "Confirm" },
  ];
</script>
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Stepper · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Basic

currentStep sits in the middle, so complete, current, and upcoming steps are all visible.

```tsx
<Stepper
  currentStep={2}
  steps={[
    { label: "Cart" },
    { label: "Shipping" },
    { label: "Payment" },
    { label: "Confirm" },
  ]}
/>
```

#### Step sections

Give a step content and the stepper shows that step's section under it. Set navigation to add built-in Back / Next buttons (Finish on the last step) that change the step for you; the step circles are clickable too. Like Sidebar and Navbar, it manages the current step itself.

```tsx
<Stepper
  defaultStep={1}
  navigation
  completedContent={<p>All done — your order is placed.</p>}
  onStepChange={(index, step) => console.log(index, step?.label)}
  steps={[
    { label: "Cart", content: <CartPanel /> },
    { label: "Shipping", content: <ShippingPanel /> },
    { label: "Payment", content: <PaymentPanel /> },
    { label: "Confirm", content: <ConfirmPanel /> },
  ]}
/>

{/* navigation shows the built-in Back / Next buttons (Finish on the last step).
    Step circles are clickable by default when a step has content — clickable={false} turns that off. */}
```

#### Components as step content

A step's content can be a component, not just an element: pass the component itself and the stepper renders it with { next, prev, goTo, index, isFirst, isLast }, so a form can advance the stepper when it validates. Define the component outside render so its identity stays stable.

```tsx
import { Stepper, type StepperStepContext } from "lojee-ui";

// Any component works — it receives the stepper's controls as props.
function AccountStep({ next }: StepperStepContext) {
  const [email, setEmail] = useState("");
  return (
    <form onSubmit={(e) => { e.preventDefault(); next(); }}>
      <input value={email} onChange={(e) => setEmail(e.target.value)} />
      <button disabled={!email.includes("@")}>Continue</button>
    </form>
  );
}

function PlanStep({ next, prev }: StepperStepContext) { /* ... */ }
function ReviewStep({ goTo }: StepperStepContext) { /* ... */ }

<Stepper
  steps={[
    { label: "Account", content: AccountStep },   // pass the component itself…
    { label: "Plan", content: <PlanStep /> },       // …or an element (no controls, just renders)
    { label: "Review", content: ReviewStep },
  ]}
/>
```

#### StepperItem

Or write each step's section as a component in the JSX: <StepperItem step={n}> renders its children only while the stepper's current step is n (step="complete" for the finished state). Inside, useStepper() gives any component the current step and next / prev / goTo.

```tsx
import { Stepper, StepperItem, useStepper } from "lojee-ui";

function AccountForm() {
  const { next } = useStepper();          // the surrounding stepper's controls
  const [email, setEmail] = useState("");
  return (
    <form onSubmit={(e) => { e.preventDefault(); next(); }}>
      <input value={email} onChange={(e) => setEmail(e.target.value)} />
      <button disabled={!email.includes("@")}>Continue</button>
    </form>
  );
}

<Stepper steps={[{ label: "Account" }, { label: "Plan" }, { label: "Review" }]}>
  <StepperItem step={0}><AccountForm /></StepperItem>   {/* shown while the current step is 0 */}
  <StepperItem step={1}><PlanPicker /></StepperItem>
  <StepperItem step={2}><Review /></StepperItem>
  <StepperItem step="complete">All done.</StepperItem>  {/* shown after Finish */}
</Stepper>
```

#### Driving it from code

Change the step from your own code: a ref with next / prev / goTo / reset, or by setting currentStep — it follows the prop whenever it changes.

```tsx
const stepper = useRef<StepperHandle>(null);

<Stepper ref={stepper} steps={steps} />

<button onClick={() => stepper.current?.next()}>Next</button>
<button onClick={() => stepper.current?.prev()}>Prev</button>
<button onClick={() => stepper.current?.goTo(2)}>Go to step 3</button>
<button onClick={() => stepper.current?.reset()}>Reset</button>

{/* or just render with a new value — it follows currentStep when it changes */}
```
