```tsx
<Stepper steps={steps} currentStep={step} />
```

_4 more examples are on the docs page._

### Stepper · 11. Component Composition

- Built from `Stepper`, `StepperItem` — use them together as shown in the examples above.
- Data-driven: pass `steps` instead of child components (works the same as a Web Component).

### Stepper · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: `tabpanel`.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: `aria-current`, `aria-label`.
- Keyboard: native keyboard behavior of the underlying element (Tab to focus, Enter / Space to activate).
- Focus: visible focus ring (`focus-visible`).
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Stepper · 13. Responsive Behavior

- No breakpoint-specific rules — it is fluid and adapts to its container width.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Stepper · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `Stepper`: `root`, `step`, `circle`, `completeCircle`, `currentCircle`, `label`, `description`, `connector`, `panel`, `navigation`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with `color`; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect`.

## Breadcrumbs

*Category: Navigation.* Exports: `BreadcrumbItem`, `Breadcrumbs`.

### Breadcrumbs · 1. Component Overview

A navigation trail showing the current page's location in a hierarchy.

- React: `import { BreadcrumbItem } from "lojee-ui"` · `import { Breadcrumbs } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-breadcrumb-item>` · `<l-breadcrumbs>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Breadcrumbs · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `BreadcrumbItem`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `href` | `string` | — | If provided, renders as a link; if omitted, renders as the current/final non-link item. |
| `icon` | `string` | — | Icon name, e.g. "home" — see src/core/icons.ts for the available set. |
| `children` | `ReactNode` | — | The item's text/content. |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class names applied to the item's link or current-page element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; separator?: string; icon?: string; }` | — | Per-part class overrides (`root`, `separator`, `icon`) — merged after the built-in styling. |

#### `Breadcrumbs`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `children` | `ReactNode` | — | The `BreadcrumbItem` elements, in order; the last one without an `href` is the current page. |
| `color` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | `"accent"` | Color of the current (last) item and of link hovers (default: "accent", which follows the theme's accent color) — one of the built-in ColorNames, or any other CSS color value. Same as Navbar / Sidebar. |
| `variant` | `BreadcrumbsVariant` | `"text"` | How the current item is drawn (default: "text"): "text" highlights only its text, "solid" / "outline" / "soft" put it in a filled, outlined or tinted pill — the same looks as active items elsewhere. |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class names applied to the list element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; }` | — | Per-part class overrides (`root`) — merged after the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay` — see §1.7.

### Breadcrumbs · 3. Events

_No component-specific events._ Native DOM events (`click`, `input`, `focus`…) bubble from the rendered element as usual.

### Breadcrumbs · 4. Variants

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Breadcrumbs` | `variant` | `solid`, `outline`, `soft`, `text` | `"text"` | How the current item is drawn (default: "text"): "text" highlights only its text, "solid" / "outline" / "soft" put it in a filled, outlined or tinted pill — the same looks as active items elsewhere. |

### Breadcrumbs · 5. Colors and Theming

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Breadcrumbs` | `color` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | `"accent"` | Color of the current (last) item and of link hovers (default: "accent", which follows the theme's accent color) — one of the built-in ColorNames, or any other CSS color value. Same as Navbar / Sidebar. |

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set.

### Breadcrumbs · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### Breadcrumbs · 7. Component States

_No dedicated state props — the visual states are default, hover, focus-visible and active._

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### Breadcrumbs · 8. Slots / Children / Content

| Component | React prop | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `BreadcrumbItem` | `children` | `ReactNode` | The item's text/content. |
| `Breadcrumbs` | `children` | `ReactNode` | The `BreadcrumbItem` elements, in order; the last one without an `href` is the current page. |

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Breadcrumbs · 9. Framework Interfaces

**`BreadcrumbItem`** → `<l-breadcrumb-item>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `href` | `href` | string |
| `icon` | `icon` | string |
| `className` | `class-name` | string |
| `classNames` | `class-names` | json (JSON string or object property) |

**`Breadcrumbs`** → `<l-breadcrumbs>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `color` | `color` | string |
| `variant` | `variant` | string |
| `transition` | `transition` | string |
| `transitionDuration` | `transition-duration` | number |
| `transitionDelay` | `transition-delay` | number |

Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Basic*):

**React**
```tsx
<Breadcrumbs>
  <BreadcrumbItem href="/">Home</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem>Settings</BreadcrumbItem>
</Breadcrumbs>
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-Breadcrumbs>
  <l-BreadcrumbItem href="/">Home</l-BreadcrumbItem>
  <l-BreadcrumbItem>Settings</l-BreadcrumbItem>
</l-Breadcrumbs>

<script type="module">
  import "lojee-ui/elements";
</script>
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Breadcrumbs · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Basic

A basic two-level trail.

```tsx
<Breadcrumbs>
  <BreadcrumbItem href="/">Home</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem>Settings</BreadcrumbItem>
</Breadcrumbs>
```

#### Multi-level

A deeper, 4-level trail.

```tsx
<Breadcrumbs>
  <BreadcrumbItem href="/">Home</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem href="/projects">Projects</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem href="/projects/lojee-ui">lojee-ui</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem>Breadcrumbs</BreadcrumbItem>
</Breadcrumbs>
```

#### With icons

Each item can carry its own leading icon.

```tsx
<Breadcrumbs>
  <BreadcrumbItem href="/" icon="home">Home</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem href="/team" icon="users">Team</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem icon="circle-user">Profile</BreadcrumbItem>
</Breadcrumbs>
```

#### Custom styling

Set color on Breadcrumbs for the current item and link hovers; classNames.separator restyles the separators. Each BreadcrumbItem draws its own leading separator, hidden on the first item via CSS `:first-child`.

```tsx
<Breadcrumbs color="indigo">
  <BreadcrumbItem href="/" classNames={{ separator: "text-indigo-300" }}>Home</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem href="/docs" classNames={{ separator: "text-indigo-300" }}>Docs</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem>Getting started</BreadcrumbItem>
</Breadcrumbs>
```

#### Transitions

Enter transitions via `transition` (with `transitionDuration` / `transitionDelay`). They play on mount — reload the page, or use Replay in the playground.

```tsx
<Breadcrumbs transition="fade">
  <BreadcrumbItem href="/">Home</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem href="/docs">Docs</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem>Fade</BreadcrumbItem>
</Breadcrumbs>

<Breadcrumbs transition="slide-up">
  <BreadcrumbItem href="/">Home</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem href="/docs">Docs</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem>Slide up</BreadcrumbItem>
</Breadcrumbs>

<Breadcrumbs transition="slide-right" transitionDelay={100}>
  <BreadcrumbItem href="/">Home</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem href="/docs">Docs</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem>Slide right</BreadcrumbItem>
</Breadcrumbs>

<Breadcrumbs transition="zoom">
  <BreadcrumbItem href="/">Home</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem href="/docs">Docs</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem>Zoom</BreadcrumbItem>
</Breadcrumbs>

<Breadcrumbs transition="blur">
  <BreadcrumbItem href="/">Home</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem href="/docs">Docs</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem>Blur</BreadcrumbItem>
</Breadcrumbs>

<Breadcrumbs transition="drop" transitionDuration={700}>
  <BreadcrumbItem href="/">Home</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem href="/docs">Docs</BreadcrumbItem>
  <BreadcrumbItem>Drop</BreadcrumbItem>
</Breadcrumbs>
```

### Breadcrumbs · 11. Component Composition

- Built from `BreadcrumbItem`, `Breadcrumbs` — use them together as shown in the examples above.

### Breadcrumbs · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: none beyond native element semantics.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: `aria-current`, `aria-hidden`, `aria-label`.
- Keyboard: native keyboard behavior of the underlying element (Tab to focus, Enter / Space to activate).
- Focus: uses the browser focus outline / the underlying control's ring.
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Breadcrumbs · 13. Responsive Behavior

- No breakpoint-specific rules — it is fluid and adapts to its container width.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Breadcrumbs · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `BreadcrumbItem`: `root`, `separator`, `icon` · `Breadcrumbs`: `root`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with the theme accent; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: no per-component motion props.

## Pagination

*Category: Navigation.* Exports: `Pagination`.

### Pagination · 1. Component Overview

A controlled page list with prev/next arrows and ellipsis collapsing.

- React: `import { Pagination } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-pagination>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Pagination · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `Pagination`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `page` **required** | `number` | — | The current 1-based page number (controlled) — highlighted as active. |
| `totalPages` **required** | `number` | — | Total number of pages available. |
| `onPageChange` | `(page: number) => void` | — | Called with the new 1-based page number when a page button, or the previous/next button, is clicked. |
| `siblingCount` | `number` | `1` | How many page buttons to show on each side of the current page before collapsing into an ellipsis (default: 1). |
| `color` | `ColorName` | `"accent"` | Color of the active page indicator (default: "accent" — follows the theme accent). |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class(es) added to the root element, merged before `classNames.root`. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; item?: string; activeItem?: string }` | — | Per-part class overrides — merged after (and win over) the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay` — see §1.7.

### Pagination · 3. Events

| Component | React callback | Signature | Web Component event | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Pagination` | `onPageChange` | `(page: number) => void` | `pagechange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with the new 1-based page number when a page button, or the previous/next button, is clicked. |
| `Pagination` | `onUpdate` | — | `update` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | The value was committed (the native `change`); `detail` = the new value (`true` / `false` for a checkbox or switch, the value `string` / `number` otherwise). React: `onChange`. |

### Pagination · 4. Variants

_This component has no variant prop — it has a single look (restyle it with colors / `classNames`)._

### Pagination · 5. Colors and Theming

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Pagination` | `color` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | `"accent"` | Color of the active page indicator (default: "accent" — follows the theme accent). |

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set.

### Pagination · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### Pagination · 7. Component States

_No dedicated state props — the visual states are default, hover, focus-visible and active._

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### Pagination · 8. Slots / Children / Content

_No children or slots — it is configured entirely through props._

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Pagination · 9. Framework Interfaces

**`Pagination`** → `<l-pagination>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `page` | `page` | number |
| `totalPages` | `total-pages` | number |
| `siblingCount` | `sibling-count` | number |
| `color` | `color` | string |
| `transition` | `transition` | string |
| `transitionDuration` | `transition-duration` | number |
| `transitionDelay` | `transition-delay` | number |

Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Basic*):

**React**
```tsx
const [page, setPage] = useState(1);

<Pagination page={page} totalPages={5} onPageChange={setPage} />
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-Pagination id="basic-pagination" totalPages="5"></l-Pagination>

<script type="module">
  import "lojee-ui/elements";

  const pagination = document.getElementById("basic-pagination");
  pagination.page = 1;
  pagination.addEventListener("pagechange", (e) => {
    pagination.page = e.detail;
  });
</script>
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Pagination · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Basic

Few pages — no ellipsis needed.

```tsx
const [page, setPage] = useState(1);

<Pagination page={page} totalPages={5} onPageChange={setPage} />
```

#### Long range

Many pages — collapses into ellipses around the current page.

```tsx
const [page, setPage] = useState(6);

<Pagination page={page} totalPages={20} onPageChange={setPage} />
```

#### siblingCount

More neighboring pages shown before collapsing.

```tsx
<Pagination page={page} totalPages={20} siblingCount={2} onPageChange={setPage} />
```

#### Color

Matches the shared color palette.

```tsx
<Pagination page={page} totalPages={10} color="indigo" onPageChange={setPage} />
```

#### Transitions

Enter transitions via `transition` (with `transitionDuration` / `transitionDelay`). They play on mount — reload the page, or use Replay in the playground.

```tsx
const [page, setPage] = useState(1);

<Pagination page={page} totalPages={5} onPageChange={setPage} transition="fade" />
<Pagination page={page} totalPages={5} onPageChange={setPage} transition="slide-up" />
<Pagination page={page} totalPages={5} onPageChange={setPage} transition="slide-right" transitionDelay={100} />
<Pagination page={page} totalPages={5} onPageChange={setPage} transition="zoom" />
<Pagination page={page} totalPages={5} onPageChange={setPage} transition="blur" />
<Pagination page={page} totalPages={5} onPageChange={setPage} transition="drop" transitionDuration={700} />
```

### Pagination · 11. Component Composition

- Standalone: drop it into any layout (Card, Section, Grid, Container, App shell) or inside forms and overlays.

### Pagination · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: `presentation`.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: `aria-current`, `aria-hidden`, `aria-label`.
- Keyboard: native keyboard behavior of the underlying element (Tab to focus, Enter / Space to activate).
- Focus: uses the browser focus outline / the underlying control's ring.
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Pagination · 13. Responsive Behavior

- No breakpoint-specific rules — it is fluid and adapts to its container width.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Pagination · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `Pagination`: `root`, `item`, `activeItem`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with `color`; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`.

## Main

*Category: Navigation.* Exports: `Main`.

### Main · 1. Component Overview

The page area of an App: a rounded surface that follows the light/dark theme, floating on the App's tinted background. Whatever you put inside it is already on a themed background, so pages need no wrapper of their own.

- React: `import { Main } from "lojee-ui"`

### Main · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `Main`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `children` | `ReactNode` | — | — |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class names for the panel, e.g. `p-0` or `rounded-none` to override a default. |
| `padding` | `MainPadding` | `"md"` | Space between the panel's edge and its content, on all four sides: "none" \| "sm" (24px) \| "md" (32px) \| "lg" (48px) \| "xl" (80px) (default: "md"). |

### Main · 3. Events

_No component-specific events._ Native DOM events (`click`, `input`, `focus`…) bubble from the rendered element as usual.

### Main · 4. Variants

_This component has no variant prop — it has a single look (restyle it with colors / `classNames`)._

### Main · 5. Colors and Theming

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set. It has no color prop of its own.

### Main · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### Main · 7. Component States

_No dedicated state props — the visual states are default, hover, focus-visible and active._

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### Main · 8. Slots / Children / Content

| Component | React prop | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Main` | `children` | `ReactNode` | — |

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Main · 9. Framework Interfaces

- `Main`: React only (no standalone Web Component).
Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Basic*):

**React**
```tsx
<App>
  <Top>…</Top>
  <Side>…</Side>
  <Main>
    <Dashboard />
  </Main>
</App>
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-Theme-Provider default-mode="light">
  <l-App>
    <l-Top>…</l-Top>
    <l-Side>…</l-Side>
    <l-Main>
      <my-dashboard></my-dashboard>
    </l-Main>
  </l-App>
</l-Theme-Provider>

<script type="module">import "lojee-ui/elements";</script>
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Main · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Basic

The built-in look: rounded corners, a theme-aware background, and an inset from the App's edges. It fills the area given by the App's layout and scrolls when its content is taller.

```tsx
<App>
  <Top>…</Top>
  <Side>…</Side>
  <Main>
    <Dashboard />
  </Main>
</App>
```

#### Padding

```tsx
<Main padding="lg">…</Main>
```

#### Overriding the defaults

Every default is overridable with a class: drop the panel look for a page that wants the bare background, or change the radius.

```tsx
<Main className="rounded-none">…</Main>
<Main className="m-0 bg-transparent">…</Main>
```

### Main · 11. Component Composition

- Combined in its own examples with: `App`.

### Main · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: none beyond native element semantics.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: none.
- Keyboard: native keyboard behavior of the underlying element (Tab to focus, Enter / Space to activate).
- Focus: uses the browser focus outline / the underlying control's ring.
- Motion: inherits the library's reduced-motion handling for transitions.

### Main · 13. Responsive Behavior

- Breakpoint modifiers in its source: `lg`, `md`, `sm`, `xl`.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Main · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with the theme accent; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: no per-component motion props.

## ── App Layout ──

## App

*Category: App Layout.* Exports: `App`, `Section`.

### App · 1. Component Overview

A themeable app shell: compose Top, Side, Main and Foot (holding a Footer), and the CSS Grid is generated from a layout matrix. Open the Playground to rearrange sections by drag and drop.

- React: `import { App } from "lojee-ui"` · `import { Section } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-app>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### App · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `App`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `theme` | `AppTheme` | — | Visual theme. Omit it and the App follows the surrounding `ThemeProvider` (light without one); pass it to pin just this App to a theme — a scoped override that doesn't touch the page's own theme. |
| `accent` | `Accent` | — | Brand accent — a built-in color name or a custom hex color. Omit to follow the surrounding `ThemeProvider`. |
| `design` | `DesignName` | — | Design language of this App: "bento" (default) or "clay" (Claymorphism). Omit to follow the surrounding `ThemeProvider`. |
| `activeVariant` | `ActiveVariant` | — | How active items inside are drawn — "solid", "outline" or "soft". Omit to follow the `ThemeProvider`. |
| `layout` | `GridLayout` | `DEFAULT_LAYOUT` | Section placement as a matrix of section names; the CSS Grid is generated from it (default: top spanning the full width, side + main in the middle row, footer spanning the full width). |
| `collapseBelow` | `AppBreakpoint` | `"md"` | Container width below which the grid gives way to a single column with `<Side>` in an off-canvas drawer (default: "md" = 28rem; "3xl" = 48rem). Measured on the App itself, not the viewport. |
| `children` | `ReactNode` | — | `<Top>`, `<Side>`, `<Main>` and `<Foot>` — in any order. |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class names for the root element, e.g. `h-full` to size the App to its parent instead of the viewport. |

#### `Section`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `children` | `ReactNode` | — | — |
| `className` | `string` | — | — |

#### Hooks

| Hook | Signature | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `useAppLayout` | `useAppLayout()` | Controls for the collapsed sidebar drawer — e.g. call `setSideOpen(false)` after a nav link is chosen. |

### App · 3. Events

_No component-specific events._ Native DOM events (`click`, `input`, `focus`…) bubble from the rendered element as usual.

### App · 4. Variants

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `App` | `layout` | `GridLayout` | `DEFAULT_LAYOUT` | Section placement as a matrix of section names; the CSS Grid is generated from it (default: top spanning the full width, side + main in the middle row, footer spanning the full width). |

### App · 5. Colors and Theming

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `App` | `theme` | `light`, `dark` | — | Visual theme. Omit it and the App follows the surrounding `ThemeProvider` (light without one); pass it to pin just this App to a theme — a scoped override that doesn't touch the page's own theme. |
| `App` | `accent` | `Accent` | — | Brand accent — a built-in color name or a custom hex color. Omit to follow the surrounding `ThemeProvider`. |
| `App` | `design` | `bento`, `clay` | — | Design language of this App: "bento" (default) or "clay" (Claymorphism). Omit to follow the surrounding `ThemeProvider`. |
| `App` | `activeVariant` | `solid`, `outline`, `soft` | — | How active items inside are drawn — "solid", "outline" or "soft". Omit to follow the `ThemeProvider`. |

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set.

### App · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### App · 7. Component States

_No dedicated state props — the visual states are default, hover, focus-visible and active._

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### App · 8. Slots / Children / Content

| Component | React prop | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `App` | `children` | `ReactNode` | `<Top>`, `<Side>`, `<Main>` and `<Foot>` — in any order. |
| `Section` | `children` | `ReactNode` | — |

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### App · 9. Framework Interfaces

**`App`** → `<l-app>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `theme` | `theme` | string |
| `accent` | `accent` | string |
| `design` | `design` | string |
| `activeVariant` | `active-variant` | string |
| `layout` | `layout` | json (JSON string or object property) |
| `collapseBelow` | `collapse-below` | string |

- `Section`: React only (no standalone Web Component — used through its parent).

### App · 10. Complete Usage Examples

No packaged examples. Minimal use:

```tsx
import { App } from "lojee-ui";

<App />
```
### App · 11. Component Composition

- Built from `App`, `Section` — use them together as shown in the examples above.

### App · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: none beyond native element semantics.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: `aria-expanded`, `aria-hidden`, `aria-label`.
- Keyboard: has keyboard handling (e.g. Escape / arrow keys) — see the docs page for the exact keys.
- Focus: uses the browser focus outline / the underlying control's ring.
- Motion: inherits the library's reduced-motion handling for transitions.

### App · 13. Responsive Behavior

- Breakpoint modifiers in its source: `@2xl`, `@3xl`, `@lg`, `@md`, `@xl`, `lg`, `md`, `xl`.
- Measures its own container (container queries / ResizeObserver), so it adapts inside narrow panels, not only on small viewports.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### App · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with the theme accent; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: no per-component motion props.

## Theme Switcher

*Category: App Layout.* Exports: `ThemeSwitcher`.

### Theme Switcher · 1. Component Overview

The menu the lojee-ui navbar uses: light/dark, the accent color and the active-item style in one dropdown. It changes the app theme by itself — no ThemeProvider and no wiring needed. It writes the theme onto the page, remembers the choice, and restores it on the next visit.

- React: `import { ThemeSwitcher } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-theme-switcher>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Theme Switcher · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `ThemeSwitcher`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `mode` | `ThemeMode` | — | Current mode — omit to use the surrounding `ThemeProvider`. |
| `accent` | `Accent` | — | Current accent — a built-in name or a custom hex color. Omit to use the surrounding `ThemeProvider`. |
| `activeVariant` | `ActiveVariant` | — | Current active-item style — omit to use the surrounding `ThemeProvider`. |
| `design` | `DesignName` | — | Current design language — "bento" or "clay". Omit to use the surrounding `ThemeProvider`. |
| `onModeChange` | `(mode: ThemeMode) => void` | — | Called when light/dark is picked (the theme is still applied unless you control it with the matching prop). |
| `onAccentChange` | `(accent: Accent) => void` | — | Called when an accent is picked (the theme is still applied unless you control it with the matching prop). |
| `onActiveVariantChange` | `(variant: ActiveVariant) => void` | — | Called when an active-item style is picked (the theme is still applied unless you control it with the matching prop). |
| `onDesignChange` | `(design: DesignName) => void` | — | Called when a design is picked (the theme is still applied unless you control it with the matching prop). |
| `align` | `ThemeSwitcherAlign` | `"end"` | Where the dropdown (which always opens below the button) lines up with the button: "start" (left edges together), "center", or "end" (right edges together). "left" / "right" also work, as start / end. Default: "end". |
| `showDesign` | `boolean` | `true` | Show the "Design" section — Bento or Claymorphism (default: true). |
| `showActiveItems` | `boolean` | `true` | Show the "Active items" section (default: true). |
| `showAccent` | `boolean` | `true` | Show the "Accent" section — the built-in colors plus a "Custom" row that opens a color picker for any color (default: true). |
| `showCustom` | `boolean` | `true` | Show the "Custom" row in the Accent section — a color picker for any accent color (default: true). |
| `customAccentLabel` | `string` | `"Custom"` | Text of the custom-accent row (default: "Custom"). |
| `open` | `boolean` | — | Controlled open state of the menu — omit to let the button open and close it. Handy to keep the menu showing in docs or screenshots. |
| `onOpenChange` | `(open: boolean) => void` | — | Called when the menu asks to open or close (the button, a click outside, Escape). |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class name(s) appended to the root element. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect` — see §1.7.

### Theme Switcher · 3. Events

| Component | React callback | Signature | Web Component event | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ThemeSwitcher` | `onModeChange` | `(mode: ThemeMode) => void` | `modechange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called when light/dark is picked (the theme is still applied unless you control it with the matching prop). |
| `ThemeSwitcher` | `onAccentChange` | `(accent: Accent) => void` | `accentchange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called when an accent is picked (the theme is still applied unless you control it with the matching prop). |
| `ThemeSwitcher` | `onActiveVariantChange` | `(variant: ActiveVariant) => void` | `activevariantchange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called when an active-item style is picked (the theme is still applied unless you control it with the matching prop). |
| `ThemeSwitcher` | `onDesignChange` | `(design: DesignName) => void` | `designchange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called when a design is picked (the theme is still applied unless you control it with the matching prop). |
| `ThemeSwitcher` | `onOpenChange` | `(open: boolean) => void` | `openchange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called when the menu asks to open or close (the button, a click outside, Escape). |

### Theme Switcher · 4. Variants

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ThemeSwitcher` | `mode` | `light`, `dark` | — | Current mode — omit to use the surrounding `ThemeProvider`. |
| `ThemeSwitcher` | `align` | `start`, `center`, `end`, `left`, `right` | `"end"` | Where the dropdown (which always opens below the button) lines up with the button: "start" (left edges together), "center", or "end" (right edges together). "left" / "right" also work, as start / end. Default: "end". |

### Theme Switcher · 5. Colors and Theming

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ThemeSwitcher` | `accent` | `Accent` | — | Current accent — a built-in name or a custom hex color. Omit to use the surrounding `ThemeProvider`. |
| `ThemeSwitcher` | `activeVariant` | `solid`, `outline`, `soft` | — | Current active-item style — omit to use the surrounding `ThemeProvider`. |
| `ThemeSwitcher` | `design` | `bento`, `clay` | — | Current design language — "bento" or "clay". Omit to use the surrounding `ThemeProvider`. |

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set.

### Theme Switcher · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### Theme Switcher · 7. Component States

| Component | Prop | Type | Default | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ThemeSwitcher` | `open` | `boolean` | — | Controlled open state of the menu — omit to let the button open and close it. Handy to keep the menu showing in docs or screenshots. |

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### Theme Switcher · 8. Slots / Children / Content

_No children or slots — it is configured entirely through props._

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Theme Switcher · 9. Framework Interfaces

**`ThemeSwitcher`** → `<l-theme-switcher>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `open` | `open` | boolean |
| `mode` | `mode` | string |
| `accent` | `accent` | string |
| `activeVariant` | `active-variant` | string |
| `design` | `design` | string |
| `align` | `align` | string |
| `showDesign` | `show-design` | boolean |
| `showActiveItems` | `show-active-items` | boolean |
| `showAccent` | `show-accent` | boolean |
| `showCustom` | `show-custom` | boolean |
| `customAccentLabel` | `custom-accent-label` | string |
| `transition` | `transition` | string |
| `transitionDuration` | `transition-duration` | number |
| `transitionDelay` | `transition-delay` | number |
| `hoverEffect` | `hover-effect` | string |

Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Basic*):

**React**
```tsx
<ThemeSwitcher />
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-Theme-Switcher></l-Theme-Switcher>

<script type="module">import "lojee-ui/elements";</script>
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Theme Switcher · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Basic

Drop it anywhere — typically in a Navbar's actions. Inside a ThemeProvider it drives that provider; without one it sets the page theme directly.

```tsx
<ThemeSwitcher />
```

#### Trimmed menus

showAccent and showActiveItems trim the menu; align picks the edge it lines up with.

```tsx
<ThemeSwitcher showActiveItems={false} align="start" />
<ThemeSwitcher showActiveItems={false} showAccent={false} />
```

#### Dropdown alignment

```tsx
<ThemeSwitcher align="start" />
<ThemeSwitcher align="center" />
<ThemeSwitcher align="end" />
```

#### Controlled

Pass mode / accent / activeVariant and the on…Change callbacks to drive it from your own state instead of a ThemeProvider.

```tsx
const [mode, setMode] = useState<"light" | "dark">("light");

<ThemeSwitcher mode={mode} onModeChange={setMode} showAccent={false} showActiveItems={false} />
```

### Theme Switcher · 11. Component Composition

- Standalone: drop it into any layout (Card, Section, Grid, Container, App shell) or inside forms and overlays.

### Theme Switcher · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: `menu`, `menuitemradio`.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: `aria-checked`, `aria-expanded`, `aria-haspopup`, `aria-hidden`, `aria-label`.
- Keyboard: has keyboard handling (e.g. Escape / arrow keys) — see the docs page for the exact keys.
- Focus: visible focus ring (`focus-visible`).
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Theme Switcher · 13. Responsive Behavior

- Breakpoint modifiers in its source: `max-sm`, `sm`.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Theme Switcher · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with the theme accent; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect`.

## ── Data & Visualization ──

## Tables

*Category: Data & Visualization.* Exports: `Table`.

### Tables · 1. Component Overview

A data-driven table — pass columns and rows, no compound children.

- React: `import { Table } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-table>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Tables · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `Table`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `columns` **required** | `TableColumn<T>[]` | — | Column definitions — each has a `key` (the field read from each row) and a `header`, plus optional `type` (built-in cells: user, payment, badges, progress), `render(row)` (any component, React only), `align`, `sortable` and `width`. See TableColumn below for every option and the value each `type` expects. |
| `data` **required** | `T[]` | — | Rows to display, one object per row. |
| `variant` | `TableVariant` | `"default"` | Look: "default", "lined" (roomy rows with thin dividers and a plain header — good for people, payments and progress), or "card" (rounded, raised container) (default: "default"). |
| `size` | `TableSize` | `"md"` | Cell padding/text size: "sm", "md" or "lg" (default: "md"). |
| `selectable` | `boolean` | `false` | Adds a checkbox column at the start so several rows can be selected, with a select-all checkbox in the header (default: false). |
| `selected` | `TableRowKey[]` | — | The selected rows, as their keys (see `rowKey`). Also settable from outside; the table keeps its own selection otherwise. |
| `rowKey` | `string` | — | Field that uniquely identifies a row, used for `selected` (default: the row's position). |
| `onSelectionChange` | `(keys: TableRowKey[], rows: T[]) => void` | — | Called with the selected keys and rows whenever the selection changes. |
| `onSortChange` | `(sort: TableSort \| null) => void` | — | Called when a sortable column header is clicked, with the new sort (or null when sorting is cleared). |
| `responsive` | `TableResponsive` | `"stack"` | How the table adapts when it gets narrower than about 36rem (measured on the table itself, not the screen, so it also works inside a sidebar or modal): "stack" (default) turns every row into a card with each cell labelled by its column header, automatically; "scroll" keeps the columns and scrolls sideways instead. |
| `view` | `TableView` | — | Shows the rows as a "table" (default) or as a "grid" of cards — one card per row, built from the same columns. Also settable from outside; the table keeps its own view otherwise. |
| `viewToggle` | `boolean` | `false` | Adds a table / grid switch above the rows so people can change the view themselves (default: false). In the grid view the same bar offers a "Sort by" menu for sortable columns. |
| `onViewChange` | `(view: TableView) => void` | — | Called with "table" or "grid" when the view is switched. |
| `hoverable` | `boolean` | `true` | Highlights the row under the pointer (default: true). |
| `emptyMessage` | `string` | `"No results"` | Text shown when there are no rows (default: "No results"). |
| `striped` | `boolean` | `false` | Shades every other row for readability (default: false). |
| `bordered` | `boolean` | `false` | Draws an outer border and dividers between rows and columns (default: false). |
| `actions` | `TableAction[]` | — | Row actions — adds a right-aligned final column with one small icon button per action. Data-driven, so it works from the Web Component too (use `onAction` / the `action` event). |
| `actionsVariant` | `TableActionsVariant` | `"buttons"` | "buttons" \| "menu" — show each action as an icon button (default), or fold them into one three-dot button that opens a dropdown menu. |
| `actionsHeader` | `string` | `"Actions"` | Header text of the actions column (default: "Actions"). |
| `onAction` | `(action: TableAction, row: T) => void` | — | Called when a row action is clicked, with the action and its row. For built-ins it runs after the change: "delete" gets the removed row, "duplicate" gets the original row, and "edit" fires on Save with the updated row (Cancel and starting an edit don't fire it). |
| `builtInActions` | `boolean` | `true` | Handle `actions` whose value is "delete", "duplicate" or "edit" inside the table (default: true). Set false to only receive `onAction` and manage rows yourself. |
| `onDataChange` | `(rows: T[]) => void` | — | Called with the updated rows after a built-in delete, duplicate or edit-save. |
| `loading` | `boolean` | `false` | Shows shimmering skeleton rows in place of the data while true (default: false). The header stays visible. |
| `skeletonRows` | `number` | `5` | Number of skeleton rows shown while `loading` (default: 5). |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class name(s) appended to the root element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; header?: string; row?: string; cell?: string }` | — | Per-part class overrides — merged after (and win over) the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay` — see §1.7.

#### Data shapes (objects passed to props)

**`TableColumn`** — passed via `columns`. One entry per column. `key` picks the field to read from each row; `type` chooses how that value is drawn (or give `render` in React to draw it yourself).

| Field | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `key` **required** | `string` | Name of the field this column reads from each row, e.g. "email". It is also what sorting and inline editing use. |
| `header` **required** | `ReactNode` | Text (or any React node) shown in the header cell. |
| `render` | `(row: T) => ReactNode` | Draws the cell yourself: gets the whole row and returns any React node — an Avatar, a Badge, a Button… React only (a Web Component can't take a function; use `type` there). When set it wins over `type`, and the cell isn't editable inline. |
| `align` | `"left" \| "center" \| "right"` | Text alignment of the header and the cells: "left" (default), "center" or "right". |
| `type` | `TableCellType` | How the cell value is drawn, with no code: "text" (default), "user", "badges", "progress", "payment", "status", "rating", "image", "link", "avatars", "currency" or "date". Each type reads a specific shape of value from the row — see TableUserCell, TableBadgeCell, TableProgressCell, TablePaymentCell, TableStatusCell, TableRatingCell, TableImageCell, TableLinkCell, TableAvatarsCell, TableCurrencyCell and TableDateCell. Works in React and in the Web Component. |
| `sortable` | `boolean` | Shows sort arrows in the header and sorts by this column when clicked: ascending, then descending, then cleared (default: false). Numbers sort as numbers and text sorts naturally; a user sorts by name, a payment by its last digits and a badge list by its first label. |
| `width` | `string` | Column width, any CSS length, e.g. "28%" or "12rem" (default: sized by its content). |

```ts
const columns = [
  { key: "name", header: "Name", sortable: true },                       // plain text, click the header to sort
  { key: "user", header: "Full Name", type: "user" },                      // avatar + name + handle
  { key: "payment", header: "Payment", type: "payment", width: "22%" },    // card logo + masked number
  { key: "tags", header: "Category", type: "badges" },                     // coloured tags
  { key: "clicks", header: "Clicks", type: "progress", align: "right" },   // a bar with its percentage
];
```

**`TableUserCell`**. Value for a column with `type: "user"`: an avatar with a name and, underneath, a handle. The avatar falls back to the initials of the name.

| Field | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `name` **required** | `string` | The person's name, shown in bold. Initials are made from it when there is no `avatar`. |
| `handle` | `string` | A second line under the name, e.g. "@alicesmith" or an email address. |
| `avatar` | `string` | Image URL of the avatar. When omitted (or it fails to load) the initials are shown instead. |

```ts
{ key: "user", header: "Full Name", type: "user" }

// in the row:
user: { name: "Alice Smith", handle: "@alicesmith", avatar: "/avatars/alice.jpg" }
```

**`TablePaymentCell`**. Value for a column with `type: "payment"`: the card brand's logo and the masked number. Visa and Mastercard keep their official colours; everything else follows the theme.

| Field | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `brand` **required** | `string` | "visa" or "mastercard" draw the brand logo; any other text shows its first letters in a small chip. |
| `last4` **required** | `string \| number` | The last digits of the card number. Only the last two are shown, as "Ends in ****-**18". |
| `note` | `string` | Optional extra detail, such as "Primary card". When set an info icon appears whose tooltip shows it. |

```ts
{ key: "payment", header: "Payment Methods", type: "payment" }

// in the row:
payment: { brand: "mastercard", last4: "1499", note: "Primary card" }   // → [logo] Ends in ****-**99  ⓘ
```

**`TableBadgeCell`**. One tag in a column with `type: "badges"`. The cell value is an array of these, or of plain strings (which are coloured automatically).

| Field | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `label` **required** | `string` | The tag text. |
| `color` | `ColorName` | Tag colour: one of the built-in colour names ("indigo", "amber", "rose", "emerald", "violet", "cyan", …). When omitted the table cycles through a set so neighbouring tags differ. |

```ts
{ key: "tags", header: "Category", type: "badges" }

// in the row — plain strings:
tags: ["Arts", "Business", "Travel"]

// or with your own colours:
tags: [{ label: "Books", color: "indigo" }, { label: "Computers", color: "violet" }]
```

**`TableProgressCell`**. Value for a column with `type: "progress"`: a bar filled to a percentage, in the theme accent. The cell value can be this object or just the number.

| Field | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `value` **required** | `number` | How far along, from 0 to 100. Values outside the range are clamped. The bar's fill follows the theme accent and the percentage is written beside it. |

```ts
{ key: "clicks", header: "Clickthrough Percentage", type: "progress", sortable: true }

// in the row — either form works:
clicks: 64
clicks: { value: 64 }
```

**`TableStatusCell`**. Value for a column with `type: "status"`: a coloured pill. Give just the text and the colour is chosen from the words (paid, active, done → green; pending, invited, draft → amber; failed, overdue, suspended → red), or set `color` yourself.

| Field | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `label` **required** | `string` | The status text, e.g. "Paid" or "Pending". |
| `color` | `ColorName` | Pill colour (a built-in colour name). When omitted it is chosen from the text: words like active, paid, done or online are green; pending, invited or draft amber; failed, suspended, overdue or offline red; anything else follows the theme accent. |

```ts
{ key: "status", header: "Status", type: "status" }

// in the row:
status: "Paid"
status: { label: "Needs review", color: "violet" }
```

**`TableRatingCell`**. Value for a column with `type: "rating"`: read-only stars. The cell value can be this object or just the number; half values draw half a star.

| Field | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `value` **required** | `number` | The rating, from 0 up to `max`. Half values (4.5) draw half a star. |
| `max` | `number` | How many stars there are (default: 5). |

```ts
{ key: "rating", header: "Rating", type: "rating", sortable: true }

// in the row:
rating: 4.5
rating: { value: 7, max: 10 }
```

**`TableImageCell`**. Value for a column with `type: "image"`: a rounded thumbnail with a title and a subtitle underneath — good for products, files and articles.

| Field | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `src` | `string` | Image URL of the thumbnail. If it can't be loaded a neutral placeholder is shown. |
| `title` **required** | `string` | The main line, in bold. Also used as the image's alt text. |
| `subtitle` | `string` | A second, smaller line, e.g. a SKU or a category. |

```ts
{ key: "product", header: "Product", type: "image" }

// in the row:
product: { src: "/products/lamp.jpg", title: "Desk lamp", subtitle: "SKU 20418" }
```

**`TableLinkCell`**. Value for a column with `type: "link"`: a text link in the theme accent. The cell value can be this object or just the URL.

| Field | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `href` **required** | `string` | Where it goes. |
| `label` | `string` | The link text (default: the URL). |
| `external` | `boolean` | Opens in a new tab (default: false). |

```ts
{ key: "invoice", header: "Invoice", type: "link" }

// in the row:
invoice: "https://example.com/invoices/2041"
invoice: { href: "https://example.com/invoices/2041", label: "INV-2041", external: true }
```

**`TableAvatarsCell`**. One person in a column with `type: "avatars"`. The cell value is an array of these (or of plain names), drawn as overlapping avatars; after the first five a "+N" chip counts the rest. Sorting uses the number of people.

| Field | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `name` **required** | `string` | The person's name: initials are made from it, and it is the avatar's tooltip. |
| `avatar` | `string` | Image URL. When omitted the initials are shown. |

```ts
{ key: "team", header: "Team", type: "avatars" }

// in the row:
team: [{ name: "Ava Chen" }, { name: "Marcus Lee", avatar: "/avatars/marcus.jpg" }, "Priya Nair"]
```

**`TableCurrencyCell`**. Value for a column with `type: "currency"`: a formatted amount with aligned digits. The cell value can be this object or just a number, which is shown in US dollars.

| Field | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `value` **required** | `number` | The amount. |
| `currency` | `string` | ISO 4217 code such as "USD", "EUR" or "PHP" (default: "USD"). |

```ts
{ key: "total", header: "Total", type: "currency", align: "right", sortable: true }

// in the row:
total: 1249.5                                  // → $1,249.50
total: { value: 1249.5, currency: "EUR" }      // → €1,249.50
```

**`TableAction`** — passed via `actions`. One icon button in the row-actions column. Actions whose `value` is "edit", "duplicate" or "delete" work out of the box.

| Field | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `label` **required** | `string` | Visible name — used as the button's tooltip and accessible label. |
| `icon` | `string` | Icon name, e.g. "pencil" — see src/core/icons.ts for the available set. |
| `value` | `string` | Optional identifier passed back with the action (defaults to the label when you need one). |
| `color` | `"default" \| "danger"` | "default" or "danger" (destructive color) (default: "default"). |

**`TableSort`**. Reported by `onSortChange` / the `sortchange` event; null when sorting is cleared.

| Field | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `key` **required** | `string` | — |
| `direction` **required** | `"asc" \| "desc"` | — |

### Tables · 3. Events

| Component | React callback | Signature | Web Component event | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Table` | `onSelectionChange` | `(keys: TableRowKey[], rows: T[]) => void` | `selectionchange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with the selected keys and rows whenever the selection changes. |
| `Table` | `onSortChange` | `(sort: TableSort \| null) => void` | `sortchange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called when a sortable column header is clicked, with the new sort (or null when sorting is cleared). |
| `Table` | `onViewChange` | `(view: TableView) => void` | `viewchange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with "table" or "grid" when the view is switched. |
| `Table` | `onAction` | `(action: TableAction, row: T) => void` | `action` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called when a row action is clicked, with the action and its row. For built-ins it runs after the change: "delete" gets the removed row, "duplicate" gets the original row, and "edit" fires on Save with the updated row (Cancel and starting an edit don't fire it). |
| `Table` | `onDataChange` | `(rows: T[]) => void` | `datachange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with the updated rows after a built-in delete, duplicate or edit-save. |
| `Table` | `onUpdate` | — | `update` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | The value was committed (the native `change`); `detail` = the new value (`true` / `false` for a checkbox or switch, the value `string` / `number` otherwise). React: `onChange`. |

### Tables · 4. Variants

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Table` | `variant` | `default`, `lined`, `card` | `"default"` | Look: "default", "lined" (roomy rows with thin dividers and a plain header — good for people, payments and progress), or "card" (rounded, raised container) (default: "default"). |

### Tables · 5. Colors and Theming

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set. It has no color prop of its own.

### Tables · 6. Sizes

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Table` | `size` | `sm`, `md`, `lg` | `"md"` | Cell padding/text size: "sm", "md" or "lg" (default: "md"). |

### Tables · 7. Component States

| Component | Prop | Type | Default | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Table` | `selected` | `TableRowKey[]` | — | The selected rows, as their keys (see `rowKey`). Also settable from outside; the table keeps its own selection otherwise. |
| `Table` | `striped` | `boolean` | `false` | Shades every other row for readability (default: false). |
| `Table` | `bordered` | `boolean` | `false` | Draws an outer border and dividers between rows and columns (default: false). |
| `Table` | `loading` | `boolean` | `false` | Shows shimmering skeleton rows in place of the data while true (default: false). The header stays visible. |

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed · loading (spinner, blocks clicks).

### Tables · 8. Slots / Children / Content

_No children or slots — it is configured entirely through props._

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Tables · 9. Framework Interfaces

**`Table`** → `<l-table>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `columns` | `columns` | json (JSON string or object property) |
| `data` | `data` | json (JSON string or object property) |
| `variant` | `variant` | string |
| `responsive` | `responsive` | string |
| `view` | `view` | string |
| `viewToggle` | `view-toggle` | boolean |
| `size` | `size` | string |
| `selectable` | `selectable` | boolean |
| `selected` | `selected` | json (JSON string or object property) |
| `rowKey` | `row-key` | string |
| `hoverable` | `hoverable` | boolean |
| `emptyMessage` | `empty-message` | string |
| `striped` | `striped` | boolean |
| `bordered` | `bordered` | boolean |
| `loading` | `loading` | boolean |
| `skeletonRows` | `skeleton-rows` | number |
| `actions` | `actions` | json (JSON string or object property) |
| `actionsHeader` | `actions-header` | string |
| `actionsVariant` | `actions-variant` | string |
| `builtInActions` | `built-in-actions` | boolean |
| `transition` | `transition` | string |
| `transitionDuration` | `transition-duration` | number |
| `transitionDelay` | `transition-delay` | number |

Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Basic*):

**React**
```tsx
const columns = [
  { key: "name", header: "Name" },
  { key: "email", header: "Email" },
  { key: "role", header: "Role", align: "right" },
];

<Table variant="lined" columns={columns} data={people} />
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-Table variant="lined" id="basic-table"></l-Table>

<script type="module">
  import "lojee-ui/elements";

  const table = document.getElementById("basic-table");
  table.columns = [
    { key: "name", header: "Name" },
    { key: "email", header: "Email" },
    { key: "role", header: "Role", align: "right" },
  ];
  table.data = [
    { name: "Ava Chen", email: "ava@acme.com", role: "Admin" },
    { name: "Marcus Lee", email: "marcus@acme.com", role: "Editor" },
    { name: "Priya Nair", email: "priya@acme.com", role: "Viewer" },
    { name: "Dan Ostrow", email: "dan@acme.com", role: "Editor" },
  ];
</script>
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Tables · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Basic

Columns + data, rendered as a plain table.

```tsx
const columns = [
  { key: "name", header: "Name" },
  { key: "email", header: "Email" },
  { key: "role", header: "Role", align: "right" },
];

<Table variant="lined" columns={columns} data={people} />
```

#### Lined variant with rich cells

```tsx
const columns = ${COLUMNS_CODE};

const data = ${DATA_CODE};

<Table variant="lined" columns={columns} data={data} />
```

#### Selectable rows

```tsx
const columns = ${COLUMNS_CODE};

const data = ${DATA_CODE};

const [keys, setKeys] = useState<(string | number)[]>(["alice"]);

<Table
  variant="lined"
  selectable
  rowKey="id"
  selected={keys}
  onSelectionChange={(selectedKeys, rows) => setKeys(selectedKeys)}
  columns={columns}
  data={data}
/>
```

#### Grid view

```tsx
<Table variant="lined" viewToggle columns={columns} data={data} onViewChange={(view) => console.log(view)} />

{/* Start as a grid and control it yourself */}
<Table variant="lined" view="grid" columns={columns} data={data} />
```

#### More cell types

```tsx
const columns = ${ORDER_COLUMNS_CODE};

const data = ${jsLiteral(ORDERS_FOR_CODE)};

<Table variant="lined" columns={columns} data={data} />
```

_10 more examples are on the docs page._

### Tables · 11. Component Composition

- Combined in its own examples with: `Badge`.
- Data-driven: pass `columns`, `data` instead of child components (works the same as a Web Component).

### Tables · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: `menu`, `menuitem`, `progressbar`, `group`.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: `aria-busy`, `aria-expanded`, `aria-haspopup`, `aria-hidden`, `aria-label`, `aria-pressed`, `aria-selected`, `aria-sort`, `aria-valuemax`, `aria-valuemin`, `aria-valuenow`.
- Keyboard: has keyboard handling (e.g. Escape / arrow keys) — see the docs page for the exact keys.
- Focus: visible focus ring (`focus-visible`).
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Tables · 13. Responsive Behavior

- Breakpoint modifiers in its source: `lg`, `max-xl`, `md`, `sm`.
- Measures its own container (container queries / ResizeObserver), so it adapts inside narrow panels, not only on small viewports.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Tables · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `Table`: `root`, `header`, `row`, `cell`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with the theme accent; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`.

## Grid View

*Category: Data & Visualization.* Exports: `GridView`.

### Grid View · 1. Component Overview

A browsable collection of cards with search, sorting and a grid / list switch. Each card has a title, a status tag, big numbers and labelled details. Data-driven, so it works in React and as a Web Component.

- React: `import { GridView } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-grid-view>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Grid View · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `GridView`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `items` **required** | `GridViewItem[]` | — | The cards. |
| `view` | `GridViewMode` | — | How the items are laid out: "grid" (cards, default) or "list" (rows). Also follows changes from outside. |
| `variant` | `GridViewVariant` | `"default"` | "default" \| "draggable" — "draggable" adds a grip to each card so the order can be changed by drag and drop (default: "default"). Reordering pauses while a search or sort is active. |
| `viewToggle` | `boolean` | `true` | Shows the grid / list switch in the toolbar (default: true). |
| `searchable` | `boolean` | `true` | Shows the search box (default: true). Matches the title, subtitle, tag and every value. |
| `searchPlaceholder` | `string` | `"Search"` | Placeholder of the search box (default: "Search"). |
| `sortOptions` | `GridViewSortOption[]` | — | Adds a "Sort by" menu with these options. |
| `actions` | `GridViewAction[]` | — | Per-card menu (the three dots). Choosing an entry calls `onAction`. |
| `createLabel` | `string` | — | Text of the primary button at the end of the toolbar. Leave empty to hide the button. |
| `minItemWidth` | `number` | `270` | Smallest width of a card in grid view, in px (default: 270). More columns appear as the space allows. |
| `loading` | `boolean` | `false` | Shows shimmering placeholder cards while true (default: false). |
| `skeletonCount` | `number` | `6` | Number of placeholder cards while `loading` (default: 6). |
| `emptyMessage` | `string` | `"Nothing to show"` | Text shown when nothing matches (default: "Nothing to show"). |
| `onViewChange` | `(view: GridViewMode) => void` | — | Called with "grid" or "list" when the view is switched. |
| `onReorder` | `(items: GridViewItem[]) => void` | — | Called with the items in their new order after a drag and drop (or an arrow-key move) in the "draggable" variant. |
| `onItemClick` | `(item: GridViewItem) => void` | — | Called with the card when it is clicked. |
| `onAction` | `(detail: { action: GridViewAction; item: GridViewItem }) => void` | — | Called with `{ action, item }` when a card-menu entry is chosen. |
| `onCreate` | `() => void` | — | Called when the primary button is clicked. |
| `onSearchChange` | `(query: string) => void` | — | Called with the text whenever the search box changes. |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class name(s) applied to the root element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; toolbar?: string; card?: string; title?: string }` | — | Per-part class overrides — merged after (and win over) the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay` — see §1.7.

### Grid View · 3. Events

| Component | React callback | Signature | Web Component event | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `GridView` | `onViewChange` | `(view: GridViewMode) => void` | `viewchange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with "grid" or "list" when the view is switched. |
| `GridView` | `onReorder` | `(items: GridViewItem[]) => void` | `reorder` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with the items in their new order after a drag and drop (or an arrow-key move) in the "draggable" variant. |
| `GridView` | `onItemClick` | `(item: GridViewItem) => void` | `itemclick` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with the card when it is clicked. |
| `GridView` | `onAction` | `(detail: { action: GridViewAction; item: GridViewItem }) => void` | `action` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with `{ action, item }` when a card-menu entry is chosen. |
| `GridView` | `onCreate` | `() => void` | `create` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called when the primary button is clicked. |
| `GridView` | `onSearchChange` | `(query: string) => void` | `searchchange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with the text whenever the search box changes. |

### Grid View · 4. Variants

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `GridView` | `variant` | `default`, `draggable` | `"default"` | "default" \| "draggable" — "draggable" adds a grip to each card so the order can be changed by drag and drop (default: "default"). Reordering pauses while a search or sort is active. |

### Grid View · 5. Colors and Theming

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set. It has no color prop of its own.

### Grid View · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### Grid View · 7. Component States

| Component | Prop | Type | Default | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `GridView` | `searchable` | `boolean` | `true` | Shows the search box (default: true). Matches the title, subtitle, tag and every value. |
| `GridView` | `loading` | `boolean` | `false` | Shows shimmering placeholder cards while true (default: false). |

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed · loading (spinner, blocks clicks).

### Grid View · 8. Slots / Children / Content

_No children or slots — it is configured entirely through props._

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Grid View · 9. Framework Interfaces

**`GridView`** → `<l-grid-view>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `items` | `items` | json (JSON string or object property) |
| `view` | `view` | string |
| `variant` | `variant` | string |
| `viewToggle` | `view-toggle` | boolean |
| `searchable` | `searchable` | boolean |
| `searchPlaceholder` | `search-placeholder` | string |
| `sortOptions` | `sort-options` | json (JSON string or object property) |
| `actions` | `actions` | json (JSON string or object property) |
| `createLabel` | `create-label` | string |
| `minItemWidth` | `min-item-width` | number |
| `loading` | `loading` | boolean |
| `skeletonCount` | `skeleton-count` | number |
| `emptyMessage` | `empty-message` | string |
| `transition` | `transition` | string |
| `transitionDuration` | `transition-duration` | number |
| `transitionDelay` | `transition-delay` | number |

Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Basic*):

**React**
```tsx
const items = ${ITEMS_CODE};\n\nconst sortOptions = ${SORT_CODE};\n\n<GridView items={items} sortOptions={sortOptions} searchPlaceholder="Search documents" />
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-GridView id="docs" search-placeholder="Search documents"></l-GridView>\n\n<script type="module">\n  import "lojee-ui/elements";\n\n  const el = document.getElementById("docs");\n  el.items = ${ITEMS_CODE.replace(/\n/g, "\n  ")};\n  el.sortOptions = ${SORT_CODE.replace(/\n/g, "\n  ")};\n</script>
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Grid View · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Basic

Search matches every value on the card. The switch in the toolbar flips between cards and rows, and the cards fill as many columns as fit.

```tsx
const items = ${ITEMS_CODE};\n\nconst sortOptions = ${SORT_CODE};\n\n<GridView items={items} sortOptions={sortOptions} searchPlaceholder="Search documents" />
```

#### List view

```tsx
<GridView items={items} view="list" searchable={false} onViewChange={(view) => console.log(view)} />
```

#### Drag and drop

```tsx
<GridView\n  items={items}\n  variant="draggable"\n  onReorder={(items) => setItems(items)}\n/>
```

#### Menu and create button

```tsx
const actions = ${ACTIONS_CODE};\n\n<GridView\n  items={items}\n  actions={actions}\n  createLabel="Create document"\n  onAction={({ action, item }) => console.log(action.value, item)}\n  onCreate={() => console.log("create")}\n/>
```

#### Loading

Placeholder cards while data loads.

```tsx
<GridView items={items} loading={isLoading} skeletonCount={3} />
```

_1 more examples are on the docs page._

### Grid View · 11. Component Composition

- Data-driven: pass `items` instead of child components (works the same as a Web Component).

### Grid View · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: `menu`, `menuitem`, `menuitemradio`, `group`.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: `aria-busy`, `aria-checked`, `aria-expanded`, `aria-haspopup`, `aria-hidden`, `aria-label`, `aria-pressed`.
- Keyboard: has keyboard handling (e.g. Escape / arrow keys) — see the docs page for the exact keys.
- Focus: visible focus ring (`focus-visible`).
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Grid View · 13. Responsive Behavior

- Breakpoint modifiers in its source: `max-sm`, `sm`.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Grid View · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `GridView`: `root`, `toolbar`, `card`, `title`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with the theme accent; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`.

## Timeline

*Category: Data & Visualization.* Exports: `Timeline`.

### Timeline · 1. Component Overview

A vertical (or horizontal) sequence of events — a connecting line with a marker per item.

- React: `import { Timeline } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-timeline>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Timeline · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `Timeline`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `items` **required** | `TimelineItem[]` | — | The events to display, in order — each has a `title`, and optional `description`, `timestamp`, `icon` and `color`. |
| `orientation` | `TimelineOrientation` | `"vertical"` | "vertical" (default) stacks items top-to-bottom; "horizontal" is a compact left-to-right variant — dot + label only, no description. |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class name(s) appended to the root element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; item?: string; marker?: string; line?: string; content?: string; }` | — | Per-part class overrides — merged after (and win over) the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay` — see §1.7.

### Timeline · 3. Events

_No component-specific events._ Native DOM events (`click`, `input`, `focus`…) bubble from the rendered element as usual.

### Timeline · 4. Variants

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Timeline` | `orientation` | `vertical`, `horizontal` | `"vertical"` | "vertical" (default) stacks items top-to-bottom; "horizontal" is a compact left-to-right variant — dot + label only, no description. |

### Timeline · 5. Colors and Theming

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set. It has no color prop of its own.

### Timeline · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### Timeline · 7. Component States

_No dedicated state props — the visual states are default, hover, focus-visible and active._

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### Timeline · 8. Slots / Children / Content

_No children or slots — it is configured entirely through props._

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Timeline · 9. Framework Interfaces

**`Timeline`** → `<l-timeline>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `items` | `items` | json (JSON string or object property) |
| `orientation` | `orientation` | string |
| `transition` | `transition` | string |
| `transitionDuration` | `transition-duration` | number |
| `transitionDelay` | `transition-delay` | number |

Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Basic*):

**React**
```tsx
<Timeline
  items={[
    { title: "Order placed", description: "We've received your order.", timestamp: "Jan 4, 9:02 AM" },
    { title: "Payment confirmed", timestamp: "Jan 4, 9:05 AM" },
    { title: "Shipped", description: "Package handed to carrier.", timestamp: "Jan 5, 2:30 PM" },
    { title: "Delivered", timestamp: "Jan 6, 1:47 PM" },
  ]}
/>
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-Timeline id="timeline-basic"></l-Timeline>

<script type="module">
  import "lojee-ui/elements";

  document.getElementById("timeline-basic").items = [
    { title: "Order placed", description: "We've received your order.", timestamp: "Jan 4, 9:02 AM" },
    { title: "Payment confirmed", timestamp: "Jan 4, 9:05 AM" },
    { title: "Shipped", description: "Package handed to carrier.", timestamp: "Jan 5, 2:30 PM" },
    { title: "Delivered", timestamp: "Jan 6, 1:47 PM" },
  ];
</script>
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Timeline · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Basic

A plain order-status history — title, description, and timestamp per item.

```tsx
<Timeline
  items={[
    { title: "Order placed", description: "We've received your order.", timestamp: "Jan 4, 9:02 AM" },
    { title: "Payment confirmed", timestamp: "Jan 4, 9:05 AM" },
    { title: "Shipped", description: "Package handed to carrier.", timestamp: "Jan 5, 2:30 PM" },
    { title: "Delivered", timestamp: "Jan 6, 1:47 PM" },
  ]}
/>
```

#### With icons and colors

Give each item an icon and a color — a common way to show completed vs. pending steps.

```tsx
<Timeline
  items={[
    { title: "Order placed", timestamp: "Jan 4, 9:02 AM", icon: "check", color: "emerald" },
    { title: "Payment confirmed", timestamp: "Jan 4, 9:05 AM", icon: "check", color: "emerald" },
    { title: "Shipped", description: "Package handed to carrier.", timestamp: "Jan 5, 2:30 PM", icon: "check", color: "emerald" },
    { title: "Out for delivery", description: "Arriving today.", icon: "clock", color: "amber" },
    { title: "Delivered", icon: "circle-dot", color: "slate" },
  ]}
/>
```

#### Horizontal

```tsx
<Timeline
  orientation="horizontal"
  items={[
    { title: "Order placed", timestamp: "Jan 4", icon: "check", color: "emerald" },
    { title: "Shipped", timestamp: "Jan 5", icon: "check", color: "emerald" },
    { title: "Out for delivery", timestamp: "Jan 6", icon: "clock", color: "amber" },
    { title: "Delivered", icon: "circle-dot", color: "slate" },
  ]}
/>
```

#### Transitions

Enter transitions via `transition` (with `transitionDuration` / `transitionDelay`). Items enter one after another, 60ms apart. They play on mount — reload the page, or use Replay in the playground.

```tsx
const items = [
  { title: "Order placed", timestamp: "Jan 4, 9:02 AM" },
  { title: "Shipped", description: "Package handed to carrier.", timestamp: "Jan 5, 2:30 PM" },
  { title: "Delivered", timestamp: "Jan 6, 1:47 PM" },
];

<Timeline items={items} transition="fade" />
<Timeline items={items} transition="slide-up" />
<Timeline items={items} transition="slide-right" transitionDelay={100} />
<Timeline items={items} transition="drop" transitionDuration={700} />
```

### Timeline · 11. Component Composition

- Data-driven: pass `items` instead of child components (works the same as a Web Component).

### Timeline · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: none beyond native element semantics.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: none.
- Keyboard: native keyboard behavior of the underlying element (Tab to focus, Enter / Space to activate).
- Focus: uses the browser focus outline / the underlying control's ring.
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Timeline · 13. Responsive Behavior

- No breakpoint-specific rules — it is fluid and adapts to its container width.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Timeline · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `Timeline`: `root`, `item`, `marker`, `line`, `content`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with the theme accent; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`.

## Stats / KPI

*Category: Data & Visualization.* Exports: `Stat`.

### Stats / KPI · 1. Component Overview

A single metric card — label, value, and an optional trend or icon. Arrange several with Grid.

- React: `import { Stat } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-stat>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Stats / KPI · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `Stat`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `animated` | `AnimatedVariant` | — | Attention animation: "glow" \| "pulse" \| "sweep" \| "bounce" \| "float" \| "wiggle" \| "border-spin" (default: none). Respects `prefers-reduced-motion`. |
| `pulseColor` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | — | Color of the animation (pulse ring, glow, spinning border): a `ColorName` or any CSS color (default: the component's own color). |
| `pulseGradientTo` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | — | Second color — turns the pulse ring and spinning border into a gradient from `pulseColor` to this (default: solid `pulseColor`). |
| `label` **required** | `string` | — | Caption describing the metric, e.g. "Total revenue". |
| `value` **required** | `string \| number` | — | The headline metric value, shown prominently; a number or preformatted string. |
| `countUp` | `boolean` | `false` | Counts the number in `value` up from 0 when the stat mounts (and when `value` changes), keeping any prefix/suffix such as "$" or "%" (default: false). Respects `prefers-reduced-motion`. |
| `countUpDuration` | `number` | — | Duration of the count-up in ms (default: 1200). |
| `change` | `string` | — | Trend text, e.g. "12.5%" — shown next to an up/down arrow icon when `trend` isn't "neutral". |
| `trend` | `StatTrend` | `"neutral"` | Default: "neutral" (no arrow/color, just plain `change` text if given). |
