| `icon` | `string` | — | Icon name, e.g. "zap" — see src/core/icons.ts for the available set. Shown in a small colored square. |
| `color` | `ColorName` | `"accent"` | Accent color for the icon square (default: "accent" — follows the theme accent). Trend arrows use a fixed green/red — an up=good/down=bad signal independent of `color` — only the icon square follows `color`. |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class name(s) appended to the root element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; icon?: string; label?: string; value?: string; change?: string; }` | — | Per-part class overrides — merged after (and win over) the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect` — see §1.7.

### Stats / KPI · 3. Events

_No component-specific events._ Native DOM events (`click`, `input`, `focus`…) bubble from the rendered element as usual.

### Stats / KPI · 4. Variants

_This component has no variant prop — it has a single look (restyle it with colors / `classNames`)._

### Stats / KPI · 5. Colors and Theming

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Stat` | `pulseColor` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | — | Color of the animation (pulse ring, glow, spinning border): a `ColorName` or any CSS color (default: the component's own color). |
| `Stat` | `color` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | `"accent"` | Accent color for the icon square (default: "accent" — follows the theme accent). Trend arrows use a fixed green/red — an up=good/down=bad signal independent of `color` — only the icon square follows `color`. |

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set.

### Stats / KPI · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### Stats / KPI · 7. Component States

| Component | Prop | Type | Default | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Stat` | `animated` | `AnimatedVariant` | — | Attention animation: "glow" \| "pulse" \| "sweep" \| "bounce" \| "float" \| "wiggle" \| "border-spin" (default: none). Respects `prefers-reduced-motion`. |
| `Stat` | `countUp` | `boolean` | `false` | Counts the number in `value` up from 0 when the stat mounts (and when `value` changes), keeping any prefix/suffix such as "$" or "%" (default: false). Respects `prefers-reduced-motion`. |

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### Stats / KPI · 8. Slots / Children / Content

_No children or slots — it is configured entirely through props._

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Stats / KPI · 9. Framework Interfaces

**`Stat`** → `<l-stat>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `countUp` | `count-up` | boolean |
| `countUpDuration` | `count-up-duration` | number |
| `label` | `label` | string |
| `value` | `value` | string |
| `change` | `change` | string |
| `trend` | `trend` | string |
| `icon` | `icon` | string |
| `color` | `color` | string |
| `animated` | `animated` | string |
| `pulseColor` | `pulse-color` | string |
| `pulseGradientTo` | `pulse-gradient-to` | string |
| `transition` | `transition` | string |
| `transitionDuration` | `transition-duration` | number |
| `transitionDelay` | `transition-delay` | number |
| `hoverEffect` | `hover-effect` | string |

Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Count up*):

**React**
```tsx
<Stat label="Revenue" value="$48,290" icon="zap" countUp />
<Stat label="Active Users" value="12,483" icon="users" countUp />
<Stat label="Conversion Rate" value="3.42%" icon="activity" countUp countUpDuration={2000} />
<Stat label="Uptime" value="99.9%" icon="clock" countUp />
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-Stat label="Revenue" value="$48,290" icon="zap" count-up></l-Stat>
<l-Stat label="Active Users" value="12,483" icon="users" count-up></l-Stat>
<l-Stat label="Conversion Rate" value="3.42%" icon="activity" count-up count-up-duration="2000"></l-Stat>
<l-Stat label="Uptime" value="99.9%" icon="clock" count-up></l-Stat>

<script type="module">
  import "lojee-ui/elements";
</script>
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Stats / KPI · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Count up

`countUp` counts the number in `value` up from 0 when the stat mounts, keeping any prefix or suffix such as $ or %. Tune it with `countUpDuration` (ms). Press Replay to run it again.

```tsx
<Stat label="Revenue" value="$48,290" icon="zap" countUp />
<Stat label="Active Users" value="12,483" icon="users" countUp />
<Stat label="Conversion Rate" value="3.42%" icon="activity" countUp countUpDuration={2000} />
<Stat label="Uptime" value="99.9%" icon="clock" countUp />
```

#### Basic

A label and a value — no trend, no icon.

```tsx
<Grid cols={4} gap="md">
  <Stat label="Revenue" value="$48,290" />
  <Stat label="Active Users" value="12,483" />
  <Stat label="Conversion Rate" value="3.42%" />
  <Stat label="Churn Rate" value="1.08%" />
</Grid>
```

#### With trend

`change` plus `trend` shows a colored up/down arrow — green for up, rose for down.

```tsx
<Stat label="Revenue" value="$48,290" change="12.5%" trend="up" />
<Stat label="Churn Rate" value="1.08%" change="0.3%" trend="down" />
```

#### With icons

A leading icon, tinted with `color`.

```tsx
<Stat
  label="Revenue"
  value="$48,290"
  change="12.5%"
  trend="up"
  icon="zap"
  color="indigo"
/>
```

#### Colors

`color` only tints the icon square — it's independent of the up/down trend colors.

```tsx
<Stat label="Violet" value="256" icon="zap" color="violet" />
```

_2 more examples are on the docs page._

### Stats / KPI · 11. Component Composition

- Combined in its own examples with: `Grid`.

### Stats / KPI · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: none beyond native element semantics.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: none.
- Keyboard: native keyboard behavior of the underlying element (Tab to focus, Enter / Space to activate).
- Focus: uses the browser focus outline / the underlying control's ring.
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.
- Labelling props: `label`.

### Stats / KPI · 13. Responsive Behavior

- No breakpoint-specific rules — it is fluid and adapts to its container width.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Stats / KPI · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `Stat`: `root`, `icon`, `label`, `value`, `change`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with `color`; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect`.

## Charts

*Category: Data & Visualization.* Exports: `Chart`.

### Charts · 1. Component Overview

A lightweight, dependency-free chart — bar, line, and donut, rendered as plain inline SVG.

- React: `import { Chart } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-chart>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Charts · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `Chart`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `type` | `ChartType` | `"bar"` | "bar" \| "line" \| "donut" (default: "bar"). |
| `data` **required** | `ChartDataPoint[]` | — | The data points to plot, in order — each with a `label`, a numeric `value` and an optional `color`. |
| `height` | `number` | `200` | Pixel height of the chart area (width always fills its container). |
| `color` | `ColorName` | `"accent"` | Default color for points that don't set their own `color`. |
| `variant` | `ChartVariant` | `"default"` | "default" \| "values" — "values" prints each data value on the chart: above every bar and line point, and with its share plus the total for a donut (default: "default"). |
| `showLabels` | `boolean` | `true` | Shows each point's label under the plot (bar/line) or as a legend (donut). |
| `countUp` | `boolean` | `true` | Grows the data in from zero when the chart mounts — bars rise, the line climbs, donut slices sweep round and the legend numbers count up (default: true — set false for a static chart). Respects `prefers-reduced-motion`. |
| `countUpDuration` | `number` | — | Duration of the count-up in ms (default: 1200). |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class names applied to the root element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; svg?: string; label?: string; }` | — | Per-part class overrides (`root`, `svg`, `label`) — merged after the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect` — see §1.7.

### Charts · 3. Events

_No component-specific events._ Native DOM events (`click`, `input`, `focus`…) bubble from the rendered element as usual.

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Chart` | `type` | `bar`, `line`, `donut` | `"bar"` | "bar" \| "line" \| "donut" (default: "bar"). |
| `Chart` | `variant` | `default`, `values` | `"default"` | "default" \| "values" — "values" prints each data value on the chart: above every bar and line point, and with its share plus the total for a donut (default: "default"). |

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set.

### Charts · 6. Sizes

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Chart` | `height` | `number` | `200` | Pixel height of the chart area (width always fills its container). |

### Charts · 7. Component States

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### Charts · 8. Slots / Children / Content

_No children or slots — it is configured entirely through props._

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

**`Chart`** → `<l-chart>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |

Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Bar*):

```tsx
  type="bar"

${REVENUE_CODE}
  ]}

```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**

### Charts · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Bar

Evenly-spaced bars scaled to the tallest value.

```tsx
<Chart
  type="bar"
  data={[
${REVENUE_CODE}
  ]}
/>
```

#### Line

A connected line with a marker at each point.

```tsx
<Chart
  type="line"
  color="emerald"
  data={[
${REVENUE_CODE}
  ]}
/>
```

#### Donut

A ring chart with a legend — each point can carry its own color.

```tsx
<Chart
  type="donut"
  data={[
${TRAFFIC_CODE}
  ]}
/>
```

#### Colors

`color` sets the default for every point that doesn't specify its own.

```tsx
<Chart type="bar" color="rose" data={data} />

{/* Any of the 12 palette colors work: slate, gray, indigo, violet, blue,
    cyan, emerald, teal, amber, orange, rose, pink. */}
```

#### Show values

```tsx
<Chart variant="values" data={revenue} />
<Chart variant="values" type="line" color="emerald" data={revenue} />
<Chart variant="values" type="donut" data={traffic} />
```

_2 more examples are on the docs page._

### Charts · 11. Component Composition

- Data-driven: pass `data` instead of child components (works the same as a Web Component).

- No breakpoint-specific rules — it is fluid and adapts to its container width.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `Chart`: `root`, `svg`, `label`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with `color`; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect`.

## Calendar

*Category: Data & Visualization.* Exports: `Calendar`.

### Calendar · 1. Component Overview

A standalone month-grid calendar — day selection, event dots, and month navigation.

- React: `import { Calendar } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-calendar>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Calendar · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `Calendar`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `month` | `string` | — | Month being displayed, as "YYYY-MM-DD" or "YYYY-MM" (only year+month matter). Uncontrolled (internal state, defaulting to today's month) unless both `month` and `onMonthChange` are given. |
| `onMonthChange` | `(month: string) => void` | — | Called with the first day of the newly shown month as "YYYY-MM-DD" when the user navigates to another month. |
| `selected` | `string` | — | Selected date "YYYY-MM-DD". Uncontrolled (internal state) unless both `selected` and `onSelect` are given. |
| `onSelect` | `(date: string) => void` | — | Called with the clicked date as "YYYY-MM-DD" when a day is picked (or with "" when the selection is cleared), in single-selection mode. |
| `events` | `CalendarEvent[]` | `[]` | Dates with a small colored dot marker under the day number. |
| `color` | `ColorName` | `"accent"` | Accent color for the selected-day fill and today's outline (default "accent"). |
| `defaultSelected` | `string` | — | Initial selected date "YYYY-MM-DD" when `selected` isn't given. |
| `selectionMode` | `CalendarSelectionMode` | `"single"` | "single" (default) picks one date; "range" picks a start and an end date — click once for the start, again for the end (clicking an earlier date swaps them; a third click starts over). |
| `selectedRange` | `CalendarRange` | — | Range mode: the selected range. Uncontrolled (internal state) unless both `selectedRange` and `onRangeSelect` are given. |
| `defaultRange` | `CalendarRange` | — | Range mode: initial range when `selectedRange` isn't given. |
| `onRangeSelect` | `(range: CalendarRange) => void` | — | Range mode: called on every click — first with only `start`, then with both `start` and `end`; `{}` when cleared. |
| `variant` | `CalendarVariant` | `"inline"` | Layout (default "inline"): - "inline" — just the month grid. - "modal" — styled like a dialog: a header showing the selected date in large type, the grid, and Cancel / Select buttons. Pass `open` (+ `onClose`) to show it as a real overlay dialog; without `open` it renders in place. |
| `title` | `string` | `"Select date"` | Caption above the selected date in the "modal" header (default "Select date"). |
| `open` | `boolean` | — | Overlay mode for `variant="modal"`: show / hide the dialog. Leave undefined to render the panel in place. |
| `onClose` | `() => void` | — | Called when the overlay should close (backdrop click, Escape, Cancel or Select). |
| `onConfirm` | `(date: string \| undefined, range?: CalendarRange) => void` | — | "modal" footer: called when Select is pressed, with the selected date — or, in range mode, `undefined` and the range as the second argument (the Web Component's `confirm` event carries only the first argument, so read the range from the last `rangeselect` event there). |
| `onCancel` | `() => void` | — | "modal" footer: called when Cancel is pressed. |
| `confirmLabel` | `string` | `"Select"` | Text of the confirm button in the "modal" footer (default: "Select"). |
| `cancelLabel` | `string` | `"Cancel"` | Text of the cancel button in the "modal" footer (default: "Cancel"). |
| `footer` | `boolean` | `false` | Built-in footer for the inline layout: the selected date, plus "Today" and "Clear" buttons. Always shown in "modal". |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class names applied to the root element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; header?: string; weekday?: string; day?: string; selectedDay?: string; }` | — | Per-part class overrides (`root`, `header`, `weekday`, `day`, `selectedDay`) — merged after the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect` — see §1.7.

### Calendar · 3. Events

| Component | React callback | Signature | Web Component event | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Calendar` | `onMonthChange` | `(month: string) => void` | `monthchange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with the first day of the newly shown month as "YYYY-MM-DD" when the user navigates to another month. |
| `Calendar` | `onSelect` | `(date: string) => void` | `select` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with the clicked date as "YYYY-MM-DD" when a day is picked (or with "" when the selection is cleared), in single-selection mode. |
| `Calendar` | `onRangeSelect` | `(range: CalendarRange) => void` | `rangeselect` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Range mode: called on every click — first with only `start`, then with both `start` and `end`; `{}` when cleared. |
| `Calendar` | `onClose` | `() => void` | `close` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called when the overlay should close (backdrop click, Escape, Cancel or Select). |
| `Calendar` | `onConfirm` | `(date: string \| undefined, range?: CalendarRange) => void` | `confirm` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | "modal" footer: called when Select is pressed, with the selected date — or, in range mode, `undefined` and the range as the second argument (the Web Component's `confirm` event carries only the first argument, so read the range from the last `rangeselect` event there). |
| `Calendar` | `onCancel` | `() => void` | `cancel` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | "modal" footer: called when Cancel is pressed. |
| `Calendar` | `onUpdate` | — | `update` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | The value was committed (the native `change`); `detail` = the new value (`true` / `false` for a checkbox or switch, the value `string` / `number` otherwise). React: `onChange`. |

### Calendar · 4. Variants

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Calendar` | `variant` | `inline`, `modal` | `"inline"` | Layout (default "inline"): - "inline" — just the month grid. - "modal" — styled like a dialog: a header showing the selected date in large type, the grid, and Cancel / Select buttons. Pass `open` (+ `onClose`) to show it as a real overlay dialog; without `open` it renders in place. |

### Calendar · 5. Colors and Theming

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Calendar` | `color` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | `"accent"` | Accent color for the selected-day fill and today's outline (default "accent"). |

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set.

### Calendar · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### Calendar · 7. Component States

| Component | Prop | Type | Default | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `Calendar` | `selected` | `string` | — | Selected date "YYYY-MM-DD". Uncontrolled (internal state) unless both `selected` and `onSelect` are given. |
| `Calendar` | `open` | `boolean` | — | Overlay mode for `variant="modal"`: show / hide the dialog. Leave undefined to render the panel in place. |

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### Calendar · 8. Slots / Children / Content

_No children or slots — it is configured entirely through props._

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Calendar · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Basic

Uncontrolled — manages its own displayed month and selection internally, defaulting to today's month.

```tsx
<Calendar />
```

#### With events

Dates with matching events show up to 3 small colored dots beneath the day number.

```tsx
<Calendar
  events={[
    { date: "2026-06-05", label: "Team sync", color: "indigo" },
    { date: "2026-06-12", label: "Deadline", color: "rose" },
    { date: "2026-06-12", label: "Release", color: "emerald" },
    { date: "2026-06-20", label: "Planning", color: "amber" },
  ]}
/>
```

#### Controlled selection

Controlled selection — pass both `selected` and `onSelect` to drive the highlighted day from your own state.

```tsx
const [selected, setSelected] = useState();

<Calendar selected={selected} onSelect={setSelected} />
```

#### Selected date

Built-in selected-date functions: defaultSelected seeds the selection, onSelect reports every pick, and the footer prop adds the selected date with Today (jump to today and select it) and Clear buttons.

```tsx
<Calendar
  footer                              // selected date + Today + Clear
  defaultSelected="2026-06-05"        // initial selection
  onSelect={(date) => console.log(date)}   // "" when cleared
/>
```

#### Range date

```tsx
<Calendar
  selectionMode="range"
  footer
  onRangeSelect={({ start, end }) => console.log(start, end)}
/>

// Modal style — Select is enabled once both dates are picked
<Calendar
  selectionMode="range"
  variant="modal"
  onConfirm={(_, range) => save(range.start, range.end)}
/>

// Seed or control the range
<Calendar selectionMode="range" defaultRange={{ start: "2026-06-05", end: "2026-06-12" }} />
```

_3 more examples are on the docs page._

### Calendar · 11. Component Composition

- Data-driven: pass `events` instead of child components (works the same as a Web Component).

### Calendar · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: `dialog`, `presentation`.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: `aria-label`.
- Keyboard: has keyboard handling (e.g. Escape / arrow keys) — see the docs page for the exact keys.
- Focus: uses the browser focus outline / the underlying control's ring.
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Calendar · 13. Responsive Behavior

- No breakpoint-specific rules — it is fluid and adapts to its container width.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Calendar · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `Calendar`: `root`, `header`, `weekday`, `day`, `selectedDay`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with `color`; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect`.

## Activity Feed

*Category: Data & Visualization.* Exports: `ActivityFeed`.

### Activity Feed · 1. Component Overview

A vertical feed of "who did what" activity entries — an avatar, a sentence, and a timestamp.

- React: `import { ActivityFeed } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-activity-feed>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Activity Feed · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `ActivityFeed`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `items` **required** | `ActivityItem[]` | — | Activity entries to display, in order, each as an avatar, an actor/action/target sentence and a timestamp. |
| `compact` | `boolean` | `false` | Denser spacing and smaller avatars, for sidebars or narrow panels. |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class names applied to the root element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; item?: string; avatar?: string; content?: string; timestamp?: string; }` | — | Per-part class overrides (`root`, `item`, `avatar`, `content`, `timestamp`) — merged after the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay` — see §1.7.

### Activity Feed · 3. Events

_No component-specific events._ Native DOM events (`click`, `input`, `focus`…) bubble from the rendered element as usual.

### Activity Feed · 4. Variants

_This component has no variant prop — it has a single look (restyle it with colors / `classNames`)._

### Activity Feed · 5. Colors and Theming

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set. It has no color prop of its own.

### Activity Feed · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### Activity Feed · 7. Component States

_No dedicated state props — the visual states are default, hover, focus-visible and active._

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### Activity Feed · 8. Slots / Children / Content

_No children or slots — it is configured entirely through props._

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Activity Feed · 9. Framework Interfaces

**`ActivityFeed`** → `<l-activity-feed>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `items` | `items` | json (JSON string or object property) |
| `compact` | `compact` | boolean |
| `transition` | `transition` | string |
| `transitionDuration` | `transition-duration` | number |
| `transitionDelay` | `transition-delay` | number |

Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Basic*):

**React**
```tsx
<ActivityFeed
  items={[
${BASIC_ITEMS_CODE}
  ]}
/>
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-ActivityFeed id="activity-feed-basic"></l-ActivityFeed>

<script type="module">
  import "lojee-ui/elements";

  document.getElementById("activity-feed-basic").items = [
${BASIC_ITEMS_CODE}
  ];
</script>
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Activity Feed · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Basic

Each entry pairs an avatar with a short sentence and a relative timestamp.

```tsx
<ActivityFeed
  items={[
${BASIC_ITEMS_CODE}
  ]}
/>
```

#### With icon badges

A small colored icon badge on the avatar's corner — useful to signal the kind of action at a glance.

```tsx
<ActivityFeed
  items={[
${ICON_ITEMS_CODE}
  ]}
/>
```

#### Compact

Set compact for tighter spacing and smaller avatars — handy in a sidebar or narrow panel.

```tsx
<ActivityFeed
  compact
  items={[
${ICON_ITEMS_CODE}
  ]}
/>
```

#### Transitions

Enter transitions via `transition` (with `transitionDuration` / `transitionDelay`) and hover effects via `hoverEffect`. Press Replay to run the enter transitions again.

```tsx
const items = [
  { actor: "Jordan Diaz", avatarInitials: "JD", action: "commented on", target: "Q3 Report", timestamp: "2h ago" },
  { actor: "Alex Chen", avatarInitials: "AC", action: "closed", target: "Bug #482", timestamp: "4h ago" },
  { actor: "Priya Nair", avatarInitials: "PN", action: "joined the team", timestamp: "6h ago" },
];

<ActivityFeed items={items} transition="fade" />
<ActivityFeed items={items} transition="slide-up" />
<ActivityFeed items={items} transition="slide-right" transitionDelay={100} />
<ActivityFeed items={items} transition="drop" transitionDuration={700} />
```

### Activity Feed · 11. Component Composition

- Data-driven: pass `items` instead of child components (works the same as a Web Component).

### Activity Feed · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: none beyond native element semantics.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: none.
- Keyboard: native keyboard behavior of the underlying element (Tab to focus, Enter / Space to activate).
- Focus: uses the browser focus outline / the underlying control's ring.
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Activity Feed · 13. Responsive Behavior

- No breakpoint-specific rules — it is fluid and adapts to its container width.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Activity Feed · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `ActivityFeed`: `root`, `item`, `avatar`, `content`, `timestamp`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with the theme accent; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`.

## ── Diagrams ──

## Flow Diagram

*Category: Diagrams.* Exports: `FlowDiagram`, `FlowToolbar`.

### Flow Diagram · 1. Component Overview

A data-driven SVG diagram. Give it nodes and edges and it lays itself out in layers, draws animated wires, highlights connections on hover and reports clicks. Six visual variants, three wire styles, two directions.

- React: `import { FlowDiagram } from "lojee-ui"` · `import { FlowToolbar } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-flow-diagram>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Flow Diagram · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `FlowDiagram`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `nodes` **required** | `FlowNodeData[]` | — | The boxes of the diagram. Each has an `id`, a `label` and optionally a `sublabel`, `icon`, `shape` ("rect", "pill" or "circle"), `tone` ("default", "accent" or "muted") and a fixed `layer` (otherwise derived from the edges). |
| `edges` **required** | `FlowEdgeData[]` | — | Connections between nodes, by `from` / `to` node id, with an optional `label` drawn on the wire. Nodes are placed in layers automatically from these. |
| `direction` | `FlowDirection \| "auto"` | `"horizontal"` | Flow direction: "horizontal" (left → right, default), "vertical" (top → bottom) or "auto" — horizontal when there is room, vertical when the container is narrow (a phone). |
| `variant` | `FlowVariant` | `"schematic"` | Visual style: "schematic" (default), "blueprint", "minimal", "solid", "outline" or "glow". |
| `curve` | `FlowCurve` | `"smooth"` | Wire shape: "smooth" (default), "step" (right angles) or "straight". |
| `color` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | `"accent"` | Accent color of active nodes, wires and packets (default "accent" — follows the theme). A built-in color name or any CSS color. |
| `packets` | `boolean` | `true` | Show small dots travelling along the wires (default true). Off automatically when the user prefers reduced motion. |
| `speed` | `number` | `2.2` | Seconds a packet takes to cross one wire (default 2.2). |
| `animated` | `boolean` | `true` | Animate the dashes of highlighted wires (default true). |
| `arrows` | `boolean` | `false` | Draw arrowheads at the end of each wire (default false). |
| `interactive` | `boolean` | `true` | Hovering or selecting a node highlights its wires and dims the rest; clicking selects it (default true). |
| `activeNode` | `string` | — | Selected node id. Uncontrolled (internal state) unless both `activeNode` and `onNodeClick` are given. |
| `defaultActiveNode` | `string` | — | Initially selected node id when `activeNode` isn't given. |
| `autoPlay` | `boolean \| number` | `false` | Walk through the nodes automatically, selecting the next one every N milliseconds (`true` = 1600). Pauses while hovering; ignored when `activeNode` is controlled. |
| `grid` | `boolean` | — | Draw a blueprint grid behind the diagram (default: on for the "blueprint" variant). |
| `captionTop` | `string` | — | Small monospace label above the diagram. |
| `captionBottom` | `string` | — | Small monospace label below the diagram. |
| `nodeWidth` | `number` | `150` | Node width in px (default 150). Circles use the node height plus a margin. |
| `nodeHeight` | `number` | `56` | Node height in px (default 56). |
| `gap` | `number` | `110` | Space between layers in px (default 110). |
| `spacing` | `number` | `22` | Space between nodes of the same layer in px (default 22). |
| `movable` | `boolean` | `false` | Let nodes be dragged with the mouse or touch (and nudged with the arrow keys — Shift for bigger steps). The wires follow; nodes stay inside the diagram (default false). |
| `onNodeMove` | `(move: { id: string; x: number; y: number }) => void` | — | Called continuously while a node moves, with its id and its new top-left position in diagram units. |
| `editable` | `boolean` | `false` | Turn the diagram into an editor: a toolbar to add elements (pick a type), connect two elements, rename the selected element or wire, delete it, and reset. Nodes become movable, double-click renames, and Delete removes the selection. The diagram keeps its own edited copy of `nodes` / `edges`, seeded from the props (pass stable arrays — a new array resets the edits). |
| `zoomable` | `boolean` | `false` | Show zoom controls (− / % / +) and allow Ctrl/⌘ + scroll to zoom, from 50% to 250% (default false; always on with `editable`). |
| `nodeTypes` | `FlowNodeType[]` | `DEFAULT_NODE_TYPES` | The kinds of element the editor can add — each has a `key`, `label` and optional `shape`, `tone` and `icon`. Defaults to Process, Start / End, Decision, Data, Service and Note. |
| `onDiagramChange` | `(data: { nodes: FlowNodeData[]; edges: FlowEdgeData[] }) => void` | — | Editor: called with the full `{ nodes, edges }` after every edit (add, connect, rename, delete, reset). |
| `toolbarPosition` | `"top" \| "right"` | `"top"` | Where the editor / zoom toolbar goes: "top" (a row above the diagram, default) or "right" (a vertical column at the top right). |
| `toolbarTarget` | `HTMLElement \| null` | — | React only: render the toolbar into this element (for example a panel beside the diagram) instead of above it. Pass `null` while the element isn't mounted yet — nothing is rendered until it is. |
| `label` | `string` | `"Flow diagram"` | Accessible description of the whole diagram. |
| `onNodeClick` | `(node: FlowNodeData) => void` | — | Called with the node when it is clicked or activated with the keyboard. |
| `onNodeHover` | `(node: FlowNodeData \| null) => void` | — | Called with the node when the pointer enters it, and with `null` when it leaves. |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class names applied to the root element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; svg?: string }` | — | Per-part class overrides (`root`, `svg`) — merged after the built-in styling. |

#### `FlowToolbar`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `editable` **required** | `boolean` | — | — |
| `zoomable` **required** | `boolean` | — | — |
| `types` **required** | `FlowNodeType[]` | — | — |
| `typeKey` **required** | `string` | — | — |
| `onTypeChange` **required** | `(key: string) => void` | — | — |
| `onAdd` **required** | `() => void` | — | — |
| `connectMode` **required** | `boolean` | — | — |
| `connectFrom` **required** | `string \| null` | — | — |
| `onToggleConnect` **required** | `() => void` | — | — |
| `canRename` **required** | `boolean` | — | — |
| `onRename` **required** | `() => void` | — | — |
| `canDelete` **required** | `boolean` | — | — |
| `onDelete` **required** | `() => void` | — | — |
| `onReset` **required** | `() => void` | — | — |
| `zoom` **required** | `number` | — | — |
| `onZoom` **required** | `(next: number) => void` | — | — |
| `vertical` | `boolean` | — | Stack the tools in a column (used when the toolbar sits at the right of the diagram). |

### Flow Diagram · 3. Events

| Component | React callback | Signature | Web Component event | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `FlowDiagram` | `onNodeMove` | `(move: { id: string; x: number; y: number }) => void` | `nodemove` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called continuously while a node moves, with its id and its new top-left position in diagram units. |
| `FlowDiagram` | `onDiagramChange` | `(data: { nodes: FlowNodeData[]; edges: FlowEdgeData[] }) => void` | `diagramchange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Editor: called with the full `{ nodes, edges }` after every edit (add, connect, rename, delete, reset). |
| `FlowDiagram` | `onNodeClick` | `(node: FlowNodeData) => void` | `nodeclick` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with the node when it is clicked or activated with the keyboard. |
| `FlowDiagram` | `onNodeHover` | `(node: FlowNodeData \| null) => void` | `nodehover` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with the node when the pointer enters it, and with `null` when it leaves. |
| `FlowToolbar` | `onTypeChange` | `(key: string) => void` | — (React only) | — |
| `FlowToolbar` | `onAdd` | `() => void` | — (React only) | — |
| `FlowToolbar` | `onToggleConnect` | `() => void` | — (React only) | — |
| `FlowToolbar` | `onRename` | `() => void` | — (React only) | — |
| `FlowToolbar` | `onDelete` | `() => void` | — (React only) | — |
| `FlowToolbar` | `onReset` | `() => void` | — (React only) | — |
| `FlowToolbar` | `onZoom` | `(next: number) => void` | — (React only) | — |

### Flow Diagram · 4. Variants

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `FlowDiagram` | `direction` | `horizontal`, `vertical`, `auto` | `"horizontal"` | Flow direction: "horizontal" (left → right, default), "vertical" (top → bottom) or "auto" — horizontal when there is room, vertical when the container is narrow (a phone). |
| `FlowDiagram` | `variant` | `schematic`, `blueprint`, `minimal`, `solid`, `outline`, `glow` | `"schematic"` | Visual style: "schematic" (default), "blueprint", "minimal", "solid", "outline" or "glow". |

### Flow Diagram · 5. Colors and Theming

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `FlowDiagram` | `color` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | `"accent"` | Accent color of active nodes, wires and packets (default "accent" — follows the theme). A built-in color name or any CSS color. |

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set.

### Flow Diagram · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### Flow Diagram · 7. Component States

| Component | Prop | Type | Default | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `FlowDiagram` | `animated` | `boolean` | `true` | Animate the dashes of highlighted wires (default true). |

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### Flow Diagram · 8. Slots / Children / Content

_No children or slots — it is configured entirely through props._

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Flow Diagram · 9. Framework Interfaces

**`FlowDiagram`** → `<l-flow-diagram>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `nodes` | `nodes` | json (JSON string or object property) |
| `edges` | `edges` | json (JSON string or object property) |

- `FlowToolbar`: React only (no standalone Web Component — used through its parent).
Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Shapes and tones*):

**React**
```tsx
<FlowDiagram
  direction="vertical"
  nodes={[
${SHAPE_CODE}
  ]}
  edges={[{ from: "rect", to: "hexagon" }, { from: "circle", to: "parallelogram" }, { from: "diamond", to: "cylinder" }]}
/>
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-flow-diagram id="shapes" direction="vertical"></l-flow-diagram>

<script type="module">
  import "lojee-ui/elements";

  const el = document.getElementById("shapes");
  el.nodes = [
${SHAPE_CODE}
  ];
  el.edges = [{ from: "rect", to: "hexagon" }, { from: "circle", to: "parallelogram" }, { from: "diamond", to: "cylinder" }];
</script>
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Flow Diagram · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Shapes and tones

```tsx
<FlowDiagram
  direction="vertical"
  nodes={[
${SHAPE_CODE}
  ]}
  edges={[{ from: "rect", to: "hexagon" }, { from: "circle", to: "parallelogram" }, { from: "diamond", to: "cylinder" }]}
/>
```

#### Interactive and controlled

Control the selected node and react to clicks — the connected wires light up and the rest fades.

```tsx
const [active, setActive] = useState("worker");

<FlowDiagram
  nodes={nodes}
  edges={edges}
  activeNode={active}
  onNodeClick={(node) => setActive(node.id)}
  onNodeHover={(node) => console.log(node?.label)}
/>
```

#### Editor and zoom

editable turns the diagram into a small editor. Pick an element type and Add it (it is connected to the selected element), use Connect to wire two elements together, double-click — or Rename — to edit a label (wires too), select and Delete to remove, and Reset to start over. zoomable adds the − / % / + controls; Ctrl/⌘ + scroll zooms too.

```tsx
<FlowDiagram
  nodes={nodes}
  edges={edges}
  editable                       // toolbar: Add, Connect, Rename, Delete, Reset + zoom
  nodeTypes={[                   // optional — the element types to choose from
    { key: "step", label: "Step", shape: "rect", icon: "box" },
    { key: "gate", label: "Gate", shape: "circle" },
  ]}
  onDiagramChange={({ nodes, edges }) => save(nodes, edges)}
/>

// Zoom controls without the editor
<FlowDiagram nodes={nodes} edges={edges} zoomable />
```

#### Draggable nodes

movable lets you drag nodes with the mouse or touch (or nudge the focused node with the arrow keys — hold Shift for bigger steps). The wires follow, nodes stay inside the diagram, and onNodeMove reports every position.

```tsx
<FlowDiagram
  nodes={nodes}
  edges={edges}
  movable
  onNodeMove={({ id, x, y }) => console.log(id, x, y)}
/>
```

#### Dynamic data

Because the data is just arrays, the diagram can change at runtime — the layout re-flows as nodes and wires come and go.

```tsx
const [nodes, setNodes] = useState([{ id: "n1", label: "Start" }]);
const [edges, setEdges] = useState([]);

const add = (from) => {
  const id = "n" + (nodes.length + 1);
  setNodes([...nodes, { id, label: "Step " + (nodes.length + 1) }]);
  setEdges([...edges, { from, to: id }]);
};

<FlowDiagram nodes={nodes} edges={edges} />
```

### Flow Diagram · 11. Component Composition

- Built from `FlowDiagram`, `FlowToolbar` — use them together as shown in the examples above.

### Flow Diagram · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: `img`, `button`, `toolbar`.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: `aria-label`, `aria-orientation`, `aria-pressed`.
- Keyboard: has keyboard handling (e.g. Escape / arrow keys) — see the docs page for the exact keys.
- Focus: visible focus ring (`focus-visible`).
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.
- Labelling props: `label`.

### Flow Diagram · 13. Responsive Behavior

- No breakpoint-specific rules — it is fluid and adapts to its container width.
- Measures its own container (container queries / ResizeObserver), so it adapts inside narrow panels, not only on small viewports.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Flow Diagram · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `FlowDiagram`: `root`, `svg`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with `color`; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: no per-component motion props.

## ── Maps ──

## Map

*Category: Maps.* Exports: `Map`, `MapControls`, ``.

### Map · 1. Component Overview

An interactive vector map built on MapLibre GL with free CARTO basemaps — no API key. It follows the light / dark theme, loads MapLibre only when a map is shown, and works as  <Map> in React and <l-map> in Vue, Angular and plain JS. Compose it with markers and routes, or pass them as data.

- React: `import { Map } from "lojee-ui"` · `import { MapControls } from "lojee-ui"` · `import {  } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-map>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Map · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `Map`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `center` | `LngLat` | `[0, 20]` | Map center as `[lng, lat]` (default `[0, 20]`). Changing it moves the map; panning does not write back. |
| `zoom` | `number` | `2` | Zoom level, 0 (world) – 22 (default 2). |
| `pitch` | `number` | `0` | Camera tilt in degrees, 0 – 85 (default 0). |
| `bearing` | `number` | `0` | Camera rotation in degrees (default 0). |
| `mapStyle` | `MapStyleName \| (string & {})` | `"auto"` | Base map: "auto" (default — light or dark to match the theme), "light", "dark", "voyager", "light-minimal", "dark-minimal" (no labels), "osm", "satellite", or the URL of any MapLibre style JSON. Named styles need no API key. |
| `controls` | `boolean \| MapControlName[]` | `false` | Show map controls: `true` for zoom, compass, locate and fullscreen, or pick from "zoom" \| "compass" \| "locate" \| "fullscreen" \| "scale" \| "style" (a base-map switcher). |
| `markers` | `MapMarkerData[]` | — | Markers drawn from plain data (the only way to add markers from a Web Component). |
| `routes` | `MapRouteData[]` | — | Routes drawn from plain data. |
| `fitBounds` | `boolean` | `false` | Zoom to fit all `markers` and `routes` (with `coordinates`) once the map is ready, and whenever they change. |
| `fitPadding` | `number` | `60` | Padding in px around the fitted bounds (default 60). |
| `interactive` | `boolean` | `true` | Allow panning, zooming and rotating with the mouse / touch (default true). Read once, when the map is created. |
| `onLoad` | `(map: MapLibre.Map) => void` | — | Called once the map and its style have loaded, with the MapLibre map instance. |
| `onStyleChange` | `(style: MapStyleName) => void` | — | Called when the user picks a base map with the "style" control. |
| `onMove` | `(view: MapViewState) => void` | — | Called when the user finishes moving the map, with the new view. |
| `onMapClick` | `(position: { lng: number; lat: number }) => void` | — | Called when the map (not a marker or route) is clicked. |
| `onMarkerClick` | `(marker: MapMarkerData) => void` | — | Called when a data-driven marker is clicked. |
| `onMarkerDragEnd` | `(marker: MapMarkerData, position: { lng: number; lat: number }) => void` | — | Called with the marker and its new position when a draggable data-driven marker is dropped. |
| `onRouteClick` | `(route: MapRouteData) => void` | — | Called when a data-driven route is clicked. |
| `onRouteLoad` | `(summary: MapRouteSummary) => void` | — | Called when a data-driven route's geometry is known, with its length and (for fetched routes) travel time. |
| `children` | `ReactNode` | — | `MapMarker`, `MapRoute`, `MapControls` or your own components that use `useMap()`. |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class names applied to the root element (its default size is `h-[360px] w-full`). |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; map?: string }` | — | Per-part class overrides (`root`, `map`) — merged after the built-in styling. |

#### `MapControls`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `controls` | `MapControlName[]` | `DEFAULT` | Which controls to show (default: zoom, compass, locate and fullscreen). Also "scale" for a distance scale bar. |
| `position` | `MapLibre.ControlPosition` | `"top-right"` | Corner of the map for the button controls (default "top-right"). The scale bar is always bottom-left. |

#### ``

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `value` **required** | `MapStyleName \| (string & {})` | — | — |
| `dark` **required** | `boolean` | — | — |
| `onChange` **required** | `(style: MapStyleName) => void` | — | — |

#### Data shapes (objects passed to props)

**`MapViewState`**. Reported by `onMove` / the `move` event.

| Field | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `center` **required** | `LngLat` | — |
| `zoom` **required** | `number` | — |
| `pitch` **required** | `number` | — |
| `bearing` **required** | `number` | — |

#### Hooks

| Hook | Signature | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `useMap` | `useMap(): MapContextValue` | The map instance and helpers for custom children. Only valid inside `<Map>`. |

| `Map` | `onRouteClick` | `(route: MapRouteData) => void` | `routeclick` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called when a data-driven route is clicked. |
| `Map` | `onRouteLoad` | `(summary: MapRouteSummary) => void` | `routeload` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called when a data-driven route's geometry is known, with its length and (for fetched routes) travel time. |

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set. It has no color prop of its own.

### Map · 10. Complete Usage Examples

No packaged examples. Minimal use:

```tsx
import { Map } from "lojee-ui";

<Map />
```
### Map · 11. Component Composition

- Built from `Map`, `MapControls`, `` — use them together as shown in the examples above.
- Data-driven: pass `markers`, `routes` instead of child components (works the same as a Web Component).

### Map · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: `listbox`, `option`.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: `aria-expanded`, `aria-haspopup`, `aria-hidden`, `aria-selected`.
- Keyboard: native keyboard behavior of the underlying element (Tab to focus, Enter / Space to activate).
- Focus: uses the browser focus outline / the underlying control's ring.
- Motion: inherits the library's reduced-motion handling for transitions.

### Map · 13. Responsive Behavior

- No breakpoint-specific rules — it is fluid and adapts to its container width.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Map · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `Map`: `root`, `map`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with the theme accent; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: no per-component motion props.

## Map Markers

*Category: Maps.* Exports: `MapMarker`.

### Map Markers · 1. Component Overview

Pins on the map. In React place <MapMarker> inside <Map>; everywhere (and in Vue, Angular and plain JS) you can pass plain data as markers instead. Colors follow the theme accent, and popups follow the light / dark theme.

- React: `import { MapMarker } from "lojee-ui"`

### Map Markers · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `MapMarker`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `lng` **required** | `number` | — | Longitude. |
| `lat` **required** | `number` | — | Latitude. |
| `label` | `string` | — | Text shown beside the pin. |
| `color` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | `"accent"` | Pin color: a built-in color name (default "accent", which follows the theme) or any CSS color. |
| `icon` | `string` | — | Icon name from the library's icon set, drawn inside the pin (e.g. "home"). |
| `popup` | `ReactNode` | — | Content of a popup opened by clicking the marker — text or any React content. |
| `tooltip` | `string` | — | Short text shown in a small bubble while the pointer is over the marker. |
| `draggable` | `boolean` | `false` | Let the user drag the marker (default false). |
| `children` | `ReactNode` | — | Replace the default pin with your own content. |
| `onClick` | `() => void` | — | Called when the marker is clicked. |
| `onDragEnd` | `(position: { lng: number; lat: number }) => void` | — | Called with the new position when a drag ends. |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class names applied to the default pin. |

#### Data shapes (objects passed to props)

**`MapMarkerData`** — passed via `markers`

| Field | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | `string` | Stable id, reported back by marker events. Defaults to the marker's index. |
| `lng` **required** | `number` | — |
| `lat` **required** | `number` | — |
| `label` | `string` | Text shown under the pin. |
| `color` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | Pin color: a built-in color name (including "accent", the default) or any CSS color. |
| `popup` | `string` | Text shown in a popup when the marker is clicked. |
| `tooltip` | `string` | Text shown in a small bubble while hovering the marker. |
| `icon` | `string` | Icon name from the library's icon set, drawn inside the pin (e.g. "home"). |
| `draggable` | `boolean` | Let the marker be dragged to a new position. |

## Map Routes

*Category: Maps.* Exports: `MapRoute`.

### Map Routes · 1. Component Overview

Draw paths on a map: give an array of two or more points (waypoints) and the real road route is found for you from the free OSRM service — or pass your own coordinates for a custom line. Lines can be dashed, animated, highlighted, clicked, and drawn partly complete. Use <MapRoute> inside <Map> in React, or pass routes data everywhere.

- React: `import { MapRoute } from "lojee-ui"`

### Map Routes · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `MapRoute`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `coordinates` | `LngLat[]` | — | The line to draw, as `[lng, lat]` pairs. |
| `waypoints` | `LngLat[]` | — | The points to travel through, in order, as `[lng, lat]` pairs — `[A, B]` for a trip from A to B, `[A, B, C, D]` for any number of stops. The route between them follows the real roads (fetched from the public OSRM demo servers; falls back to straight lines if they can't be reached). Use this instead of `coordinates`. |
| `prefer` | `RoutePreference` | `"shortest"` | Which route to draw when there are alternatives between two points: "shortest" (least distance) or "fastest" (least travel time) (default: "shortest"). Alternatives only exist for a trip of exactly two points. |
| `profile` | `RouteProfile` | `"driving"` | How the trip is made: "driving", "cycling" or "walking" — each uses roads / paths that mode may take (default: "driving"). |
| `routingUrl` | `string` | — | Base URL of your own OSRM routing server, e.g. "https://osrm.example.com/route/v1/driving" — replaces the public demo servers (use this in production). |
| `onRouteError` | `(error: Error) => void` | — | Called when the road route could not be fetched (the line then falls back to straight segments between the points). |
| `color` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | `"accent"` | Line color: a built-in color name (default "accent", which follows the theme) or any CSS color. |
| `width` | `number` | `4` | Line width in px (default 4). |
| `opacity` | `number` | `0.85` | Line opacity from 0 to 1 (default 0.85). |
| `dashArray` | `[number, number]` | — | Dash pattern as `[dash, gap]` in line widths, e.g. `[2, 2]`. |
| `progress` | `number` | — | 0 – 1: how much of the route has been travelled — the rest is drawn faded. Replaces `dashArray`. |
| `active` | `boolean` | `false` | Highlights the route: it is drawn with `activeWidth` / `activeOpacity` and above the others. |
| `activeWidth` | `number` | — | Width while active (default: width + 2). |
| `activeOpacity` | `number` | `1` | Opacity while active (default 1). |
| `animated` | `boolean \| RouteAnimation` | `false` | Animate the line: `true` / `"flow"` for marching dashes, or `"draw"` (draws on), `"pulse"` (a light comet), `"trail"` (a tracer over a ghost line), `"glow"` (breathing halo) or `"shimmer"` (a soft sheen). With `progress`, the animation plays over the travelled part only. |
| `animationSpeed` | `number` | `1` | Animation speed multiplier (default 1). |
| `animationDirection` | `"forward" \| "reverse"` | `"forward"` | Direction the animation travels (default "forward"). |
| `fit` | `boolean` | `false` | Zoom the map to this route once its geometry is known. |
| `onClick` | `() => void` | — | Called when the route line is clicked. |
| `onLoad` | `(summary: MapRouteSummary) => void` | — | Called with the route's length (and travel time for `waypoints` routes) once its geometry is known. |
| `id` | `string` | — | Stable id, reported in `onLoad`. Defaults to a generated one. |

#### Data shapes (objects passed to props)

**`MapRouteData`** — passed via `routes`

| Field | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | `string` | — |
| `coordinates` | `LngLat[]` | The line to draw, as `[lng, lat]` pairs. |
| `waypoints` | `LngLat[]` | Instead of `coordinates`: the points to travel through, in order — `[A, B]` or any number of stops (A → B → C …). The route between them follows the real roads (fetched from the public OSRM demo servers). |
| `prefer` | `"shortest" \| "fastest"` | Which route to pick when there are alternatives: "shortest" (default) or "fastest". |
| `profile` | `"driving" \| "cycling" \| "walking"` | "driving" (default), "cycling" or "walking". |
| `routingUrl` | `string` | Base URL of your own OSRM server (replaces the public demo servers). |
| `color` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | — |
| `width` | `number` | Line width in px (default 4). |
| `opacity` | `number` | Line opacity from 0 to 1 (default 0.85). |
| `dashArray` | `[number, number]` | Dash pattern as `[dash, gap]` in line widths, e.g. `[2, 2]`. |
| `progress` | `number` | 0 – 1: how much of the route has been travelled. The rest is drawn faded. |
| `active` | `boolean` | Highlights the route (uses `activeWidth` / `activeOpacity`). |
| `activeWidth` | `number` | — |
| `activeOpacity` | `number` | — |
| `animated` | `boolean \| RouteAnimation` | Animate the line: `true` / `"flow"` for marching dashes, or `"draw"`, `"pulse"`, `"trail"`, `"glow"`, `"shimmer"`. |
| `animationSpeed` | `number` | Animation speed multiplier (default 1). |
| `animationDirection` | `"forward" \| "reverse"` | Direction the animation travels (default "forward"). |

**`MapRouteSummary`**. Reported by `onLoad` / the `routeload` event.

| Field | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `id` **required** | `string` | — |
| `distance` **required** | `number` | Length in metres. |
| `duration` | `number` | Estimated travel time in seconds (only for fetched `waypoints` routes). |
| `coordinates` **required** | `LngLat[]` | — |

### Map Routes · 3. Events

| Component | React callback | Signature | Web Component event | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `MapRoute` | `onRouteError` | `(error: Error) => void` | — (React only) | Called when the road route could not be fetched (the line then falls back to straight segments between the points). |
| `MapRoute` | `onClick` | `() => void` | — (React only) | Called when the route line is clicked. |
| `MapRoute` | `onLoad` | `(summary: MapRouteSummary) => void` | — (React only) | Called with the route's length (and travel time for `waypoints` routes) once its geometry is known. |

With `progress`, the animation plays over the travelled part only. |

### Map Routes · 8. Slots / Children / Content

_No children or slots — it is configured entirely through props._

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Map Routes · 9. Framework Interfaces

- `MapRoute`: React only (no standalone Web Component).
Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Route planning*):

**React**
```tsx
import { Map, MapRoute, fetchRoutes } from "lojee-ui";

const routes = await fetchRoutes([amsterdam, rotterdam], { alternatives: true });

<Map fitBounds>
  {routes.map((r, i) => (
    <MapRoute
      key={i}
      coordinates={r.coordinates}
      active={i === selected}
      activeWidth={7}
      opacity={0.45}
      onClick={() => setSelected(i)}
    />
  ))}
</Map>
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
import "lojee-ui/elements";
import { fetchRoutes } from "lojee-ui";   // or call the OSRM API yourself

const map = document.getElementById("map");
const found = await fetchRoutes([amsterdam, rotterdam], { alternatives: true });
let selected = 0;

const draw = () => {
  map.routes = found.map((r, i) => ({ coordinates: r.coordinates, active: i === selected, activeWidth: 7, opacity: 0.45 }));
};
map.addEventListener("routeclick", (e) => { selected = Number(e.detail.id.split("-")[1]); draw(); });
draw();
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Map Routes · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Route planning

fetchRoutes() returns alternatives you can show side by side. The active route is drawn wider and brighter (active, activeWidth, activeOpacity); onClick lets users pick one.

```tsx
import { Map, MapRoute, fetchRoutes } from "lojee-ui";

const routes = await fetchRoutes([amsterdam, rotterdam], { alternatives: true });

<Map fitBounds>
  {routes.map((r, i) => (
    <MapRoute
      key={i}
      coordinates={r.coordinates}
      active={i === selected}
      activeWidth={7}
      opacity={0.45}
      onClick={() => setSelected(i)}
    />
  ))}
</Map>
```

### Map Routes · 11. Component Composition

- Combined in its own examples with: `Map`.

### Map Routes · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: none beyond native element semantics.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: none.
- Keyboard: native keyboard behavior of the underlying element (Tab to focus, Enter / Space to activate).
- Focus: uses the browser focus outline / the underlying control's ring.
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Map Routes · 13. Responsive Behavior

- No breakpoint-specific rules — it is fluid and adapts to its container width.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Map Routes · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with `color`; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: no per-component motion props.

## ── User / Account ──

## Profile Card

*Category: User / Account.* Exports: `ProfileCard`.

### Profile Card · 1. Component Overview

A user profile summary — avatar, name, role, bio, and optional stats or actions.

- React: `import { ProfileCard } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-profile-card>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Profile Card · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `ProfileCard`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `animated` | `AnimatedVariant` | — | Attention animation: "glow" \| "pulse" \| "sweep" \| "bounce" \| "float" \| "wiggle" \| "border-spin" (default: none). Respects `prefers-reduced-motion`. |
| `pulseColor` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | — | Color of the animation (pulse ring, glow, spinning border): a `ColorName` or any CSS color (default: the component's own color). |
| `pulseGradientTo` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | — | Second color — turns the pulse ring and spinning border into a gradient from `pulseColor` to this (default: solid `pulseColor`). |
| `name` **required** | `string` | — | The person's display name, shown as the card heading. |
| `role` | `string` | — | e.g. "Product Designer at Acme". |
| `bio` | `string` | — | Short biography paragraph shown below the role. |
| `avatarSrc` | `string` | — | Image URL for the avatar; falls back to `avatarInitials` when omitted. |
| `avatarInitials` | `string` | — | Initials shown in the avatar when there is no `avatarSrc`. |
| `stats` | `ProfileCardStat[]` | — | A short row of stats, e.g. Followers/Following/Posts. |
| `actions` | `ReactNode` | — | Action buttons/content, e.g. Follow + Message buttons — this component doesn't know about Button, the consumer supplies real elements here. |
| `color` | `ColorName` | `"accent"` | Accent color for the banner strip behind the avatar (default "accent"). |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra CSS class(es) added to the root element, merged before `classNames.root`. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; banner?: string; avatar?: string; stats?: string; actions?: string; }` | — | Per-part class overrides — merged after (and win over) the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect` — see §1.7.

### Profile Card · 3. Events

_No component-specific events._ Native DOM events (`click`, `input`, `focus`…) bubble from the rendered element as usual.

### Profile Card · 4. Variants

_This component has no variant prop — it has a single look (restyle it with colors / `classNames`)._

### Profile Card · 5. Colors and Theming

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ProfileCard` | `pulseColor` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | — | Color of the animation (pulse ring, glow, spinning border): a `ColorName` or any CSS color (default: the component's own color). |
| `ProfileCard` | `color` | `slate`, `gray`, `indigo`, `violet`, `blue`, `cyan`, `emerald`, `teal`, `amber`, `orange`, `rose`, `pink`, `accent` | `"accent"` | Accent color for the banner strip behind the avatar (default "accent"). |

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set.

### Profile Card · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### Profile Card · 7. Component States

| Component | Prop | Type | Default | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ProfileCard` | `animated` | `AnimatedVariant` | — | Attention animation: "glow" \| "pulse" \| "sweep" \| "bounce" \| "float" \| "wiggle" \| "border-spin" (default: none). Respects `prefers-reduced-motion`. |

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### Profile Card · 8. Slots / Children / Content

| Component | React prop | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ProfileCard` | `actions` | `ReactNode` | Action buttons/content, e.g. Follow + Message buttons — this component doesn't know about Button, the consumer supplies real elements here. |

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Profile Card · 9. Framework Interfaces

**`ProfileCard`** → `<l-profile-card>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `animated` | `animated` | string |
| `pulseColor` | `pulse-color` | string |
| `pulseGradientTo` | `pulse-gradient-to` | string |
| `name` | `name` | string |
| `role` | `role` | string |
| `bio` | `bio` | string |
| `avatarSrc` | `avatar-src` | string |
| `avatarInitials` | `avatar-initials` | string |
| `stats` | `stats` | json (JSON string or object property) |
| `color` | `color` | string |
