Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Basic*):

**React**
```tsx
<ProfileCard
  name="Priya Nair"
  role="Product Designer at Lojee"
  bio="Building accessible, joyful interfaces. Previously at Figma and Notion."
  avatarInitials="PN"
/>
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-ProfileCard
  name="Priya Nair"
  role="Product Designer at Lojee"
  bio="Building accessible, joyful interfaces. Previously at Figma and Notion."
  avatarInitials="PN"></l-ProfileCard>

<script type="module">import "lojee-ui/elements";</script>
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Profile Card · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Basic

Name, role, and a short bio — no stats or actions.

```tsx
<ProfileCard
  name="Priya Nair"
  role="Product Designer at Lojee"
  bio="Building accessible, joyful interfaces. Previously at Figma and Notion."
  avatarInitials="PN"
/>
```

#### With stats

A short row of stats, e.g. Followers/Following/Posts.

```tsx
<ProfileCard
  name="Priya Nair"
  role="Product Designer at Lojee"
  avatarInitials="PN"
  stats={[
    { label: "Followers", value: "2,481" },
    { label: "Following", value: "312" },
    { label: "Posts", value: "48" },
  ]}
/>
```

#### With actions

`actions` is rich content — this component doesn't know about Button, you supply the real elements.

```tsx
<ProfileCard
  name="Priya Nair"
  role="Product Designer at Lojee"
  avatarInitials="PN"
  actions={
    <>
      <Button label="Follow" className="flex-1" />
      <Button variant="outline" label="Message" className="flex-1" />
    </>
  }
/>
```

#### Colors

Tints the banner strip behind the avatar.

```tsx
<ProfileCard name="Priya Nair" role="Design" avatarInitials="PN" color="indigo" />
<ProfileCard name="Alex Chen" role="Engineering" avatarInitials="AC" color="emerald" />
<ProfileCard name="Marcus Lee" role="Marketing" avatarInitials="ML" color="rose" />
```

#### Transitions

Enter transitions via `transition` (with `transitionDuration` / `transitionDelay`) and hover effects via `hoverEffect`. Press Replay to run the enter transitions again.

```tsx
<ProfileCard name="Priya Nair" role="fade" avatarInitials="PN" transition="fade" />
<ProfileCard name="Marcus Lee" role="slide-up" avatarInitials="ML" transition="slide-up" />
<ProfileCard name="Ana Souza" role="slide-right" avatarInitials="AS" transition="slide-right" transitionDelay={100} />
<ProfileCard name="Tom Reed" role="zoom" avatarInitials="TR" transition="zoom" />
<ProfileCard name="Lena Fox" role="flip" avatarInitials="LF" transition="flip" />
<ProfileCard name="Kai Wong" role="drop" avatarInitials="KW" transition="drop" transitionDuration={700} />

<ProfileCard name="Priya Nair" role="lift" avatarInitials="PN" hoverEffect="lift" />
<ProfileCard name="Marcus Lee" role="glow" avatarInitials="ML" hoverEffect="glow" />
<ProfileCard name="Ana Souza" role="shine" avatarInitials="AS" hoverEffect="shine" />
```

_1 more examples are on the docs page._

### Profile Card · 11. Component Composition

- Combined in its own examples with: `Button`.

### Profile Card · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: none beyond native element semantics.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: none.
- Keyboard: native keyboard behavior of the underlying element (Tab to focus, Enter / Space to activate).
- Focus: uses the browser focus outline / the underlying control's ring.
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Profile Card · 13. Responsive Behavior

- No breakpoint-specific rules — it is fluid and adapts to its container width.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Profile Card · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `ProfileCard`: `root`, `banner`, `avatar`, `stats`, `actions`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with `color`; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect`.

## User Menu

*Category: User / Account.* Exports: `UserMenu`.

### User Menu · 1. Component Overview

An avatar-triggered dropdown menu — the classic "click your avatar" pattern, built on top of DropdownMenu.

- React: `import { UserMenu } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-user-menu>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### User Menu · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `UserMenu`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `name` **required** | `string` | — | The user's display name, shown in the trigger and at the top of the menu; also the avatar's alt text. |
| `email` | `string` | — | Email address shown under the name at the top of the menu. |
| `avatarSrc` | `string` | — | Image URL for the avatar; falls back to `avatarInitials` when omitted. |
| `avatarInitials` | `string` | — | Initials shown in the avatar when no `avatarSrc` is given. |
| `items` **required** | `UserMenuItem[]` | — | Menu entries, in order — each has a `label`, and optional `icon` and `danger`. |
| `onItemSelect` | `(item: UserMenuItem, index: number) => void` | — | Called with the clicked item and its index in `items` when the user selects a menu entry. |
| `align` | `DropdownMenuAlign` | `"end"` | Which edge of the trigger the panel hugs (default "end" — a user menu is almost always top-right, so its panel should hug the right edge). |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class name(s) appended to the root element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; trigger?: string; menu?: string }` | — | Per-part class overrides — merged after (and win over) the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect` — see §1.7.

### User Menu · 3. Events

| Component | React callback | Signature | Web Component event | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `UserMenu` | `onItemSelect` | `(item: UserMenuItem, index: number) => void` | `itemselect` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with the clicked item and its index in `items` when the user selects a menu entry. |

### User Menu · 4. Variants

| Component | Prop | Values | Default | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `UserMenu` | `align` | `start`, `end` | `"end"` | Which edge of the trigger the panel hugs (default "end" — a user menu is almost always top-right, so its panel should hug the right edge). |

### User Menu · 5. Colors and Theming

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set. It has no color prop of its own.

### User Menu · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### User Menu · 7. Component States

_No dedicated state props — the visual states are default, hover, focus-visible and active._

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### User Menu · 8. Slots / Children / Content

_No children or slots — it is configured entirely through props._

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### User Menu · 9. Framework Interfaces

**`UserMenu`** → `<l-user-menu>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `name` | `name` | string |
| `email` | `email` | string |
| `avatarSrc` | `avatar-src` | string |
| `avatarInitials` | `avatar-initials` | string |
| `items` | `items` | json (JSON string or object property) |
| `align` | `align` | string |
| `transition` | `transition` | string |
| `transitionDuration` | `transition-duration` | number |
| `transitionDelay` | `transition-delay` | number |
| `hoverEffect` | `hover-effect` | string |

Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Basic*):

**React**
```tsx
<UserMenu
  name="Jordan Diaz"
  email="jordan@lojee.io"
  avatarInitials="JD"
  items={[
    { label: "Profile", icon: "user" },
    { label: "Settings", icon: "settings" },
    { label: "Billing", icon: "tag" },
    { label: "Log out", icon: "arrow-right", danger: true },
  ]}
/>
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-UserMenu id="user-menu" name="Jordan Diaz" email="jordan@lojee.io" avatarInitials="JD"></l-UserMenu>

<script type="module">
  import "lojee-ui/elements";

  const el = document.getElementById("user-menu");
  el.items = [
    { label: "Profile", icon: "user" },
    { label: "Settings", icon: "settings" },
    { label: "Billing", icon: "tag" },
    { label: "Log out", icon: "arrow-right", danger: true },
  ];
  el.addEventListener("itemselect", (e) => {
    console.log("Selected:", e.detail);
  });
</script>
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### User Menu · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Basic

Data-driven `items` — icon, label, and an optional `danger` item like "Log out".

```tsx
<UserMenu
  name="Jordan Diaz"
  email="jordan@lojee.io"
  avatarInitials="JD"
  items={[
    { label: "Profile", icon: "user" },
    { label: "Settings", icon: "settings" },
    { label: "Billing", icon: "tag" },
    { label: "Log out", icon: "arrow-right", danger: true },
  ]}
/>
```

#### Align start

Aligns the panel to the leading edge of the trigger instead.

```tsx
<UserMenu name="Jordan Diaz" avatarInitials="JD" items={items} align="start" />
```

#### With selection handling

`onItemSelect` reports which item was clicked and its index.

```tsx
const [lastClicked, setLastClicked] = useState(null);

<UserMenu
  name="Jordan Diaz"
  avatarInitials="JD"
  items={items}
  onItemSelect={(item) => setLastClicked(item.label)}
/>
```

#### Transitions

Enter transitions via `transition` (with `transitionDuration` / `transitionDelay`) and hover effects via `hoverEffect`. They play on mount — reload the page, or use Replay in the playground.

```tsx
const items = [
  { label: "Profile", icon: "user" },
  { label: "Settings", icon: "settings" },
  { label: "Billing", icon: "tag" },
  { label: "Log out", icon: "arrow-right", danger: true },
];

<UserMenu name="Jordan Diaz" avatarInitials="JD" items={items} transition="fade" />
<UserMenu name="Jordan Diaz" avatarInitials="JD" items={items} transition="slide-down" />
<UserMenu name="Jordan Diaz" avatarInitials="JD" items={items} transition="zoom" transitionDuration={600} />
<UserMenu name="Jordan Diaz" avatarInitials="JD" items={items} transition="blur" />

<UserMenu name="Jordan Diaz" avatarInitials="JD" items={items} hoverEffect="lift" />
<UserMenu name="Jordan Diaz" avatarInitials="JD" items={items} hoverEffect="glow" />
<UserMenu name="Jordan Diaz" avatarInitials="JD" items={items} hoverEffect="shine" />
```

### User Menu · 11. Component Composition

- Data-driven: pass `items` instead of child components (works the same as a Web Component).

### User Menu · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: none beyond native element semantics.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: none.
- Keyboard: native keyboard behavior of the underlying element (Tab to focus, Enter / Space to activate).
- Focus: uses the browser focus outline / the underlying control's ring.
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### User Menu · 13. Responsive Behavior

- Breakpoint modifiers in its source: `sm`.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### User Menu · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `UserMenu`: `root`, `trigger`, `menu`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with the theme accent; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect`.

## Login Form

*Category: User / Account.* Exports: `LoginForm`.

### Login Form · 1. Component Overview

A ready-to-use login block — email, password, remember-me, and submit — built from this library's own form primitives.

- React: `import { LoginForm } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-login-form>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Login Form · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `LoginForm`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` | `string` | `"Welcome back"` | Form heading (default: "Welcome back"). |
| `description` | `string` | `"Log in to your account to continue."` | Helper text shown under the title (default: "Log in to your account to continue."); pass an empty string to hide it. |
| `submitLabel` | `string` | `"Log in"` | Submit button label (default: "Log in"). |
| `showRemember` | `boolean` | `true` | Shows the "Remember me" checkbox (default: true). |
| `showForgotPassword` | `boolean` | `true` | Shows the "Forgot password?" link next to the password label (default: true). |
| `onSubmit` | `(values: LoginFormValues) => void` | — | Fires on form submit with `{ email, password, remember }`; the default browser submit is prevented. |
| `onForgotPassword` | `() => void` | — | Fires when the "Forgot password?" link is clicked, with no arguments. |
| `footer` | `ReactNode` | — | Content below the form, e.g. a "Don't have an account? Sign up" link — this component has no opinion about routing/links, the consumer supplies it. |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class name(s) applied to the root element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; header?: string; field?: string; footer?: string }` | — | Per-part class overrides — merged after (and win over) the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect` — see §1.7.

### Login Form · 3. Events

| Component | React callback | Signature | Web Component event | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `LoginForm` | `onSubmit` | `(values: LoginFormValues) => void` | `submit` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Fires on form submit with `{ email, password, remember }`; the default browser submit is prevented. |
| `LoginForm` | `onForgotPassword` | `() => void` | `forgotpassword` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Fires when the "Forgot password?" link is clicked, with no arguments. |

### Login Form · 4. Variants

_This component has no variant prop — it has a single look (restyle it with colors / `classNames`)._

### Login Form · 5. Colors and Theming

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set. It has no color prop of its own.

### Login Form · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### Login Form · 7. Component States

_No dedicated state props — the visual states are default, hover, focus-visible and active._

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### Login Form · 8. Slots / Children / Content

| Component | React prop | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `LoginForm` | `footer` | `ReactNode` | Content below the form, e.g. a "Don't have an account? Sign up" link — this component has no opinion about routing/links, the consumer supplies it. |

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Login Form · 9. Framework Interfaces

**`LoginForm`** → `<l-login-form>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `title` | `title` | string |
| `description` | `description` | string |
| `submitLabel` | `submit-label` | string |
| `showRemember` | `show-remember` | boolean |
| `showForgotPassword` | `show-forgot-password` | boolean |
| `transition` | `transition` | string |
| `transitionDuration` | `transition-duration` | number |
| `transitionDelay` | `transition-delay` | number |
| `hoverEffect` | `hover-effect` | string |

Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Basic*):

**React**
```tsx
const [values, setValues] = useState(null);

<LoginForm onSubmit={setValues} />
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-LoginForm id="login"></l-LoginForm>

<script type="module">
  import "lojee-ui/elements";

  document.getElementById("login").addEventListener("submit", (e) => {
    console.log(e.detail); // { email, password, remember }
  });
</script>
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Login Form · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Basic

onSubmit reports the current field values — nothing is actually sent anywhere in this demo.

```tsx
const [values, setValues] = useState(null);

<LoginForm onSubmit={setValues} />
```

#### With footer link

footer projects rich content below the form — this component has no opinion about routing.

```tsx
<LoginForm
  footer={
    <p>
      Don't have an account? <a href="/signup">Sign up</a>
    </p>
  }
/>
```

#### Minimal

showRemember={false} showForgotPassword={false} for the minimal variant.

```tsx
<LoginForm showRemember={false} showForgotPassword={false} />
```

#### Transitions

Enter transitions via `transition` (with `transitionDuration` / `transitionDelay`) and hover effects via `hoverEffect`. They play on mount — reload the page, or use Replay in the playground.

```tsx
<LoginForm transition="fade" />
<LoginForm transition="slide-up" />
<LoginForm transition="zoom" transitionDelay={100} />
<LoginForm transition="flip" transitionDuration={700} />
<LoginForm hoverEffect="lift" />
<LoginForm hoverEffect="glow" />
```

- Standalone: drop it into any layout (Card, Section, Grid, Container, App shell) or inside forms and overlays.

- ARIA roles used: none beyond native element semantics.

- Keyboard: native keyboard behavior of the underlying element (Tab to focus, Enter / Space to activate).

- No breakpoint-specific rules — it is fluid and adapts to its container width.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `LoginForm`: `root`, `header`, `field`, `footer`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).

## Signup Form

*Category: User / Account.* Exports: `SignupForm`.

### Signup Form · 1. Component Overview

A ready-to-use signup block — name, email, password, confirm password, and terms — built from this library's own form primitives.

- React: `import { SignupForm } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-signup-form>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Signup Form · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `SignupForm`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `title` | `string` | `"Create your account"` | Heading shown at the top of the form (default: "Create your account"). |
| `description` | `string` | `"Start your free trial — no credit card required."` | Supporting text shown under the title (default: "Start your free trial — no credit card required."). |
| `submitLabel` | `string` | `"Create account"` | Text of the submit button (default: "Create account"). |
| `onSubmit` | `(values: SignupFormValues) => void` | — | Called with the entered `SignupFormValues` (name, email, password, confirmPassword, agreeTerms) when the form is submitted and the two password fields match. |
| `mismatchError` | `string` | `"Passwords don't match"` | Shown inline under the confirm-password field when the two password fields don't match at submit time — `onSubmit` is not called in that case. |
| `footer` | `ReactNode` | — | Rich content below the form, e.g. a "Already have an account? Log in" link — this component has no opinion about routing/links, the consumer supplies it. |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class name(s) appended to the root element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; header?: string; field?: string; footer?: string }` | — | Per-part class overrides — merged after (and win over) the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect` — see §1.7.

### Signup Form · 3. Events

| Component | React callback | Signature | Web Component event | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `SignupForm` | `onSubmit` | `(values: SignupFormValues) => void` | `submit` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with the entered `SignupFormValues` (name, email, password, confirmPassword, agreeTerms) when the form is submitted and the two password fields match. |

### Signup Form · 4. Variants

_This component has no variant prop — it has a single look (restyle it with colors / `classNames`)._

### Signup Form · 5. Colors and Theming

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set. It has no color prop of its own.

### Signup Form · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### Signup Form · 7. Component States

_No dedicated state props — the visual states are default, hover, focus-visible and active._

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### Signup Form · 8. Slots / Children / Content

| Component | React prop | Type | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `SignupForm` | `footer` | `ReactNode` | Rich content below the form, e.g. a "Already have an account? Log in" link — this component has no opinion about routing/links, the consumer supplies it. |

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Signup Form · 9. Framework Interfaces

**`SignupForm`** → `<l-signup-form>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `title` | `title` | string |
| `description` | `description` | string |
| `submitLabel` | `submit-label` | string |
| `mismatchError` | `mismatch-error` | string |
| `transition` | `transition` | string |
| `transitionDuration` | `transition-duration` | number |
| `transitionDelay` | `transition-delay` | number |
| `hoverEffect` | `hover-effect` | string |

Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Basic*):

**React**
```tsx
const [values, setValues] = useState(null);

<SignupForm onSubmit={setValues} />
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-SignupForm id="signup"></l-SignupForm>

<script type="module">
  import "lojee-ui/elements";

  document.getElementById("signup").addEventListener("submit", (e) => {
    console.log(e.detail); // { name, email, password, confirmPassword, agreeTerms }
  });
</script>
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Signup Form · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Basic

onSubmit only fires once the two password fields match and terms are agreed to — nothing is actually sent anywhere in this demo.

```tsx
const [values, setValues] = useState(null);

<SignupForm onSubmit={setValues} />
```

#### With footer link

footer projects rich content below the form — this component has no opinion about routing.

```tsx
<SignupForm
  footer={
    <p>
      Already have an account? <a href="/login">Log in</a>
    </p>
  }
/>
```

#### Password mismatch

Typing different values into Password and Confirm password, then submitting, shows an inline error under the confirm field instead of calling onSubmit.

```tsx
<SignupForm mismatchError="Those passwords don't match — try again." />
```

#### Transitions

Enter transitions via `transition` (with `transitionDuration` / `transitionDelay`) and hover effects via `hoverEffect`. They play on mount — reload the page, or use Replay in the playground.

```tsx
<SignupForm transition="fade" />
<SignupForm transition="slide-up" />
<SignupForm transition="zoom" transitionDelay={100} />
<SignupForm transition="flip" transitionDuration={700} />
<SignupForm hoverEffect="lift" />
<SignupForm hoverEffect="glow" />
```

### Signup Form · 11. Component Composition

- Standalone: drop it into any layout (Card, Section, Grid, Container, App shell) or inside forms and overlays.

### Signup Form · 12. Accessibility

- ARIA roles used: none beyond native element semantics.
- ARIA attributes set by the component: none.
- Keyboard: native keyboard behavior of the underlying element (Tab to focus, Enter / Space to activate).
- Focus: uses the browser focus outline / the underlying control's ring.
- Motion: respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.

### Signup Form · 13. Responsive Behavior

- No breakpoint-specific rules — it is fluid and adapts to its container width.
- Mobile-first; touch targets keep a comfortable minimum size.

### Signup Form · 14. Customization

- `className` adds classes to the root; `classNames` overrides individual parts — `SignupForm`: `root`, `header`, `field`, `footer`. Conflicting Tailwind utilities passed here win (tailwind-merge).
- Recolor with the theme accent; restyle globally with the theme tokens or `data-design="clay"`.
- Motion: `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect`.

## Account Settings

*Category: User / Account.* Exports: `AccountSettings`.

### Account Settings · 1. Component Overview

Account-level settings — email, password, notification preferences, and account deletion — as three independent sections, each saved on its own.

- React: `import { AccountSettings } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-account-settings>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Account Settings · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `AccountSettings`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `email` | `string` | `""` | Initial value of the email field (uncontrolled — only read on mount; default: ""). |
| `onEmailChange` | `(email: string) => void` | — | Called with the entered email address when the email section's Save button is submitted. |
| `onPasswordChange` | `(currentPassword: string, newPassword: string) => void` | — | Called with `(currentPassword, newPassword)` when the password form is submitted; both fields are cleared afterwards. |
| `notifications` | `NotificationPreference[]` | `[]` | Notification preferences rendered as toggle switches (controlled); the section is hidden when the array is empty. |
| `onNotificationsChange` | `(notifications: NotificationPreference[]) => void` | — | Called with the FULL updated array whenever any one toggle flips. |
| `onDeleteAccount` | `() => void` | — | Called when the Delete account button in the danger zone is clicked; no confirmation is shown by the component itself. |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class names applied to the root element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; section?: string }` | — | Per-part class overrides (`root`, `section` — applied to every section card) — merged after the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay`, `hoverEffect` — see §1.7.

### Account Settings · 3. Events

| Component | React callback | Signature | Web Component event | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `AccountSettings` | `onEmailChange` | `(email: string) => void` | `emailchange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with the entered email address when the email section's Save button is submitted. |
| `AccountSettings` | `onPasswordChange` | `(currentPassword: string, newPassword: string) => void` | `passwordchange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with `(currentPassword, newPassword)` when the password form is submitted; both fields are cleared afterwards. |
| `AccountSettings` | `onNotificationsChange` | `(notifications: NotificationPreference[]) => void` | `notificationschange` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called with the FULL updated array whenever any one toggle flips. |
| `AccountSettings` | `onDeleteAccount` | `() => void` | `deleteaccount` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called when the Delete account button in the danger zone is clicked; no confirmation is shown by the component itself. |

### Account Settings · 4. Variants

_This component has no variant prop — it has a single look (restyle it with colors / `classNames`)._

### Account Settings · 5. Colors and Theming

Follows the global theme (§1.4): it recolors with the accent, switches with light / dark mode, and takes the Claymorphism look when `data-design="clay"` is set. It has no color prop of its own.

### Account Settings · 6. Sizes

_No size prop — it sizes to its container / content (see `className` to constrain it)._

### Account Settings · 7. Component States

_No dedicated state props — the visual states are default, hover, focus-visible and active._

Standard visual states: default · hover · focus-visible (ring) · active/pressed.

### Account Settings · 8. Slots / Children / Content

_No children or slots — it is configured entirely through props._

In a Web Component, the element's light-DOM text/children go into the default slot; a prop that accepts a node (e.g. `header`, `footer`) can usually also be filled with a child carrying `slot="<name>"`.

### Account Settings · 9. Framework Interfaces

**`AccountSettings`** → `<l-account-settings>`

| React prop | HTML attribute | Attribute type |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `email` | `email` | string |
| `notifications` | `notifications` | json (JSON string or object property) |
| `transition` | `transition` | string |
| `transitionDuration` | `transition-duration` | number |
| `transitionDelay` | `transition-delay` | number |
| `hoverEffect` | `hover-effect` | string |

Minimal use in each target (from the first docs example, *Basic*):

**React**
```tsx
const [notifications, setNotifications] = useState([
  { key: "product", label: "Product updates", enabled: true },
  { key: "security", label: "Security alerts", enabled: true },
]);

<AccountSettings
  email="jordan@lojee.io"
  notifications={notifications}
  onEmailChange={(email) => saveEmail(email)}
  onPasswordChange={(current, next) => changePassword(current, next)}
  onNotificationsChange={setNotifications}
  onDeleteAccount={() => deleteAccount()}
/>
```
**Plain HTML / JavaScript**
```html
<l-AccountSettings id="account-settings" email="jordan@lojee.io"></l-AccountSettings>

<script type="module">
  import "lojee-ui/elements";

  const el = document.getElementById("account-settings");
  el.notifications = [
    { key: "product", label: "Product updates", enabled: true },
    { key: "security", label: "Security alerts", enabled: true },
  ];
  el.addEventListener("emailChange", (e) => { /* e.detail */ });
  el.addEventListener("passwordChange", (e) => { /* e.detail */ });
  el.addEventListener("notificationsChange", (e) => { el.notifications = e.detail; });
  el.addEventListener("deleteAccount", () => { /* confirm + delete */ });
</script>
```
Vue and Angular use the same `<l-*>` tag and attributes — see the framework templates in §1.3.

### Account Settings · 10. Complete Usage Examples

#### Basic

Each section has its own save action — email, password, and notification toggles are saved independently.

```tsx
const [notifications, setNotifications] = useState([
  { key: "product", label: "Product updates", enabled: true },
  { key: "security", label: "Security alerts", enabled: true },
]);

<AccountSettings
  email="jordan@lojee.io"
  notifications={notifications}
  onEmailChange={(email) => saveEmail(email)}
  onPasswordChange={(current, next) => changePassword(current, next)}
  onNotificationsChange={setNotifications}
  onDeleteAccount={() => deleteAccount()}
/>
```

#### Transitions

Enter transitions via `transition` (with `transitionDuration` / `transitionDelay`) and hover effects via `hoverEffect`. They play on mount — reload the page, or use Replay in the playground.

```tsx
<AccountSettings transition="slide-up" />
<AccountSettings transition="zoom" transitionDelay={100} />
<AccountSettings transition="flip" transitionDuration={700} />
<AccountSettings hoverEffect="lift" />
<AccountSettings hoverEffect="glow" />
```

- Standalone: drop it into any layout (Card, Section, Grid, Container, App shell) or inside forms and overlays.

- Keyboard: native keyboard behavior of the underlying element (Tab to focus, Enter / Space to activate).

## Plan & Billing

*Category: User / Account.* Exports: `PlanBilling`.

### Plan & Billing · 1. Component Overview

The plan and billing summary of an account — current plan and price, what it includes, usage against its limits, the next charge and the card on file.

- React: `import { PlanBilling } from "lojee-ui"`
- Web Component: `<l-plan-billing>` (after `import "lojee-ui/elements"`)

### Plan & Billing · 2. Props and Interfaces

#### `PlanBilling`

| Prop | Type | Default | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `planName` **required** | `string` | — | Name of the current plan, e.g. "Pro". |
| `price` **required** | `number \| string` | — | Price per period — a number or a ready-made string such as "Free". |
| `currency` | `string` | `"$"` | Currency of a numeric price: a symbol shown before it ("$", "€", "₱" …) or a 3-letter code such as "EUR" or "PHP", which is formatted for you (default: "$"). |
| `interval` | `"month" \| "year"` | `"month"` | Billing period: "month" or "year" (default: "month"). |
| `description` | `string` | — | One line under the plan name. |
| `status` | `PlanStatus` | `"active"` | State of the subscription: "active", "trial", "past-due" or "canceled" (default: "active"). |
| `features` | `string[]` | — | What the plan includes, shown as a checked list. |
| `usage` | `PlanUsage[]` | — | Usage meters against the plan's limits — drawn in `color`, so they follow the theme accent. |
| `nextBillingDate` | `string` | — | Date of the next charge, e.g. "Nov 3, 2026". |
| `paymentMethod` | `PlanPaymentMethod` | — | The card on file. |
| `color` | `ColorName \| (string & {})` | `"accent"` | Accent of the meters and the action button: a built-in ColorName or any CSS color such as "#8b5cf6" (default: "accent" — follows the theme accent). |
| `actionLabel` | `string` | `"Select Plan"` | Label of the action button (default: "Select Plan"). |
| `onAction` | `() => void` | — | Called when the action button is pressed; the button is hidden when omitted. |
| `onCancel` | `() => void` | — | Called when the cancel icon in the top-right corner is pressed; the icon is hidden when omitted (cancelling stays optional). |
| `cancelLabel` | `string` | `"Cancel plan"` | Tooltip and accessible label of the cancel icon (default: "Cancel plan"). |
| `cancelIcon` | `string` | `"circle-x"` | Icon of the cancel button, e.g. "circle-x" or "trash-2" — see src/core/icons.ts for the available set (default: "circle-x"). |
| `className` | `string` | — | Extra class name(s) applied to the root element. |
| `classNames` | `{ root?: string; header?: string; price?: string; features?: string; usage?: string; details?: string; actions?: string; cancel?: string; }` | — | Per-part class overrides — merged after (and win over) the built-in styling. |

Also accepts the shared motion props `hoverEffect`, `transition`, `transitionDuration`, `transitionDelay` — see §1.7.

### Plan & Billing · 3. Events

| Component | React callback | Signature | Web Component event | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `PlanBilling` | `onAction` | `() => void` | `action` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called when the action button is pressed; the button is hidden when omitted. |
| `PlanBilling` | `onCancel` | `() => void` | `cancel` (CustomEvent, payload in `detail`) | Called when the cancel icon in the top-right corner is pressed; the icon is hidden when omitted (cancelling stays optional). |

# Part 3 — Index and quick lookups

## 3.1 All components

| Page | Category | React exports | Web Component tags |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

## 3.3 Checklist for generating code with this library

1. Import the stylesheet once (`import "lojee-ui/theme.css"`) and, for non-React use, `import "lojee-ui/elements"`.
2. Wrap React apps in `<ThemeProvider>` (or add `<ThemeSwitcher />`); set `data-theme` / `data-accent` / `data-design` for plain HTML.
3. Pick the component from Part 2; use **only** props from its table; icons by name; colors from `ColorName`.
4. Prefer the data-driven props (`items`, `options`, `data`, `columns`) — they work identically in React and as Web Components.
5. For Web Components: kebab-case attributes, explicit `="true"` / `="false"` for booleans, JSON or property assignment for objects/arrays, events via `detail`.
6. Customize with `className` / `classNames` and theme tokens rather than forking the component.
